- [ ] A bare Grep/Glob can hang a turn indefinitely and searches from the wrong root `bug` `#native-runtime` `#harness` (added 2026-08-26, backfilled — spec written 2026-08-17)
  Motivating incident: one `Grep {pattern:…, path:"."}` from a `$HOME` cwd ran 181 s and hung the session. Spec `docs/active/specs/2026-08-17-search-scope-and-timeout-design.md` splits the work: **Chunk A** (per-tool timeout + git-toplevel default root) has a reviewed plan at `docs/active/plans/2026-08-17-search-scope-timeout-chunk-a.md` that already absorbed a review pass (defects D1–D5, one of which would have killed the whole turn instead of the search); **Chunk B** (gitignore/hidden semantics + Glob rewrite + opt-in) is specified in the same spec and has **no plan**. **Status 2026-08-26: nothing built, 0/29 Chunk A steps, no branch.** Just needs scheduling — no human gate, no dependency.
  **Second incident, 2026-08-26 (Destin's plan 1c hands-on check 1) — same shape, far worse:** a background Explorer hired in a conversation whose folder was `/home/destin` ran `Grep` for `(?i)(^|\b)(purpose|description|about|…)` in `*` files; `tools/grep.ts` passes `--hidden --glob !.git --glob *` with no deadline (`bash.ts` has `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS` 120s / max 600s; Grep/Glob have nothing), ripgrep descended into `~/GoogleDrive/…` — an rclone FUSE mount where every read is a network fetch — and after **4h 02m** had 4 min of CPU and 13 threads parked in `__fuse_simple_request`. The specialist card correctly said "no activity for a while — may be stuck"; only Stop (→ SIGKILL) could end it. Adds to the fix list: (3) skip `fuse.*` mounts from `/proc/mounts` unless the path is explicitly inside one; (4) the hire consent card should warn when the work dir is `$HOME` or `/`.
  **Destin's directive (2026-08-26): before building, a session must check how Pi, OpenCode, Claude Code and Codex bound their own Grep/Glob** — timeout value, what happens on timeout (partial results + "narrow your search" vs. error), whether hidden folders are searched by default, and whether network/FUSE mounts are avoided — and fold the answers into the spec. Starting points: `docs/active/investigations/2026-08-26-native-tools-vs-other-harnesses.md` §6 (its table records Gemini at **30 s** but shows `—` for Claude Code, OpenCode, Pi and Hermes, which means *not measured*, not *none* — verify against source, don't copy the dash) and the 2026-08-10 prior-art round in `docs/archive/investigations/2026-08-10-harness-search-tools-prior-art.md`. Codex is in neither doc yet.

- [ ] Window-resize black bars + content lag — fix committed, never eyeballed, never pushed `bug` `#renderer` `#linux` (added 2026-08-26, work done 2026-08-12)
  `html { background-color: var(--canvas) }` so a resize-exposed region shows the theme canvas instead of the unpainted (black) buffer, plus rAF-coalescing the two always-mounted unthrottled `window.resize` listeners that were forcing a layout sweep + React render in the frame the compositor was waiting on. 1 commit `a1f82f8b`, 3 files +56/-4, worktree `worktrees/resize-paint` (clean), **no PR and never pushed** — `git ls-remote --heads origin` has no `fix/resize-paint-race`. `merge-tree` exits 0 against today's master and both TS files are byte-identical to the branch base, so **zero drift — this is the most merge-ready work in the workspace.** Remaining, all Destin's: (1) drag-resize a dev instance and confirm the symptom is gone — the only thing that verifies the fix; (2) compare 100% vs 200% scale, which says whether Ozone rounding is also involved; (3) an `assembleDebug` eyeball, since the opaque `html` now covers Compose's hardcoded `Color(0xFF111111)` behind the transparent WebView — this is the fix the Android `#theme-bg` item is waiting on. Push the branch regardless of the decision.

- [ ] The installed app holds ~247k inotify watches; two instances exhaust the default limit `bug` `#tooling` (added 2026-08-26)
  Found while running workbench design rounds: Vite died repeatedly with `ENOSPC` on `watch` — not disk, inotify watch exhaustion. Measured 2026-08-26: the installed app alone held ~247k watches, a second dev Electron ~272k, against the 524,288 default. Worked around by raising the limit on Destin's machine, which fixes his box and no user's. Likely an unbounded recursive watch over `node_modules` or `~/.claude/`. User-visible symptom for anyone else: the app or a second instance fails to start file watching with a disk-space error that has nothing to do with disk space.

- [ ] Chat file chips refuse extensions the artifact pane can display — a 28-entry allowlist the viewer stopped using `bug` `#renderer` `#artifacts` `#ui` (added 2026-08-25, found by Destin: three of eight test files rendered as plain text while the other five became chips)
  Two lists decide "can YouCoded show this file" and they disagree. The **pane** stopped using one: `getViewer` (`artifact-views/RendererRegistry.ts:110-125`) routes an unknown extension to `CodeEditorView` whenever the `artifacts:get` response sniffed the bytes as text (`textHint`), so `.log`, `.sh`, `.sql`, `.toml`, `.go`, `.rs` and extensionless files open and edit fine from the Files tab — that was the D4 change, and its own comment says "an unknown extension is no longer an automatic BinaryFallback." The **chat chip** still gates on a hardcoded `WHITELIST` (`hooks/useInlineFilepathDetector.ts:1-13`) of exactly **28** extensions (`avif bmp css csv docx gif htm html ico jpeg jpg js json jsx markdown md pdf png py svg ts tsv tsx txt webp xlsx yaml yml`), so a path the pane would happily open renders as dead grey text in both assistant messages (`MarkdownContent.tsx:66-100`) and user messages (`UserMessage.tsx:53`). Repro: paste `/tmp/x.log` and `/tmp/x.txt` into chat — only the second becomes a chip. **The current behaviour is pinned, not accidental:** `tests/artifacts/inline-filepath-detector.test.ts:25-28` asserts `.log` produces no match, so any fix updates that test too. **Why this is not a one-line list edit:** the pane decides AFTER reading the file; the chip must decide from a path inside a sentence, before anything is read, and the allowlist is also what stops `w3.org/intro.html` and every dotted word from becoming a dead chip (the `DOMAIN_FIRST_SEGMENT_RE` guard only covers hostnames). Fix shapes worth weighing: (a) widen the list with the common agent-output extensions and accept it stays a list, (b) invert to a deny-list plus a stricter path grammar, (c) let the chip render optimistically and resolve on click, falling back to a plain-text render when the path can't be opened. Blast radius is user-visible either way — a too-loose rule turns ordinary prose into dead chips, which is worse than the current miss. Affects anything an agent commonly writes: `.log`, `.sh`, `.env`, `.sql`, `.toml`, `.go`, `.rs`, `Dockerfile`, `Makefile`.
- [ ] Phone-originated set-tag/set-note never notifies the DESKTOP renderer — remote-server has no path to `sendForSession` `bug` `#sync` `#remote` (added 2026-08-22, found while fixing the remote-broadcast half of the 2026-07-23 SESSION_META_CHANGED entry)
  The `fix/roadmap-easy-knockouts` batch gave the remote WS `session:set-tag`/`set-note` handlers the missing `broadcast({type:'session:meta-changed'})`, so remote↔remote clients now stay fresh — but the ipcMain twins ALSO call `sendForSession(...)` to refresh the desktop window, and remote-server has no equivalent wired (`setSessionMetaWiring` injects only `resolve`/`canWrite`). A tag applied from a phone still doesn't refresh an open desktop window until an unrelated meta event or refetch. Fix shape: extend `setSessionMetaWiring` with a notify callback that ipc-handlers backs with `sendForSession`, called from both WS handlers next to the new broadcast.
- [ ] Kotlin `removeProject` is now test-only — prune or re-justify `chore` `#android` `#artifacts` (added 2026-08-22, expected fallout of pruning `rebuildIndex`, its last production caller)
  `rg -n "removeProject" app/src/main` → only the `CentralIndex.kt` declaration remains; remaining references are test-only (`ArtifactStoreTest.kt`). Same shape as the detectOrphan/rebuildIndex entry this fell out of; desktop's central-index has no exported twin to keep parity with. Small standalone prune.
- [x] Native turns go silent for ~3min after a tool result, then end with zero visible output — model's final step returns no content, turn completes "normally" `bug` `#native-runtime` `#harness` (added 2026-08-21, observed three times live in one session; **SHIPPED 2026-08-22 — youcoded PR #324, merge `748480d2`**; spec — `docs/archive/specs/2026-08-21-empty-final-step-turn-recovery-design.md`)
  Transcript `dd55e932`: all three stalls share one fingerprint — final `tool-result` → 180–190s event void (no thinking/text) → a VALID `turn-complete` (`stopReason: "end_turn"`, full usage) with **zero assistant content** after the last tool card. The turn state machine actually completed 25–130s before Destin nudged it; the user experience is an endless-looking spinner followed by… nothing. **Correction 2026-08-21: an earlier version of this entry claimed the turn-complete payloads were empty — that was an extraction-script bug (payloads live under `data:`); the payloads are valid, which makes this a degenerate-model-response problem, not a bookkeeping one.** Two stacked defects: (1) upstream — provider returns an empty `end_turn` final step after a long silence (can't fix the provider); (2) ours — the harness accepts "zero content blocks after a tool result" as a legitimate turn ending with no bounded retry and no honest user-facing explanation. Related: the 2026-08-16 park fix (`28d3f82e`) kept the turn alive through the void (neither old 75s death nor a hang occurred), but parking only helps if the model eventually says something — it never handles "it spoke, and the something was empty." **MERGED 2026-08-22 — PR #324, merge `748480d2`; worktree and branch cleaned up, docs archived.** Spec reviewed+ratified and implementation plan independently reviewed (both in `docs/archive/`): bounded silent retry (finishReason-gated, consecutive-counter), honest `empty_response` stop + footer copy, fully-silent-turn rendering (spec decision 4). **2026-08-21 third session: a deep PR review found the rendered footer was DEAD CODE (ChatView/BubbleFeed drop segment-less turns upstream) plus 9 more defects; all fixed in 5 follow-up commits on the branch, each round independently re-reviewed — see spec §8b for the correction list and the two flagged visible side effects (Interrupted/pause_turn footers on previously-invisible turns).**
- [ ] Live-vs-rebuilt history divergence for whitespace-only empty steps (cosmetic residual of the empty-step-recovery fix) `bug` `#native-runtime` `#harness` (added 2026-08-21)
  A whitespace-only step's deltas still stream and persist as `assistant-text` events (can't know it ends whitespace-only until finish), but live history now skips the message (PR #324's shared emptiness predicate). On resume, `rebuildHistory` coalesces the persisted whitespace into the retry step's text — resumed history says `'\n  \nrecovered'` where live said `'recovered'`. Smaller divergence than pre-fix (two messages vs one), invisible to the user, but the history-rebuild deep-equal arbiter has no whitespace case pinning either behavior. Fix shape: teach `rebuildHistory` the same trim rule + add the arbiter case in the same commit. Same family: whitespace-only INTERRUPTED partials are now skipped live but their persisted deltas still rebuild.
- [ ] Orphaned "Preparing…" card on a NON-empty step spins until turn end `bug` `#native-runtime` `#harness` (added 2026-08-21, pre-existing — surfaced by PR #324's second review)
  A step containing both a completed tool call and an announced-then-dropped one (malformed second call) gets no preparing-card withdrawal — PR #324's withdrawal runs only on the empty-step retry path, and `endTurn`'s reaping only fires at turn end, so the orphan spins beside real tool execution for the rest of the turn. Low severity. Fix shape: `StepResult.pendingPreparing` already carries the orphan ids out of the stream (PR #324); emit the same `toolPreparing {cleared: true}` withdrawal for them on the tool-execution path too.
- [x] `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` is unset inside Bash tool calls, so chatsearch's documented invocation fails on first use `bug` `#skills` `#tooling` (added 2026-08-21, **SHIPPED 2026-08-27 — youcoded#345 + #346, wecoded-marketplace#69 + #70.** Both halves of the fix landed: YouCoded's native harness now substitutes the placeholder with the real plugin root when it renders a plugin skill (`skill-catalog.ts`), and chatsearch's SKILL.md documents the concrete fallback path for harnesses that leave it empty.)
  The chatsearch skill says to run `node "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/skills/chatsearch/scripts/chatsearch.js"` via Bash, but the var isn't set in the Bash tool's shell — first call died with MODULE_NOT_FOUND (`/skills/chatsearch/...`). Workaround: resolve the real path under `~/.claude/plugins/cache/<marketplace>/<plugin>/<version>/skills/...` and call it directly. Fix options: substitute a concrete path when the skill loads, or document the cache-path fallback in the skill body.
- [ ] Cache efficiency — cloud + local sessions are leaving cache hits on the table `bug` `#native-runtime` `#cost` (added 2026-08-17, investigation — `docs/active/investigations/2026-08-17-cache-efficiency.md`)
  **The ~50% "Reuse" reading on a DeepSeek-via-OpenRouter session is NOT a bug** — it's a measurement artifact: the Reuse chip is `cached_tokens / prompt_tokens`, structurally low-early for an agentic loop (every step appends uncacheable new tokens), and DeepSeek caching is automatic server-side. `selectCacheReuse` (`StatusBar.tsx:256-297`). **Documented, not implemented** (Destin chose doc-only this session). But the investigation flagged **genuine cache-defeating defects** to track:
  1. **OpenRouter sends no `session_id`** — `provider-registry.ts:249-255` adds only identity headers, so a turn can drift to a different provider endpoint and lose the server-side cache. Fix: pass a stable per-session id.
  2. **Local: `<specialists-status>` spliced out of mid-history every turn** — `harness-session.ts:1573-1597` removes/re-adds the status block, forcing a full local KV re-prefill every specialist turn (comment at :1564-1565 admits it). Genuine bug.
  3. **Local: `fitToContext` front-trim + aggressive idle teardown** — drops oldest messages once past budget (`harness-session.ts:989-1035`), plus 5-min model sleep / 10-min engine stop (`engine-supervisor.ts:303,472-485`) and no exposed `n_cache`/keep-alive; all silently throw away local KV reuse on long sessions.
  4. **Anthropic `cache_control` absent** (native path only) — harness sends no breakpoints (`harness-session.ts:1903-1931`); only relevant if native Claude becomes a priority, no effect on DeepSeek.
  Full cache rules (Anthropic / DeepSeek / OpenRouter) + stable-prefix playbook in the investigation doc.
- [ ] Desktop CI is red on master's Windows runner — two pre-existing, unrelated failures found while landing specialists plan 1c `bug` `#ci` `#tests` (added 2026-08-16, found verifying plan 1c's Task 0 PR)
  Neither is caused by plan 1c or its Task 0 branch — both were already failing on plain master before that work started. (1) `task-tool.test.ts:211,222` assert a POSIX-absolute path literal (`'read-only:/proj'`) but Windows resolves the same fixture to `'read-only:D:/proj'` — the test hardcodes a POSIX root instead of normalizing per-platform. (2) A case-sensitivity failure — `expected 'roadmap.md' to be 'ROADMAP.md'` — somewhere the harness or a test reads this very file's name off a case-insensitive Windows filesystem and compares it against a hardcoded-case string. Both need a fix that's platform-aware rather than assuming Linux/macOS path/filesystem semantics; see the 2026-07-19 "Scoped Bash cwd persistence was broken on Windows" Shipped entry below for the established pattern (`fs.realpathSync.native`) if either turns out to be the same root cause.
- [x] `native-session-host.test.ts` — two `expect(rec.delivered).toBe(true)` assertions raced under load and flaked on CI (ubuntu/macOS/Windows), though they passed locally every time `bug` `#ci` `#tests` `#native-runtime` (added 2026-08-16, root-caused while landing specialists plan 1c's Task 0 PR; **FIXED 2026-08-16 — youcoded PR #322, merge `bf55513e`**)
  `beginTurn()` (`harness-session.ts`) emits its transcript event SYNCHRONOUSLY, with an explicit "not gated on the disk write" comment; `rec.delivered` only flips later, after a real mkdir-lock write (`confirmDelivered` → `mutateJson`, 10ms poll / 3s ceiling). Six assertions `vi.waitFor`'d the event and then read `rec.delivered` one-shot, leaving ~29ms of unloaded headroom that CI's slower runners could burn through. Reproduced twice on ordinary Linux hardware under artificial CPU+I/O load with the harness unmodified — not a flake, a real race, confirmed NOT caused by the PR's own diff (zero overlap with the delivery path; the same two assertions were already red on master's own Windows run). Fix is test-only (commit `514fcef2` on the PR #322 branch): each `delivered` read now gets its own `vi.waitFor`, still asserting `=== true` — not a loosened assertion. 4 more latent sites of the same shape were found and fixed the same way. Full writeup: `docs/archive/plans/2026-08-16-native-specialists-plan-1c-implementation.md`'s SDD trail (`ci-failure-investigation.md` in the task's scratchpad).
- [ ] `native-session-host.test.ts` "steerSpecialist appends the note…" is load-sensitive — a fixed 20ms delay stands in for a real completion signal `bug` `#tests` `#specialists` (added 2026-08-16, found alongside the `rec.delivered` race above while landing plan 1c)
- [ ] `mcp-startup-wiring.test.ts` "resolves totalSlots: null for a non-local-engine binding…" times out at the default 5s under load — the test `await import()`s all of `ipc-handlers.ts` INSIDE the test body, so a cold or contended import graph eats the whole budget `bug` `#tests` `#ci` (added 2026-08-26, found in plan 1c's pre-merge full run; **pre-existing on master** — 5 runs on plain `master` 73e2defe: 1 timeout at 5008ms, the rest 0.7–1.5s; 5 runs on the 1c branch: 0 timeouts. Same fixed-budget family as the entries above; fix is a per-file `testTimeout` or hoisting the import out of the `it()`, not a code change)
  Same shape as the fixed race directly above (a test racing an async write instead of waiting for a real signal) but NOT yet root-caused or fixed — flagged during plan 1c's Task 9 review and deliberately left for a dedicated pass rather than patched inline. Look for a `setTimeout`/sleep of ~20ms standing in for the ledger's actual write-completion signal, and replace it with a `vi.waitFor` on that signal the way the `rec.delivered` fix above did.
- [ ] A stale `SPECIALIST_RUN_CHANGED` resend can flip a completed specialist card back to "running" — the run record has no ordering or version guard `bug` `#specialists` (added 2026-08-16, found in plan 1c Task 11's review)
  Every push overwrites the WHOLE `specialistRun` on the card; only `notes` merges safely (by index-id — see the ROADMAP note-ordering item below). If a stale `specialists:event` lands on the renderer AFTER a newer one for the same run (a plausible replay-then-live race, or a slow IPC hop), the reducer has no way to tell old from new and simply applies whichever arrived last — so a card that already reads "completed" can revert to "running" with no further update to correct it. Needs a monotonic field (sequence number or timestamp) on `SpecialistRunView` that the reducer can compare before applying an update; out of scope for 1c because it touches the run-view type every consumer reads.
  **Verified 2026-08-22 (easy-knockout triage): NOT actionable until 1c merges** (1c merged 2026-08-26 as `62c1f182` — actionable now) — `SPECIALIST_RUN_CHANGED`/`SpecialistRunView` have ZERO occurrences on master (`grep -rn "SPECIALIST_RUN_CHANGED" . | wc -l` → 0); the code lives only on `feat/specialists-1c-ui`. Fix notes for whoever picks it up post-merge: reducer case is `chat-reducer.ts:1880` (whole-overwrite, only a stringify-equality dedupe); stamp the seq in `toRunView` (`delegation-ledger.ts:554`, the single projection); use a per-record main-process counter, not `Date.now()` (coarse clock ties) and make the field optional (replayed pre-1c records); the remote replay buffer (`remote-server.ts:831`) is a second stale-source the guard must cover; Android carries no specialist events at all (0 hits in `app/`).
- [ ] A background specialist's Activity note rows are appended at the end of the list rather than interleaved with tool/text segments by timestamp `ux` `#specialists` (added 2026-08-16, plan 1c Task 11 review — a deliberate trade-off, not an oversight)
  The plan asked for notes to render in their real chronological position among the card's tool calls and thinking text; the branch instead appends each new note segment to the tail of `subagentSegments`, because the merge is by index-id (see the note-record item in `.claude/rules/native-specialists.md` → "Specialists (plan 1c)") and `SubagentTimeline` never re-sorts. A note sent mid-run can visually land after tool calls that actually happened later. Low-severity (Notes are rare and self-explanatory once read) but worth fixing if the Activity trail is ever used as a real audit log.
- [ ] `missedSteers` (the ledger's record of a steer that arrived too late to deliver) stores the steer's full, UNCLAMPED text in the same read-modify-write ledger file the 2,000-char note cap was built to protect `bug` `#specialists` (added 2026-08-16, plan 1c Task 5 review — deliberate scope cut, not a miss)
  The note cap (`.claude/rules/native-specialists.md` → notes on `run.notes`) only clamps the RECORDED copy of a delivered note; the fix brief explicitly said leave `missedSteers` alone, so a model that repeatedly steers a child that isn't live reopens the same unbounded-ledger-growth concern the cap was built to close, just through a different field. Worth closing once the note cap's real-world usage is understood — a model steering a dead child in a loop is the failure mode to design against.
  **Verified 2026-08-22 (easy-knockout triage): NOT actionable until 1c merges** (1c merged 2026-08-26 as `62c1f182` — actionable now) — the 2,000-char cap (`SPECIALIST_NOTE_MAX_CHARS`, `specialists/limits.ts:53`) exists only on `feat/specialists-1c-ui`; master's only ledger clamp is `RAW_REPORT_CAP_CHARS` on reports. Fix notes post-merge: clamp inside `appendMissedSteers` (`delegation-ledger.ts:256`) AND the `appendSteers` params of `update`/`updateIfRunning` (`:168`/`:223`) — two independent append paths; nothing bounds array LENGTH anywhere, so add a tail-keeping cap (`.slice(-N)`) too; clamp on write only (no `FILE_VERSION` bump needed — oversized on-disk records stay readable) and do it inside the existing `mutate` callbacks to preserve the commutative-append guarantee (`:238-253`).

- [ ] **Loading a second large local model can take the whole machine down — the residency cap counts models, not bytes, and the memory guard is blind to the unified-memory ceiling** `bug` `#local-models` `#engine` `#memory` (added 2026-08-16, hit live by Destin — lost the desktop shell, YouCoded, Chrome, and Steam)
  **Full evidence: `docs/active/investigations/2026-08-16-dual-model-oom-desktop-crash.md`.** Read it first; everything below assumes it.
  **What happened:** Qwen3.5-122B (73.2 GB on disk, 3-part split) and Qwen3.6-35B (30.4 GB) resident together on a 121.5 GB machine whose GPU pool is capped at 80 GiB (`amdgpu.gttsize=81920`). GPU/GTT memory on an APU **is** system RAM and is unswappable while a model holds it — the kernel's `gpu_reclaim` fell to literally 0 while `gpu_active` sat at 78–83 GB. Everything else got crushed into zram (8.5 → 37 GB in three minutes), then the OOM killer fired and picked four processes with near-zero RSS (Chromium stamps `oom_score_adj:300` on helpers), freeing nothing while killing their parent apps. `llama-server` reported `anon-rss:0kB` — **GPU-pool memory is not charged to the process, so the kernel cannot see the actual holder.** `plasmashell` was not killed; it exited on a failed graphics allocation once the GTT pool hit its ceiling.
  **Four defects, all verified against the tree, none inferred from the symptom:**
  1. **`MODELS_MAX = 2` is a count, not a byte budget** (`engine-supervisor.ts:43`). Its own comment reasons in 8 GB terms ("two 8GB models already hurt"). Two 70 GB models are inside the limit, so LRU eviction never fired. Note this constant is **not** app-side eviction — its only use is `'--models-max', String(MODELS_MAX)` at `engine-supervisor.ts:299`, i.e. it configures the router's own LRU. Whatever replaces it must decide whether the budget is enforced app-side before `loadModel`, router-side, or both.
  2. **The guard does not know the GPU pool has its own ceiling.** Nothing in the repo reads `mem_info_gtt_total` (`rg 'gtt' src/` returns nothing). On unified-memory machines the cap for offloaded weights is `amdgpu.gttsize` (80 GiB here), and that memory is unreclaimable once held — a fundamentally different constraint from "we have 121 GB of RAM".
  3. **`capacity` is TOTAL memory, not available memory** (`fit-estimator.ts:66`). It ignores everything already resident.
  4. **`gpu-detector` misreads this APU as a dedicated 4 GB GPU.** `readAmdSysfsVram` (`gpu-detector.ts:140`) reads `mem_info_vram_total` = 4 GiB — the BIOS-carved UMA window — which clears the 2 GB `MIN_DEDICATED_VRAM_BYTES` floor, so `detectLinux()` returns it as dedicated VRAM. The file's stated safety bias ("only when a real DEDICATED GPU's VRAM was confidently probed", `gpu-detector.ts:4-8`) does not hold on Linux AMD APUs.
  **The guard was reached and stayed silent — replicating `checkMemoryForLoad` (`fit-estimator.ts:56`) with this machine's real numbers and `gpu-detector`'s real output:** capacity 125.5 GB (121.5 + the bogus 4.0), combined need 105.6 GB, warn threshold 106.7 GB → **`ok`, by 1.1 GB. No warning of any kind was shown.** Had defect 4 not inflated capacity, the verdict would have been `tight` — still only a dismissible warning ("You can still continue.", `fit-estimator.ts:92`), so fixing defect 4 alone does not fix the bug. `estimateFit` also rates the 122B alone as "Runs well — uses your GPU plus memory", so nothing blocked at any point.
  **Fix shape (proposed, not settled — the first job is to agree it):** make the resident-weights budget a **byte** budget derived from the real ceiling; on Linux AMD read `/sys/class/drm/card*/device/mem_info_gtt_total` (81920 MiB here) and treat `min(gttTotal, availableSystemMemory)` as the cap for GPU-offloaded models; base the guard on available rather than total memory; and when the combined load would exceed that cap, **auto-unload the resident model rather than warning** — "the second big model just works, slowly" is a better outcome than either a scary dialog or a dead desktop. Loading a model that fits alone should never be able to kill the machine.
  **Decisions Destin has to make (do not guess):** (a) auto-unload silently, auto-unload with a toast, or hard-block? He previously chose "BLOCK only when clearly too large, otherwise WARN" (`fit-estimator.ts:46-49`) — this incident is the counter-example to that call and it should be re-litigated, not quietly overridden. (b) Does the byte budget also cap a *single* model, i.e. should the 122B alone be blocked on an 80 GiB pool once KV cache is counted?
  **Cross-platform:** the same shape exists on Apple Silicon, where `gpu-detector.ts:131` already returns `os.totalmem() * 0.7` as "VRAM" — a unified-memory machine reported as having both full RAM *and* 70% of it again as VRAM, i.e. `capacity` is inflated ~1.7x there. Check whether that double-count is real before shipping; if it is, it is the same bug with a worse multiplier. Android does not run the local engine, so it is out of scope.
  **Do not** solve this by lowering `amdgpu.gttsize` or otherwise shrinking what the machine can run — the 80 GiB pool is what makes big models usable on this hardware, and the fix must not cost model capacity. Also do not treat the OOM-killer victim selection as the bug to fix; that is a system-level concern tracked separately (earlyoom / `OOMScoreAdjust`).
  **Guards to add:** `fit-estimator.test.ts` cases pinning (i) a two-large-model load on a unified-memory machine is refused or evicts, (ii) an APU reporting a small `mem_info_vram_total` does not inflate capacity; a `gpu-detector` test pinning that a 4 GiB AMD sysfs reading on a machine with a large GTT is NOT reported as dedicated VRAM. Worktree per the workspace rule; `bash scripts/verify.sh` before claiming done. **No live-app testing** — `bash scripts/run-dev.sh` only.

- [x] A freshly-downloaded local model is selectable but 400s on first send — the router only scans `--models-dir` at boot `bug` `#local-models` `#engine` (added 2026-08-16, hit live by Destin; **SHIPPED 2026-08-23 — merged to youcoded master as `df96b4a5` after diff review + full-suite verify; branch and worktree cleaned up**)
  **BUILT 2026-08-16 on youcoded branch `fix/engine-model-reload` (commit `97dbf0d0`).** This resolves the open question left by Amendment K2 (see ## Shipped, 2026-07-15, and `youcoded/docs/engine-dependencies.md`): the running llama.cpp router does NOT hot-load a post-boot file. It 400s until it re-scans — **a rescan, NOT a restart**; `GET /models?reload=1` is enough and costs 0.8–1.6 ms. Measured 2026-08-16 on Destin's machine: engine spawned 14:41 with `--models-dir ~/.cache/llama.cpp`; `Qwen3.8-27B-UD-Q8_K_XL.gguf` finished downloading 17:49 (31,457,991,680 bytes, byte-exact against HF's `lfs.oid`-verified blob — the download is sound); `GET /v1/models` at 18:2x still lists only the six boot-time models. First send produced `model 'Qwen3.8-27B-UD-Q8_K_XL' not found (provider error 400)` from `describeProviderError` (`harness-session.ts:381`), rendered in `AttentionBanner`.
  **Why it reaches the user as a hard failure rather than a nudge:** K2's union in `EngineSupervisor.listModels()` (`engine-supervisor.ts:577-580`) merges a fresh disk scan into the router's rows, so the model is a fully selectable picker row (`installed: true`) that the server has never heard of — visibility without serveability. `loadModel()`'s warm-up completion (`engine-supervisor.ts:683-693`) would catch this at select time but its failure is swallowed (`.catch()` at :689), as is the eager-load path (`ipc-handlers.ts:769`). Nothing anywhere detects the shape or suggests the fix; the only recovery is Settings → Providers → "Restart engine", or the 10-minute idle stop. Download completion re-reads disk only (`LocalModelsSection.tsx:66-69` → `scanGgufCache`) and never tells the server anything.
  **Fix shape: `GET /models?reload=1`.** Verified 2026-08-16 twice over — disassembly of the shipped `libllama-server-impl.so` (b9992-6eddde06a) and upstream `tools/server/server-models.cpp` at the b9992 tag. The router's `GET /models` handler re-runs `load_models()` (a fresh `--models-dir` scan, plus a re-read of `--models-preset`) when the `reload` query param is non-empty; documented at `tools/server/README.md:1765`. **No engine restart is needed.** Rescan is otherwise dirty-flag-gated (`need_reload` at `this+0xe9`) and set ONLY when a download the ROUTER itself started finishes — our downloads are app-side, so it is never set for us. There is no timer, no inotify, no SIGHUP, no 404-miss rescan; a plain `GET /models` does not rescan. Treat `?reload=1` as a WRITE, not a poll: `load_models()` unloads a running model whose source changed or vanished (log strings `(reload) unloading model … (source updated or removed)` / `(reload) removing model …`), so it must never be wired into `startModelPoll`.
  Ruled out: `POST /models` is HF-download-only — its handler carries exactly one JSON key (`model`), assigns it to `p.model.hf_repo` unconditionally, and never inspects it as a path (hence `invalid HF repo format` for both a bare name and an absolute path, from `common/download.cpp:113`). There is no register-an-existing-local-file endpoint anywhere on the router; the full route surface was enumerated from the binary to establish that.
  Note `tools/server/README.md:1613` still says "The server must be restarted after adding a new model" — **stale upstream**, contradicted by its own `?reload=1` note at :1765 and by the code. That stale line is the likely source of the 2026-07-15 "restart required" belief; don't re-derive it from the README.
  **What landed in `97dbf0d0`:** `EngineSupervisor.refreshModels()` / `ensureServable()` (rescan-once single-flight, **failing OPEN** — a probe that can't answer must never block a send); the serveability check at the local-send chokepoint in `provider-registry.ts`, which is the ONE path every local send crosses (create, mid-session swap, remote, retry) and now throws a message naming the model, cause and remedy instead of passing the router's 400 through; `refreshModels()` after every download completion (`ipc-handlers.ts`, the `download-progress` fan-out) and after `deleteModel()`; the K2 comment at `engine-supervisor.ts` rewritten to say LISTED IS NOT SERVABLE. 9 new tests incl. "the background model poll NEVER sends reload=1". `verify.sh` green; `ipc-channels.test.ts` 202/202 (no new IPC surface — nothing to mirror to preload/remote/Android).
  **Live-measured against a real b9992 router on an isolated port (not the app's):** post-boot file absent from `GET /models` after 8s → completion returns `400 model 'X' not found` → `?reload=1` → the same completion returns 200 and generates. A model already `loaded` SURVIVES a rescan, and so does an in-flight stream (599 SSE chunks, clean exit); a model whose file was deleted is dropped. **Deliberately NOT built:** the progress indicator from the design conversation — at 0.8–1.6 ms it would flash for under one frame. Destin's call if he wants one anyway.

- [ ] Main-chat thinking bubble flickers (disappears/reappears with no visible tokens) during a very slow native stream `bug` `#native-runtime` `#chat-ui` `needs-repro` (added 2026-08-16, Destin during the Specialists 1b hands-on — NOT specialist-related: the parent's own reducer state was probed live and was steady)
  Seen on OpenRouter `qwen/qwen3.8-27b` in a turn that streamed at **6 tokens/s** (444 output tokens over ~75 s, per the persisted `turn-complete` usage) — a reasoning model whose thinking trickles in ~one token every 150 ms. Destin's own read: an artifact of the slow stream. Candidates to check with a live probe next time it happens: the reasoning bubble's collapse/merge on each `partId` delta, and the ThinkingIndicator's `lastOutputAt`-based state changes at sub-second token gaps. Repro needs a slow provider; not reproducible on demand yet.

- [x] **A native permission card vanishes if the renderer reloads (window re-dock / session move / dev HMR) while the ask is still open — the turn stays stuck on an ask with no buttons** `bug` `#native-runtime` `#permissions` (added 2026-08-16, found while tracing the Specialists 1b Test 1 hang; **FIXED 2026-08-16 — youcoded merge `3a06dde0`**)
  Transcript replay (`TRANSCRIPT_REPLAY` → `nativeHost.getHistory`) rebuilds tool cards from the JSONL, but pending asks live only in `PermissionBroker.pending` (memory) — nothing re-sends the open `PermissionRequest` after a replay, so the card comes back as `running` with no `requestId`. Observed twice live: after a Vite HMR reload the hire card sat spinning while main was still waiting; answering the ask through the API (`respondToPermission`) unstuck it. Distinct from the preparing-card overwrite bug fixed in plan 1b (`cd6fb766`, on youcoded master via merge `e5ec5b3c`), which hit with NO reload.
  **Fixed more generally than the "re-emit on replay" shape proposed here.** Replay is only one of at least three ways the card can go missing, and the other two were found the same day tracing Destin's hung local Qwen session (`5442ba1b`): the preparing-card overwrite above, and `sendForSession` silently dropping a hook event when every target webContents is destroyed. The common cause is that a ROOT ask has no timeout at all — `native-session-host.ts` wires `askUser` straight to `broker.ask` with no `opts.timeoutMs`, so `harness-session.ts` awaits it forever and ANY delivery failure is a permanently hung turn that looks exactly like a slow model. So rather than patch the replay path, `PermissionBroker` now heartbeats every open ask every 3 s (`ASK_REANNOUNCE_MS`) until it is answered, and `PERMISSION_REQUEST` was made idempotent-on-repeat: a repeat for an intact ask returns `state` by reference, a stale `requestId` on a non-awaiting card is cleared before the match loop (otherwise tier-2's name-only match hands one ask to a second card — that hazard was real and its test was red), and a lost card is re-created AND re-added to `activeTurnToolIds`. Whole class downgraded from "hangs forever" to "≤3 s flicker". Guards: `native-permission-broker.test.ts` → "pending-ask heartbeat"; `chat-reducer.test.ts` → "re-delivered by the heartbeat" (includes a replay-loss case).
  **Correction (2026-08-16, specialists plan 1c investigation): "rather than patch the replay path" is now stale.** The replay path landed too — `youcoded` merge `bf55513e` (PR #322, plan 1c's Task 0) added `PermissionBroker.pendingEventsFor(sessionId)`, re-sent by the `TRANSCRIPT_REPLAY` IPC handler — and it is NOT redundant with the heartbeat above: `reannounce()` deliberately stops heartbeating an ask once it has timed out (`entry.timedOut`), so a HELD ask gets no further heartbeat, and only a replay re-emits it. Plan 1c's `PermissionHeld` hook event (Task 6) is built on top of that same replay path.

- [x] **YouCoded dies 16–21 s after opening one long conversation — the artifact tracker replays the whole transcript into ~1,000 concurrent rewrites of a 4.4 MB `artifacts.json` until the main process runs out of memory** `bug` `#artifacts` `#conversations` (added 2026-08-15 from Destin's live app — 6 crashes in one day, all after opening the "subagents parity planning" session in `~/youcoded-dev`; **FIXED 2026-08-15 — youcoded PR #318, merge `ebf00c81`**)
  Diagnosed with a heap profile, not guessed: `readSidecar`/`writeSidecar` were the top allocators when the process died ("JavaScript heap out of memory", ~2.9 GB). Opening a conversation replays its transcript through the renderer artifact tracker (App.tsx) TWICE over — `TRANSCRIPT_REPLAY` streams `getHistory()` on the live channel, and `TranscriptWatcher.startWatching` reads the JSONL from `offset: 0` and emits it as live — and the tracker cannot tell that history from live events. That session carries ~1,000 Write/Edit/doc-Read tool calls; each fired `artifacts:append-version` (full parse + stringify of the sidecar, held while polling the mkdir lock) plus `artifacts:list-session` (another full parse), and `ensureProject` queued ~850 lock waits on the central index (`could not acquire lock` in the journal). Fix, three layers, all guarded: (1) `appendVersion` is a per-project **queue** — concurrent callers are applied in one read/apply-all/CAS-write cycle, so memory is one parsed sidecar per project no matter the burst (`appendVersionsDirect` is the unqueued primitive); (2) `ensureProjectCoalesced`/`applyGitTreatmentCoalesced` answer a burst with one index write and one `.gitignore` read; (3) replays are **idempotent by `toolUseId`** — `VersionEvent.toolUseId` (optional, additive; mirrored in Kotlin `SidecarSchema.kt` and Android's `appendVersion`), deduped on `(sessionId, toolUseId)` with no write and no `changed` broadcast for a duplicate. Layer 3 closes the sidecar's unbounded growth **for native sessions and same-session duplicates** — every re-open used to append the same edits again (14,385 versions / 2,996 files by 2026-08-15). **Corrected in the 2026-08-15 review: NOT for Claude Code resumes** — see the follow-up entry directly below. The renderer tracker was extracted to `state/artifact-tool-use-tracker.ts` (pure, pinned) and refreshes the drawer once per session per burst instead of once per event. Guards: `artifact-store.test.ts` "burst coalescing" + "replay dedupe", `project-manager.test.ts` "…Coalesced", `artifact-tool-use-tracker.test.ts` (a 1,000-event burst refreshes once), `ArtifactStoreTest.kt`. Depth: `youcoded/docs/artifacts.md` → Tracking pipeline.
  **Review follow-ups (youcoded PR #320):** `invalidateSidecarIdCache` moved after the queued write; unused import dropped; docs/type comment corrected to the real dedupe scope. Dates: the work landed 2026-08-15 local (CI timestamps read 08-16 UTC).
  **Deliberately NOT done: trimming the existing 4.4 MB `~/youcoded-dev/.youcoded/artifacts.json`.** The fix makes it survivable (a few parses per open, not a thousand) and it stops growing on re-opens; a trim is a data-touching action on Destin's own project record (version history would lose entries) and should stay a deliberate call, not a side effect of a bugfix. Records written before the field existed get re-recorded ONCE (now with the id) on their next replay, then never again. Also NOT done: Android's append path is still one full read/write per event (no queue) — the same React tracker runs there, so a huge session opened on the phone would be slow, not OOM-crashy in the same way (no 4.4 MB sidecar exists there); left as-is.

- [x] **The app still OOM-crashes on a long session — PR #318 queued the sidecar WRITER and left every READER unguarded; ~477 concurrent parses of the now-6.4 MB `artifacts.json` = ~3.0 GB** `bug` `#artifacts` `#memory` `#conversations` (added 2026-08-27 from Destin's live app — crashed after 12h32m; same ceiling hit 2026-08-23 after 60h)
  **Full evidence: `docs/active/investigations/2026-08-27-artifacts-sidecar-oom-crash.md`.** Diagnosed from the core dump, not guessed: `coredumpctl dump 185608 | strings` yields 9,903,385 `sessionId` fragments across only 20,742 distinct `ver_` ids — **477 copies of one index** — and those ids are `~/youcoded-dev/.youcoded/artifacts.json` (6.4 MB, 3,917 artifacts, 21,311 versions), not `~/.youcoded/artifacts.json`. 477 x 6.4 MB = ~3.0 GB against a 2,825 MB ceiling; two last-resort Mark-Compacts freed **0 bytes**, so the live set was genuinely 2.8 GB. **What #318 missed:** only `appendVersion` is queued (`artifact-store.ts:152`) and only `ensureProject`/`applyGitTreatment` are coalesced (`project-manager.ts:98,113`); all **20** `readSidecar` call sites are unguarded (`rg -n "readSidecar\\(" src/main | wc -l` -> 20). `LIST_SESSION` (`ipc-handlers.ts:3573`) — which `artifact-store.ts:333` says "fires after every tracked write" — does a full 6.4 MB read + parse plus a `.filter` over all 21,311 versions, per call, with nothing capping concurrency. `listProjectsIndex` (`projects-index.ts:144`) is the same shape via `Promise.all` across every project. The sizing comment at `artifact-store.ts:333` still assumes a "2,800-record array"; actual is 21,311 (7.6x). **One `Edit` now costs FIVE full passes over 6.4 MB** (~35 MB of transient string, four complete object graphs, all >16 KB so allocated straight into old space): `readSidecar` (`artifact-store.ts:214`), `JSON.stringify(next, null, 2)` (`:85`), the CAS re-read's **full parse to read one ISO timestamp** (`cas-write.ts:180` — `(raw) => JSON.parse(raw).updatedAt`, the most gratuitous and a one-line fix via a bounded regex), `LIST_SESSION` (`ipc-handlers.ts:3573`), and the watcher's `resolveArtifactId` (`project-watcher.ts:126`, whose cache the write just invalidated at `:112`). The code already confesses this failure mode at `ipc-handlers.ts:3470`: appendVersion was queued "instead of a thousand, **each of which used to pin a parsed 4.4 MB sidecar in memory until the app OOM'd**". By event type: `edit` 15,282 (72%) / `read` **3,602 (17%)** / `create` 2,423 — the `read` events exist only to make a tool card clickable and are the cheapest thing to stop recording. **Why the file grew:** nothing prunes it (`rg "versions\\.(splice|slice|shift|length\\s*=)|MAX_VERSIONS|pruneVersions" src/` -> no matches); +2 MB in the 12 days since #318 (4.4 -> 6.4 MB), 2,333 versions on 2026-08-26 alone. Measured composition: **8,416 versions (2.5 MB) belong to 107 worktrees that no longer exist on disk** (44% of all artifacts are under `worktrees/`), and **2,951 (0.9 MB) are the same `toolUseId` re-recorded under a new desktop session id** — the entry directly below, now confirmed on disk (a floor, not a total: only 32% of versions carry a `toolUseId`). **Fix shape (proposed, not agreed):** a read-side coalescer mirroring `appendQueues` so in-flight reads for one project root share a parse — that alone bounds memory to one parsed sidecar per project and stops the crash; trimming the file alone only postpones it. Treat the worktree records and the resume re-records as separate, Destin-decided data questions (#318 deliberately declined to trim and that call should stand). **Guard:** `artifact-store.test.ts` — a burst of N concurrent LIST_SESSION-shaped reads performs ONE parse, mirroring the existing "burst coalescing" write test. **Ruled out:** sync leases (the 2026-08-23 crash hit the same ceiling with leases `ok=true`; `lease-client.ts` audited clean), hub reconnect fan-out (3 events all run), system memory (68 GB free at death). **No live-app testing** — `bash scripts/run-dev.sh` only.
  **FIXED 2026-08-27 — youcoded PR #335, master `9dc0d9c7`:** a shared parsed-sidecar cache in main (one unpacked copy per project, validated by size + mtime, in-flight reads share one parse, seeded after each committed write, idle-evicted) plus a head-only probe for the CAS `updatedAt` check. Desktop only; touches no data. **The rest of the bug class** — the six-lens sweep found it in five places, the biggest being transcript replay (75 MB + 90 MB subagents per session on every reload) and Android's artifact store (never got #318's write queue) — is specced, PAUSED and UNREVIEWED in `docs/archive/specs/2026-08-27-paged-history-and-read-hardening-design.md`; see the two entries directly below.

- [x] **Paged conversation history — the window holds a cursor into the transcript, not the transcript** `feature` `#conversations` `#chat-reducer` `#memory` `#android` (added 2026-08-27; design direction approved by Destin, spec PAUSED and unreviewed)
  Six load paths use three mechanisms today (reload replay, re-dock, native resume, CC resume via the live tailer from byte 0 PLUS a text-only "last 10", phone `chat:hydrate` of the desktop's entire chat state, "See previous messages"). Reload replays every open tab's whole transcript + every subagent file synchronously (`transcript-watcher.ts getHistory`, `subagent-watcher.ts getHistory`) — 75 MB + 90 MB on Destin's largest session — one IPC message per event. Replace all six with one `transcript:page` request (tail-first, 30 turns / 2 MB per page, streamed line by line, one read in flight), auto-fetch on scroll-to-top with scroll anchoring, and evict runs of completed turns out of view >5 min once loaded history exceeds two pages (never the active turn, never below one page). Retires `HISTORY_LOADED`/`hist-` entries/`HistoryExpandButton`/`resumeInfo`; the live tailer starts at file END on resume. Three-surface parity incl. a Kotlin tail reader. Also folds in: `listPastSessions` concurrency cap + memoised old-encoding dir resolution (25 MB re-read per list open today), tail reads for `model:read-last`/`loadHistory`, catalog single-flight, and `artifact-tracker.ts` session cleanup. **Spec (reviewed 2026-08-28, 12 corrections): `docs/archive/specs/2026-08-27-paged-history-and-read-hardening-design.md` §2, §4.** **SHIPPED 2026-08-28 — youcoded PR #349, merge `a09b58c6`** (desktop only; Destin's scope decisions 1a/2a). Open a huge conversation **21.6 s → 0.61 s**, medium 14.0 s → 0.64 s, worst-case session switch 10.1 s → 0.23 s, memory after six sessions 7.0 GB → 1.7 GB, app-freeze during resume 14.5 s → 0 ms; reproduced across three full rig runs. The rig found **four** defects no unit test saw, all the same shape — features depending on whole-file replay as a side effect (history requested from only three call sites; a stale Files drawer; duplicated messages; session totals counting nothing on resume). Lesson written up in `docs/PITFALLS.md` → "Removing a broadcast". Plan + full gate audit: `docs/archive/plans/2026-08-28-paged-history-cycle-2.md`. **Still open, deferred by decision:** eviction of off-screen turns (cycle 3), Android on-device paging + its Kotlin tail reader, and the §4 smaller readers (`listPastSessions` cap, `model:read-last`/`loadHistory` tails, catalog single-flight, `artifact-tracker.ts` session cleanup).

- [ ] **Android artifact store has neither the write queue nor a read guard — PR #318 never reached Kotlin** `bug` `#artifacts` `#memory` `#android` (added 2026-08-27, found in the OOM-class sweep)
  `ArtifactStore.kt appendVersion` is read → parse → mutate → pretty-print → CAS-write per call with no queue (`rg -n "Mutex|Semaphore|withLock|synchronized" app/…/artifacts/` → 0 hits); `SessionService.kt:262` launches every bridge message on `Dispatchers.IO`, so a replay burst runs dozens of full parses in parallel; `artifacts:append-version` does 3 sidecar reads + 1 index read per tool event (`ensureProject` + `applyGitTreatment` + `appendVersion`); `CasWrite.kt:86` re-parses the whole file for one timestamp; `readSidecar` holds three copies (string, `JSONObject`, Kotlin objects). No `largeHeap` in the manifest. A 6.4 MB sidecar × 3 copies × parallel coroutines OOMs a phone long before desktop's 2.8 GB ceiling. Fix = mirror desktop: per-project append queue, shared parsed cache, head-only CAS probe, compact `toString()`. **Spec: `…/2026-08-27-paged-history-and-read-hardening-design.md` §3.**

- [ ] **Six small per-session structures in main are never torn down — four maps that `session-exit` simply missed, plus two with the wrong cleanup scope** `bug` `#memory` `#harness` (added 2026-08-27, found sweeping main during the sidecar-OOM investigation)
  Not the OOM cause (each is a few hundred bytes; together <1% of the sidecar cost) but all genuine misses, and a single small cleanup PR. **Four maps keyed by session id with NO removal anywhere:** `lastModelSeen` (`ipc-handlers.ts:2178`), `lastSessionModelState` (`:2655`), `specialistSpawnCounts` (`native-session-host.ts:329`), `childApprovedAsks` (`:397`, cleared only inside `resumeSpecialist` at `:797`, so a child never resumed keeps its entry). Their neighbours ARE cleaned — `lastAttentionBySession` at `ipc-handlers.ts:2976` and `lastContextByDesktopId`/`lastGitBranchByDesktopId`/`lastSessionStatsByDesktopId` at `:2979-2981`, and `NativeSessionHost.destroy()` (`:3596-3622`) explicitly deletes seven siblings — these four just weren't added to those lists. (Note `specialistSpawnCounts`' "never decremented" comment is about the counter VALUE, not the map entry.) **Two with the wrong scope:** `WindowRegistry.subscriptions` (`window-registry.ts:104`) — `releaseSession()` (`:95`) clears `ownership` only, so a dead session's entry survives until its subscriber window closes; and `attentionReports`' inner map (`main.ts:426`) — per-session entries clear only when the renderer volunteers `{ clear: true }` (`:1839`), with no main-side `session-exit` path. **Also worth capping while in there:** `SubagentIndex.unmatchedParents` (`subagent-index.ts:49`) has no TTL sweep unlike the sibling `pending` map's `pruneExpired` (`:123`), so a `Task` whose subagent JSONL never materialises is retained for the session's life; and `pendingOutput` (`ipc-handlers.ts:1637`) is an uncapped PTY string buffer for any session that never mounts a `TerminalView` (normal path drains in ~1s via `TerminalView.tsx:220`, so low risk). Full list incl. the structures verified CLEAN: `docs/active/investigations/2026-08-27-artifacts-sidecar-oom-crash.md` -> Secondary findings.

- [ ] **A resumed Claude Code conversation still re-records its files once per resume — the artifact dedupe is keyed by DESKTOP session id, and a CC `--resume` mints a new one** `bug` `#artifacts` `#conversations` (added 2026-08-15, found in the adversarial review of PR #318)
  `session-manager.ts` gives every CC session `randomUUID()` even on resume (native sessions reuse `resumeSessionId`), and the transcript watcher replays the JSONL from offset 0 tagged with that NEW id, so `(sessionId, toolUseId)` never matches what an earlier resume recorded → ~1,000 versions (~300 KB) appended per resume of a long conversation. Bounded and crash-free after #318 (the queue caps memory), but the file keeps growing and each open pays a full parse of a bigger file. **Why it is not a one-line fix:** that same re-recording is currently what populates the resumed session's drawer — `LIST_SESSION` (`ipc-handlers.ts`) and `SessionDrawer.tsx` `versionsInSession`/`lastModifiedInSession` all filter versions by desktop id, so deduping across resumes without a second identity would EMPTY the drawer for resumed CC sessions. Design shape: record a conversation-stable id on the version (the CC session id, which main already has in `sessionIdMap` at APPEND_VERSION time; native = same as sessionId), dedupe on `(conversationId, toolUseId)`, and let LIST_SESSION + the drawer helpers match either id (the renderer does not know the CC id, so LIST_SESSION would have to resolve and pass it, or return an "effective session ids" set). Also decide what `/clear` (rotates the CC id) and resume-from-summary mean for the drawer. Until then, the "records get re-recorded once, then never again" line holds only for native sessions.

- [ ] **Android terminal mode hides the wallpaper to reveal a native terminal that no longer exists** `bug` `#themes` `#android` (added 2026-08-12, found while investigating desktop resize repaint)
  `globals.css`'s `html[data-platform="android"][data-view-mode="terminal"] #theme-bg, … #theme-pattern { display: none }` rule exists, per its own comment, "so the native TerminalView (rendered below the transparent WebView in ChatScreen.kt) shows through". That TerminalView is **gone** — `ChatScreen.kt:12-15` documents removing the TerminalView Compose block, `applyTerminalColors`, and the BaseTerminalViewClient/TerminalSession imports, and `youcoded/CLAUDE.md` states xterm.js in the WebView has been the sole Android terminal renderer since Tier 2.
  So on Android in terminal mode the wallpaper and pattern layers are hidden to reveal… whatever Compose paints behind the transparent WebView, which is a hardcoded `Color(0xFF111111)` (`ChatScreen.kt:30`, with `WebViewHost.kt:44` setting `Color.TRANSPARENT` on the WebView). Under a light theme that reads as a dark rectangle where the wallpaper should be. After `fix/resize-paint-race` gives `html` an opaque `background-color: var(--canvas)`, the exposed area becomes the theme canvas instead of `#111111` — better, but the rule is still hiding the wallpaper for no remaining reason.
  Fix is probably to **split the selector, not delete the rule**: the `body[data-mode="buddy-mascot"] #theme-bg, body[data-mode="buddy-chat"] #theme-bg` half of the same rule is still correct and load-bearing for buddy transparency. Wants an `assembleDebug` eyeball on a light theme in terminal mode to confirm the symptom before changing anything.

- [ ] **Slug-repair follow-ups from the 2026-08-12 final branch review** `bug` `#slugs` `#tech-debt` (added 2026-08-12; deferred by the final whole-branch review of `fix/project-slug-encoding` — all safe-direction, none block the merge)
  Four items the review triaged DEFER, recorded here so they survive the session: (1) ~~**fork snapshots re-accumulate every launch**~~ **SHIPPED on the branch 2026-08-15 (commit `6be7925d`)** — the `surfacedForks` set now exists in `~/.youcoded/slug-repair-state.json`, gates re-snapshot, AND holds surfaced forks out of both mirror directions (the real-data run showed the size-gated grow-only mirror clobbering the smaller fork copy within 2 s). (2) **Stale deferred-key sweep** — `state.deferred` entries are only cleared when the session re-appears in findings; a session whose mis-filed location vanishes without a resolving finding leaves a dead key forever (harmless growth). (3) **Windows lowercase-drive over-cap probe** (spec risk 6) — no real-CC fixture pins hash-of-drive-normalized vs hash-of-raw on a >200-char lowercase-drive path; needs a probe run on a Windows machine. (5) **`originalPath` on cross-device sessions is device-relative** — a session that ran on two devices (e.g. `4a774005`, started on Windows, continued here) gets `originalPath` = the local folder on whichever device repairs it, and that syncs; each device's resume resolves it locally anyway (`resolveLocalProject`), so it is functionally fine, but the field's "path on the device that created it" semantic (`store-core.ts:26`) is not what the record carries after a repair. Worth a per-device path map if the sync design ever wants to lean on it. (6) **A held fork stays cross-device-stale until resolved** — while `26d919ff`-style forks are held, a user's new turns on the resumed copy don't reach the sync space until the local file outgrows the (larger) space copy, so peers see that one conversation stale; and the record note tells the user the fork exists but not HOW to resolve it. A "resolve fork" affordance (pick a copy; the other goes to quarantine; hold released) is the real fix. (4) **Kotlin non-BMP divergence** — Kotlin regex matches per code point, JS per UTF-16 unit, so an emoji in a path would slug differently on Android vs CC; unreachable today (only ASCII `canonicalHome()` is passed), WHY-commented in `CcProjectSlug.kt`; add an emoji pair next probe run.

- [ ] **Settings → Backup & Sync takes the whole workbench down with "YouCoded failed to start"** `bug` `#workbench` `#sync` `#ui` (added 2026-08-12, found while building the permissions screen in the workbench)
  Opening Settings → Backup & Sync in `bash scripts/run-workbench.sh` throws `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'length')` and the RootErrorBoundary replaces the app with the full "YouCoded failed to start" screen. **Reproduced against a clean master workbench during the M5 2a session — pre-existing, NOT caused by that branch.**
  **Root cause, read out of source rather than inferred from the symptom:** `mock-shim.ts` has no `sync` namespace at all (zero hits for `getStatus` or a `sync` impl), so `window.claude.sync.getStatus()` falls through to `withCatchAll`'s `apply` trap, which returns `delay([])` — an empty ARRAY. `[]` is truthy, so `SyncPanel.tsx:1479`'s `status && status.syncedCategories.length` reads `.length` off `undefined`. Note that line is the outlier on its own screen: every neighbouring status read (`:362`, `:384`, `:387`) is defensively `?.`-guarded, so the product-side hardening is a one-character fix and the real work is a `sync` impl in the shim.
  **`workbench-boot-check.mjs` cannot catch this class and says so** — its header documents that it "only exercises MOUNT", and its `ROUTES` list covers frames and scenarios, never a Settings sub-panel. Anything reachable only by clicking into a panel is invisible to it, which is why a green boot check accompanied this crash.

- [x] **`NativeSessionHost.askPermission` has zero callers** `bug` `#native-runtime` `#permissions` (added 2026-08-12, found while wiring M5 2a's revocation path) **SHIPPED 2026-08-22 — merged `17931fc0` (branch fix/roadmap-easy-knockouts; plan: `docs/archive/plans/2026-08-22-roadmap-easy-knockout-batch.md`)**
  `rg "askPermission"` over the whole youcoded repo returns exactly one line — the declaration itself (`native-session-host.ts:243` on `feat/native-permissions-ui`, `:230` on master). Its doc comment says "Task 12's `decide()` will call this"; what actually shipped hands each `HarnessSession` an `askUser: (req) => this.broker.ask(req)` closure (`:415`), which reaches the same broker without ever going through this method. Dead code, and knip will not flag it because it is an exported class method — the same shape as `HookRelay.closeSocket()` above. Either delete it or record why the API surface is kept.

- [ ] **`review-harness.mjs`'s API-key scrub does not actually stop the model reading the key** `bug` `#harness-eval` `#security` (added 2026-08-12)
  `test-engine/review-harness.mjs:113` reads `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` into a local and then `delete process.env.OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, with a comment stating this stops the key reaching a saved transcript. It does not. `delete` compiles to `unsetenv`, which edits the **in-heap** environ array; it never rewrites the `mm->env_start..env_end` region the kernel exposes at `/proc/<pid>/environ`. Every descendant of the runner can still read the parent's original environment — and the Bash tool the model drives is exactly such a descendant.
  **Measured 2026-08-12**, reproducing the runner's exact shape (key arrives in env at exec, captured, deleted, then a child spawned with `env: process.env`): the child reports `inherited-env: 0` — the delete does work for the child's own copy — but `parent-environ: 1` from `/proc/$PPID/environ`. `ps eww -p $PPID` leaks it too. The commonly-checked channels (`env`, `printenv`, `ps -eo args`, `/proc/self/cmdline`) all read clean, which is why this survived: the obvious probes all pass.
  **Why it is reachable, not theoretical:** `BATTERY_PROMPT` area 5 instructs the model to "test env var persistence across calls", so it is already poking at the environment; anything it reads lands in `run.events`, which the CLI writes verbatim to `docs/active/investigations/harness-review-runs/<date>/<model>.json`. Those transcripts are gitignored, so the realistic blast radius is local disk plus whatever the model chose to quote back in its review — not a repo leak.
  **Severity is moderate, not urgent:** a dev-only script, an OpenRouter-scoped key, run by hand against models that are semi-trusted by construction. But the key has been rotated for unrelated reasons before, and the rule file repeated the same false assurance ("the CLI deletes it from `process.env` before any run"), so the claim needed correcting wherever it appears. **Corrected 2026-08-13** in `.claude/rules/harness-evaluator.md` (renamed from `harness-review-runner.md`).
  **Fix, implemented on `feat/harness-evaluator`:** the new CLI passes the credential over the child's **stdin** rather than argv or environment — nothing in `/proc` for a descendant to read — reads it from `--key-file`, and **refuses to start** if `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` is in its own environment at all. It also allowlists the child environment, so `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`GITHUB_TOKEN` and anything else in the operator's shell stop riding along into every tool subprocess. Guard: `tests/harness-eval-key-leak.test.ts`, with a negative control that must report LEAKED.
  **Still open:** `review-harness.mjs` itself is untouched and still leaks. It is now otherwise superseded — it imports its runner logic from `dist/main/harness/eval/`, and the evaluator's `harness-battery` case runs the same battery. **Decide: retire it, or port the three fixes.** Retiring also drops its one unique behaviour, appending each model's free-form review into `docs/active/investigations/`.

- [ ] **A trimmed-for-the-wire image is still claimed as "already visible" by the dedupe cache** `bug` `#native-runtime` (added 2026-08-11)
  `fitToContext` drops oldest messages from the outgoing request only — `this.history` is untouched — so an image can scroll out of what the model actually sees while `shownImages` still vouches for it. The model then gets `[image not re-attached: <path> is unchanged and already visible earlier in this conversation]` about a picture it can no longer see, and no image, until that file's mtime changes. Same misleading-text-plus-silent-non-delivery class the rest of youcoded#293 closed; this is the one path left open, and it is documented at the `shownImages` field rather than papered over.
  **Bounded:** needs the prune-protected window to exceed the fit budget (context under ~8,500 tokens) AND `supportsVision: true`, which for a local engine requires an explicit registry entry — i.e. a registry-declared small local vision model. Everything larger is covered because compaction re-estimates un-trimmed history at each turn start and fires first.
  **Do not "fix" it by clearing the cache at `fitToContext`** — it runs on every request, so an unconditional clear defeats dedupe entirely and a count-diff latches permanently true once history outgrows the window. Two reviewers rejected both. Intended fix: re-key `shownImages` to `Map<path, {mtime, toolCallId}>` and reconcile against the fitted window after trimming — exact, no over-clearing, ~8 lines.

- [x] OpenRouter session start reads the model catalog twice `bug` `#native-runtime` `#perf` (added 2026-08-11) **SHIPPED 2026-08-22 — merged `17931fc0` (branch fix/roadmap-easy-knockouts; plan: `docs/archive/plans/2026-08-22-roadmap-easy-knockout-batch.md`)**
  `contextLengthFor` and `visionSupportFor` each independently call `ModelCatalog.get()`, and `get()`/`ensureFresh()` re-read the on-disk cache file with no in-memory memoization. On a stale cache that is a duplicated 15s-bounded fetch pair rather than a shared one; on the happy path it is a second `readFileSync` + full `JSON.parse` + re-parse of the whole OpenRouter payload, synchronously on the main process, per create/resume/swap. Non-OpenRouter bindings short-circuit and are unaffected. Disclosed honestly in both closures' comments. Fix is memoization inside `ModelCatalog`, not another short-circuit.

- [x] Chat/terminal toggle's sliding indicator disappears after a narrow→wide round trip `bug` `#renderer` `#ui` (added 2026-08-11; FIXED same day — youcoded `a39e287d`. Investigation: `docs/archive/investigations/2026-08-11-chat-terminal-toggle-indicator-disappears.md`)
  Root cause was ownership, not math. Endpoint geometry lived in four `--pill-*` custom properties written imperatively onto the container node, while the `measured` readiness flag was `HeaderBar` state that was only ever set to `true`. Crossing 640px swaps `<NarrowViewToggle/>` for a different element type, so React unmounted the node holding the properties — and the new one rendered with `measured` still `true`, so `left`/`width` fell back to `auto` and an empty absolutely-positioned div collapsed to zero size. Intermittent only because any of the four re-measure triggers silently repaired it.
  The same ownership gap explained the separate theme-switch jank: the trigger list (mount, `showToggleLabels`, `document.fonts.ready`, `window.resize`) could not cover theme swaps, since `fonts.ready` describes *current* pending font work rather than subscribing to later stylesheets, and a theme swap is not specified to emit `resize`. Fix replaces the enumerated triggers with observed geometry: `WideViewToggle` owns endpoints in state tied to its own mount and measures an inert sizing layer holding two non-animating endpoint copies, so any event that actually changes layout changes the observed boxes. Live-following the animated buttons was tried three times before (`ae5776ee`, `68462e9b`, `a0103014`) and is why the sizing layer exists — the WHY comment at the observer carries those SHAs. Environmental corrections snap via `data-geometry-syncing`; only `viewMode` changes animate. Guards: `WideViewToggle.test.tsx` (19 tests incl. the narrow→wide remount regression), `tests/toggle-motion-policy.test.ts`.

- [ ] **Presence can wedge OFF permanently — `suspended` is clearable only by a `powerMonitor` event** `bug` `#social` `#desktop` (added 2026-08-11; **spec: `docs/active/specs/2026-08-11-presence-self-healing-design.md`**)
  Found from a friend row reading `Last seen 7/26/2026` on 2026-08-11 while the person was still using the app, signed in, not incognito, on a MacBook. **No data or server defect** — `users.last_seen_at` is accurate; the client simply never opened a socket again. Ruled out by inspection: client caching (`GameLobby.tsx:238` refetches on mount, no cache), duplicate accounts (`identities` is keyed `(provider, provider_user_id)`), server version-gating of old clients (none exists in `worker/src/social/routes.ts`), session expiry (`SESSION_MAX_IDLE_SEC` is 90 days), and column-type drift (`last_seen_at` is INTEGER with a `MAX()` no-go-backwards guard).
  **The defect:** `presence-socket.ts:114` composes `rendererDesired && !suspended && !idle`, and `social-handlers.ts:126-127` is the *entire* lifecycle of `suspended` — set on `powerMonitor` `suspend`, cleared only on `resume`. If that clearing edge is ever missed, presence is off for the rest of the process lifetime: user input can't clear it (the 15s poller only writes the `idle` axis), sign-out/sign-in can't (that writes `rendererDesired`), and the reconnect engine can't (it isn't retrying). Only a full quit-and-relaunch does. macOS is the worst case *by construction* — the comment at `social-handlers.ts:120` deliberately relies on dark wakes not firing `resume`, but nothing compensates for a missed **real** wake, and a MacBook runs for weeks of lid-close cycles without being quit. The suspend handler's last act before wedging is a clean close, which stamps `last_seen_at` — so the frozen date is the last lid-close with a working socket.
  **Diagnostic property worth keeping:** every socket teardown also stamps the timestamp (`presence-room.ts` lines 202/237/243), so any fault that connects-then-drops yields a *moving* date. A frozen date means nothing has connected since. That is what rules out the idle gate, OS suspend, Wi-Fi drops, Android Doze, and the old-Android missing-ping flapping as candidates — all of them would keep the date advancing.
  Second wedge, same machinery: presence uses `noToken: 'wait'`, and `reconnecting-ws.ts:81-86` schedules **no retry** when the token is missing at connect time (only `'poll'` does); `usePresence` re-invokes only on `signedIn`/`incognito`/`isLeader` changes.
  Trap for whoever implements the obvious fix: a timer that re-asserts desire does NOT work — `setDesired(true)` on a desired-but-disconnected engine cancels the pending backoff timer and reconnects immediately (`reconnecting-ws.ts:124-139`, deliberate), so it would defeat capped backoff during an outage and spam `onReplay` on healthy sockets. The spec keys the repair on an explicit stall predicate instead. Safety argument for the whole fix: `idle` is the correctness gate and `suspended` is only a fast-offline optimization, so over-clearing `suspended` is bounded by `idle` and cannot resurrect the 2026-07-22 ghost-socket bug.
  **Not verifiable here** — the workspace's only machine is Linux, so the macOS `resume` behavior can't be reproduced; the fix is designed not to depend on knowing it. Field test on the affected machine: a full quit-and-relaunch restoring Online is positive evidence, since a relaunch is the only thing that clears the latch today.

- [ ] **Any 401 silently destroys the local account session** — no notice, no log `bug` `#social` `#desktop` (added 2026-08-11)
  `handler-utils.ts` wraps every social and marketplace handler with `if (!result.ok && result.status === 401) store.signOut()`. One 401 from any of them unhooks the user's account with zero feedback; `usePresence` then drops presence, so the symptom is indistinguishable from the wedge above (frozen `last_seen_at`, friends see them offline forever) and the user only finds out by opening the friends panel. Deleting the sign-out would be worse — the UI would claim signed-in while every call failed — so the fix is to make it *announced*, not to remove it. Scoped separately from the presence repair (auth surface, own blast radius); see Part 4 of `docs/active/specs/2026-08-11-presence-self-healing-design.md`.

- [x] **`youcoded-dev`'s OWN CI is red, and has been silently** — seven rule files bust the 600-word budget, plus PITFALLS, plus a NEW broken anchor `bug` `#docs` `#ci` (added 2026-08-11, **FIXED 2026-08-12 — `node scripts/audit-anchors.mjs` exits 0: anchors 224/224, MAP 142/142, zero budget violations.** The anchor: parked (test exists only on `feat/project-description`; restore comment in the rule frontmatter). The budgets: overflow migrated VERBATIM into the lazy docs (youcoded PR #302, merge `2026495c`) per the README prescription; `native-runtime.md` (3300 words) split into `native-runtime` + `harness-tools` with cleanly partitioned `paths:` globs — reviewer verified ZERO coverage holes across all 41 harness files and 10/12 sampled invariants surviving verbatim, with the 2 losses restored before merge. PITFALLS 2694→1987 by replacing its single-repo "Native harness" section with a pointer. Residue, deliberate: PITFALLS' Buddy Floater section is still single-repo content with no other home — future migration when a buddy rule/doc exists.)
  **Re-measured 2026-08-12 (still exit 1):** seven rule files now, not eight — `harness-review-runner.md` passes the counter today. That is exactly the cross-repo anchor drift the cron was built to catch, live and unheard.
  `node scripts/audit-anchors.mjs` exits 1 on `FAIL budget violations`, so `.github/workflows/workspace-ci.yml` fails on every push AND on the daily cron. Verified 2026-08-11: the last five runs (4 push + 1 schedule) are all `failure`. Offenders and word counts: `native-runtime.md` 3181/600, `react-renderer.md` 967, `artifacts.md` 897, `conversations.md` 848, `chat-reducer.md` 789, `registries.md` 605. **Correction (2026-08-11, measured while landing the sync-warning fix): that list was short by three.** `sync-spaces.md` (1143 at the time this entry was written, ~2× budget — the second-worst offender after `native-runtime.md`) and `harness-review-runner.md` (617) were both already failing and both omitted; `docs/PITFALLS.md` also fails its own separate 2500-word budget at 2694. So the real count is **eight rule files plus PITFALLS**, and the two biggest are `native-runtime.md` and `sync-spaces.md` — which is where the migration work actually is. Re-measure before starting rather than trusting either list; an entry about an unwatched check having a miscounted offender list is the same failure one layer up.
  **Why this matters more than the numbers:** this is the *self-verifying documentation* system — the thing meant to catch doc/code drift before it misleads a session — and its alarm has been ringing unheard. It is the same failure the `web-fetch.ts` ast-grep entry and the Windows/macOS master red both describe: a check that is always red stops being a check. The cron exists precisely because anchors point into the sub-repos and break when `youcoded` moves without a push here, so a permanently-red cron means anchor drift is currently undetectable.
  The `.claude/rules/README.md` prescription is already written: *"Overflow migrates to the lazy doc the rule points to, or becomes a pinning test."* `native-runtime.md` at 5× budget is the real work — it has absorbed M1/M2/M3/MCP/tools/web-tools/Plan-C in one file and wants splitting per subsystem, with depth pushed into `youcoded/docs/native-runtime.md`. Note the path-vocabulary fix (2026-08-11) knowingly added ~220 words to that file rather than lose the invariant; it did not create the violation.

- [x] **master is RED on Windows — 1 test remaining (was 7), from work merged 2026-08-11** `bug` `#native-runtime` `#ci` `#harness` (added 2026-08-11; **6 of 7 CLOSED 2026-08-12 by youcoded `16a9adb5`; ALL CLOSED 2026-08-15 — youcoded PR #317, merge `c4fdc19c`: Desktop CI green on Windows + macOS + Ubuntu for the first time since 2026-08-10**)
  **2026-08-15 close-out.** The `persistent_env` Windows failure was a REAL product bug, not a test defect: Git Bash's `mktemp` prints an MSYS path (`/tmp/tmp.XXXX`), Node on Windows resolves it as `C:\tmp\...`, the env dump was never read and the best-effort catch hid it — the probe now announces the path via `cygpath -w` (bash.ts). Also fixed in the same PR, none of them in this entry before: the "Windows without Git Bash" test really spawns `powershell.exe` and needed a 30 s timeout; `harness-eval-orchestrator.test.ts` had 78 Windows failures from `import('D:\...')` (`ERR_UNSUPPORTED_ESM_URL_SCHEME` — every computed-absolute-path `import()` in `test-engine/*.mjs` and the test's probes now goes through `pathToFileURL`), and once THAT was fixed vite's test runner mangled the `file:///D:/` form in-process, so `vitest.config.ts` now externalizes `test-engine/*.mjs` (native Node load — measured to also make `require()`/`import()` module identity agree, which the test's own comment had recorded as diverging); plus one test asserting a POSIX-shaped absolute path. Lesson stands: `verify.sh` is Linux-only, and it took three Windows CI round-trips to land — each one found the next layer.
  **Not the path-vocabulary bug** (that one is fixed, entry below). Two PRs merged onto an already-red master on 2026-08-11:
  - ~~`43a9c43a` → six `harness-review-runner.test.ts > wrap-up turn` failures~~ **CLOSED by `16a9adb5` ("fix(test): the red wrap-up tests are timing out, not failing")** — these were vitest 5,000ms-default timeouts on tests whose work doubled when `BATTERY_STEP_BUDGET` moved to 100; every failure sat at exactly 5,000ms. Not a native-runtime defect and not a mid-review-merge logic bug; the fix gives the nine tests explicit 30s timeouts, no production code touched.
  - `a2b0e35f` ("warn when shell state won't carry…") → `harness-tools-core.test.ts > Bash > env-state warning + opt-in persistence (persistent_env) > a persistent_env call carries an exported var to the NEXT call`, Windows-only. **Still open as of 2026-08-12** — no Windows guard, no skip, nothing since `a2b0e35f` touches it (`922fcd32` fixed a different pair of round-8 tests).
  Measured, not inferred: master at `a2b0e35f` had **11** Windows failures and **4** macOS; PR #291 cut that to **7** Windows and **0** macOS by fixing the four originals plus `workspace-root miss hint` (a NEW `a2b0e35f` test that depends on the cwd behavior #291 corrects). Run ids: master `31521789535`, PR `31522517435`.
  **Same lesson as the entry below, one day later:** `scripts/verify.sh` is Linux-only, so a green local run says nothing about the two platforms these broke on. Both PRs were merged over a red matrix.

- [x] **master was RED on Windows and macOS CI** — four Bash-cwd / Grep-path tests `bug` `#native-runtime` `#ci` `#harness` (added 2026-08-11; **FIXED 2026-08-11 — youcoded PR #291, merge `71c4014a`**. Spec + plan archived: `docs/archive/specs/2026-08-11-harness-cross-platform-path-vocabulary.md`)
  Broke at `eba51705` ("Merge harness tool honesty + review runner", 2026-08-10) and still failing at master tip. Ubuntu passes; the `build` aggregate passes. Failing tests, verified identical on master and on every branch cut from it — **four distinct cases across six platform/case combinations**, not six tests:
  - **Windows:** `Bash reports the tracked cwd after a cd, so the model never has to guess` · `ANSI stripping does not disturb the cwd sentinel` · `Grep returns workspace-relative paths for targets inside the workspace`
  - **macOS:** `Bash always states the cwd and exit code` · `Bash reports the tracked cwd after a cd…` · `ANSI stripping does not disturb the cwd sentinel`
  The first three are in `tests/harness-tools-core.test.ts` (`:468`, `:491`, `:521`); the Grep case is in `tests/harness-tool-bounds.test.ts:53`.
