  **CORRECTED 2026-08-11 — the original `__YC_CWD__` sentinel hypothesis below was WRONG.** The sentinel works correctly on all three platforms. These are **two independent defects**, both real product bugs, neither a test defect: (1) `bash.ts:499` compares the shell's reported cwd to `startCwd` with `path.resolve`, which neither follows symlinks nor expands Windows 8.3 short names — so two spellings of ONE directory read as a change and the canonical form lands in `setShellCwd` and the `[cwd: …]` metadata line (macOS `/var`→`/private/var`; Windows `RUNNER~1`→`runneradmin`). Reproduced on **Linux** with a symlinked root, so this is not a CI-only artifact — it hits any symlinked workspace root on any platform. (2) `glob.ts:232` normalizes separators to `/`; `grep.ts` prints ripgrep's stdout verbatim and rg uses the platform separator, so Windows gets `src\a.ts` from Grep and `src/a.ts` from Glob — the sibling Glob test passing on Windows is the proof Grep is the outlier. Fix, decisions, rejected alternatives, and the traps (rebasing must not outrun the scope guard; `--path-separator=/` alone silently breaks the currently-agreeing external-directory case; `guards.ts:25 canonicalize()` must NOT be reused for output because it lowercases on Windows) are all in the spec.
  **Why this matters beyond the red X:** the desktop CI is the ONLY thing exercising these two platforms — `scripts/verify.sh` runs on the developer's machine, which is Linux, so a local green says nothing about them. Two PRs (#289, #290) were merged on 2026-08-11 with these failures present, after confirming the same failures on master first; they are inherited, not caused. But the longer master stays red the less any future red means, which is the same "trains sessions to ignore the unignorable check" failure already recorded for the `web-fetch.ts` ast-grep entry.

- [x] **Sync health warnings never clear themselves — "No internet" sat under "All synced · 1m ago"** `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-08-11, reported by Destin from his live app, **SHIPPED 2026-08-11 — youcoded PR #292, merge `883bf3ad`**)
  `SyncService.runHealthCheck()` ran **exactly once per app run**, at the end of `start()`. It owns three codes — `OFFLINE`, `PERSONAL_NOT_CONFIGURED`, `PERSONAL_STALE` — and all three describe conditions that change *while the app is open*, but nothing re-evaluated them. A launch that lost the race with the WiFi coming up (or any resolver hiccup — the probe is a single `dns.lookup('github.com')` with a 5s timeout) wrote `OFFLINE` into `.sync-warnings.json`, and it stayed there for the rest of the session. Destin's screenshot is the two states side by side: a red "No internet — can't reach the network" card directly under a green "All synced · GitHub · itsdestin · instant sync on · 1m ago" header, four devices listed, self "Synced just now".
  This is the residue the **PR #254 entry below predicted in its own last sentence** — *"Landing #248 alone does NOT close this: any transient warning (a real OFFLINE at launch) still pins the row red until restart."* #254 fixed the renderer half (the Settings row read a frozen mount-time snapshot instead of the live file); after it, every surface reads the file faithfully — and the **file** was the stale thing. Worth noting as a pattern: three separate entries here (#248, #254, this one) are the same bogus-warning symptom at three different layers, each fix correct and each insufficient alone.
  Fix: re-run the check every 60s, cleared in `stop()`; the periodic run passes `probeBackends: false` so `autoDetectBackend`'s rclone shell-out (35s, measured on the Z13) stays a launch-only cost; `OFFLINE` now needs two consecutive failed probes so a launch race can't produce it at all; a dismissed code stays dismissed for the app run (else the re-check resurrects it a minute later); the file is rewritten only when the set actually changes, with `createdEpoch` excluded from that comparison — which also upgrades the field's meaning to "when this condition was first seen". Guard: `tests/sync-warning-self-clear.test.ts`, 9 cases, all red on the parent commit; the load-bearing one is "start() keeps re-checking, and stop() ends it", since the sweep logic itself was never the missing piece.

- [ ] **Pasting a bare filepath was eaten as a slash command** `bug` `#native-runtime` `#renderer` (added 2026-08-10, Destin during the M1–M3 dogfood) — **PARTIALLY FIXED, merged 2026-08-10 (youcoded `d8c4d59c` on PR #287). STAYS OPEN: the shipped gate does not close the class.** The review of #287 measured the residue against the real dispatcher — `/My Files/notes.md` → `invoke-skill skill="my" args="Files/notes.md"`, `/Users Shared/x/y.md` → `skill="users"`, `/README.md` → `skill="readme.md"`, and bare `/etc` / `/home` — all still consumed, input still deleted. The gate tests the COMMAND WORD for a second `/`, so any path whose first segment lacks one sails through.
  **DEPRIORITIZED 2026-08-10 — Destin is not concerned about the residual cases.** The common shape (`/home/destin/notes.md`) is fixed and shipped; what remains are root-level files and paths with a space in the first segment. Do not surface this as pending work or spend a session on it unless he raises it. The analysis below is kept because it is measured and would otherwise have to be redone.
  **It is not a discriminator problem, so tightening the string test cannot fix it.** `InputBar.tsx` does `void runNativeSlashAction(...); return true;` — fire-and-forget — and `sendMessage` is synchronous, so the caller clears the draft before the skill result is known. `runNativeSkill` DOES return `false` on not-found and main already knows the skill does not exist; the renderer discards that answer. Three shapes, needing a product decision: (a) restore the draft on failure — smallest, but the box flickers and a bogus "Couldn't run /my" toast still fires for text that was never a command; (b) fall through to sending it as a normal message when the skill is not found — matches what CC sessions already do, best outcome, but a genuine typo like `/compcat` would go to the model instead of toasting; (c) resolve against the skill catalog in the dispatcher — closes the class at the source but reverses the standing decision recorded at `slash-command-dispatcher.ts:76-85`.
  Pasting an absolute path into the input box to ask about a file toasted a "skill not found" warning and DELETED the input. `/home/destin/notes.md` parsed as the command word `/home`, missed every case in the dispatcher's switch, and fell to the `/skill-name` branch (M3 item 1), which returns an `invoke-skill` intent; `routeSlashResult` consults `nativeAction` BEFORE `handled` by design, so the native route claimed the input as consumed and the caller cleared the box. Claude Code sessions never showed it — they reach `passthrough` and send the path as ordinary text, which is what the fix restores for native. The gate tests the command word only, so a path passed as an argument still works. Guard: `tests/slash-command-filepath.test.ts`.
  **Worth noting for whoever touches the dispatcher next:** this is the second bug caused by `/skill-name` riding the `handled:false` branch. That branch means "not a known command", and it now carries an intent, so ANY unrecognized `/`-prefixed text becomes a skill invocation. Two shapes are known to reach it wrongly (a filepath; anything with a second slash). A URL pasted with a leading slash, or a regex, would too. The narrow fix is in; the general question — whether an unrecognized token should be *assumed* to be a skill — is not settled.

- [x] **A permission prompt can describe a DIFFERENT tool than the one it approves** — *raises the priority of M5 item 3 (no way to revoke an "Always allow"); see program §6* `bug` `#native-runtime` `#chat-reducer` `#permissions` (added 2026-08-09, Destin during the M1–M3 dogfood; **merged 2026-08-10, PR #287**)
  Reported sequence, native session: `Bash 1 → "Allow Bash 1?" → Bash 1 active → Read 1 → "Allow Bash 1?" → Read 1 active`. The card names and describes the earlier tool; approving it runs the new one. **This is a consent bug, not a display bug** — the user is shown one action and authorizes another, on the app's highest-stakes control. Treat it as higher severity than its "sometimes" framing suggests.
  **Mechanism, confirmed present on master (not reproduced, but the code path is unambiguous).** `PERMISSION_REQUEST` binds an incoming ask to an existing card through a three-tier match (`chat-reducer.ts:1135-1162`): (1) same name AND identical input, (2) first running tool with the same name, (3) **`anyRunningId` — the first running tool of ANY name.** Tier 3 is the bug: it hands the ask to an unrelated card, and the code comment at `:1177-1179` records that swapping the input in was *deliberately declined* there ("would render tool Y's name over tool X's input, a worse lie"). So tier 3 knowingly leaves the earlier tool's name AND input on a card whose buttons now authorize a different tool. Native asks reach this same reducer path — they ride the CC `hook:event` channel by design (`ipc-handlers.ts:2372`) into `hook-dispatcher.ts:25` — so this is NOT native-specific, though native is where it was seen.
  **Two conditions must coincide, which is why it is intermittent:** a tool of a different name is still `status: 'running'`, AND the new tool's own card does not exist yet, so tiers 1–2 miss. **The first condition is very likely the stuck-"running" bug logged directly below** — an orphaned card poisons this matcher for the rest of the session. Fixing that one may reduce this one's frequency without touching the real hole; do not let that mask it.
  **Answering "did we already fix this?": partly, and not this part.** `a4232a18` (merged 2026-08-09, `fix/askuserquestion-stale-card`) fixed the adjacent tier-2 case — a name-matched card showing an earlier call's input, which is how the second AskUserQuestion of a session re-displayed the first one's options. Tier 3 was consciously excluded from that fix. Destin's installed build predates it regardless.
  **FIXED — merged 2026-08-10 on PR #287 (youcoded `fad49656`).** The gating question is answered: `git show d6c90c1a^` shows tier 3 WAS the original naive implementation (its own comment: "the arbitrary first-running-tool fallback"); tiers 1–2 were added in front of it and it was never removed. Vestigial, not load-bearing — deleted. An unmatched ask now falls through to the pre-existing synthetic card, which describes the ask's own payload. Guard: `chat-reducer.test.ts` → "PERMISSION_REQUEST tool identity".
  **Original fix direction, kept for the record:** an ask should never bind to a card whose `toolName` differs from the ask's. Dropping tier 3 lets it fall through to the synthetic-card path that already exists at `:1203`, which mints a card from the ask's own payload and is reclaimed by `TRANSCRIPT_TOOL_USE`'s merge when the real event lands — honest by construction. **Before removing it, establish why tier 3 was added** (tier 2 already degrades on input mismatch, so tier 3 only earns its place if some producer reports a name the transcript does not — MCP's `mcp__server__tool` namespacing is the obvious suspect). If such a case exists, reconcile the names rather than keeping a blind fallback.

- [ ] **A resumed session shows tool calls still "running" — a tool cannot be live in a session that was closed** `bug` `#native-runtime` `#chat-reducer` (added 2026-08-09, Destin during the M1–M3 dogfood)
  Reported symptom: after resuming a session, some tool cards still render as active/running. As Destin put it, the tool obviously can't be running — the session was closed and reopened, so whatever it was doing died with the process.
  **Hypothesis, NOT yet reproduced or confirmed** — read the code before trusting it. Replay dispatches the same `TRANSCRIPT_*` actions the live path uses: `TRANSCRIPT_TOOL_USE` creates the card at `status: 'running'` (`chat-reducer.ts:243`) and adds it to `activeTurnToolIds`; `TRANSCRIPT_TOOL_RESULT` closes it. The only thing that reaps a tool left running is `endTurn()` (`chat-reducer.ts:171-181`), which flips every still-`running`/`awaiting-approval` id in `activeTurnToolIds` to `'failed'` — and it only fires on a turn-ending action. So a transcript whose last turn was cut off mid-tool-call (app quit, crash, kill) contains a `tool_use` with no matching result AND no turn-complete after it, and replaying it reproduces the orphan faithfully. Nothing appears to run at the *end* of replay to reap what the transcript never closed. `HISTORY_LOADED` is not the culprit — it only rebuilds text segments and never reconstructs tool calls at all.
  **Check both providers before scoping.** The suspected mechanism is reducer-level and provider-agnostic (CC replays via `transcript-watcher.ts:469 getHistory`, native via its own eager transcript load on resume), so this may well affect Claude Code sessions identically — do not assume it is native-only just because it was found in a native session.
  **FIXED FOR NATIVE SESSIONS ONLY — merged 2026-08-10 on PR #287 (`fad49656`, plus `c7cf8692` for the buddy window).** The replay handler now appends a synthesized `replay-complete` marker and the reducer reaps orphans through the existing `endTurn()`.
  **Second surface, found reviewing #287 and fixed in the same PR:** the original fix landed in `App.tsx` only. `BubbleFeed` feeds its OWN `chatReducer` instance — the buddy is a separate `BrowserWindow` and cannot share the main window's `ChatProvider` — and already called `requestTranscriptReplay`, so main was delivering the marker to that window all along; it just had no `case` for it, and the card kept spinning in the buddy feed. Guard: `tests/transcript-event-surface-parity.test.ts`, which measures the App/BubbleFeed divergence rather than asserting a single case. **That test also records three further gaps in its `KNOWN_GAPS` ledger — `user-interrupt`, `skill-invoked`, `context-clear` — none investigated.** `user-interrupt` is the likeliest real bug of this same family: ESC in the main window ends the turn, and the buddy would not see it. **Claude Code sessions still have this bug, deliberately:** the reap is gated on a `sessionIdle` flag because the SAME replay fires when a window re-docks a genuinely mid-turn session, where the running tool is real and must not be failed. Only `NativeSessionHost.isIdle()` (`entry.inFlight`) can affirm idleness; CC has no equivalent signal, so it reports false and keeps today's behaviour rather than risk failing a live tool. **Closing the CC half needs a mid-turn signal for PTY sessions that does not exist today** — that is the remaining work on this entry, and it is why this stays [ ] after the branch merges. Guard: `chat-reducer.test.ts` → "TRANSCRIPT_REPLAY_COMPLETE" (incl. a test that a non-idle replay does NOT reap).
  **Do not render these as "complete."** We do not know whether the tool finished before the process died, and a card claiming success for work that may never have happened is the failure mode `docs/error-message-standards.md` exists to prevent. The honest terminal state is failed/interrupted with a reason that names the real cause — the session was closed while this was running — which is exactly what `endTurn`'s existing `error` field already carries. The likely fix is to reap orphans once replay completes, reusing `endTurn`'s treatment rather than adding a second notion of "tool that never finished."

- [ ] **Sustained UI sluggishness in real use: hiccups, lagging animations, freezes** `bug` `#performance` `#renderer` (added 2026-08-27, reported by Destin during the perf-lab build)
  Destin reports frequent freezes, stutter and sluggishness in daily use. Reported profile, from his own answers: **all four surfaces** (streaming, session switching, scrolling long conversations, opening panels); **noticeable from launch AND worse over hours AND worse with more concurrent sessions**; and **equally present on plain Midnight and on blur/glass themes**.
  Theme-independence is the diagnostic: if it were compositing/`backdrop-filter` it would track theme weight. It does not, so this is **main-thread JavaScript**, not paint.
  Measured corroboration from the perf lab (`perf-reports/`, sha `4256ade`, 6 sessions + streaming, 43s window): **22–36 frame gaps >40ms per window** (max ~128ms), **14–17 long tasks totalling ~1s**, switch p95 **118ms**; PSS **450MB idle → 2.5GB with six sessions**; resuming a 50,000-message conversation freezes the main thread **~124s** while `loadHistory` itself returns in 245ms (so it is entirely render cost).
  **UPDATE 2026-08-27 (supersedes the numbers on the line above — those came from an EMPTY-session fixture; rig defect #4 in the handoff):** trusted baseline `perf-reports/2026-08-27-1141-16ea12e-post-rebase-baseline` on `16ea12e` (rebased onto master 40feb750): open medium (5,000 entries) **14.8 s**, huge (7,000) **22.0 s**, first entry visible 148 ms; switch into huge with six sessions open **11.1 s**; switch into an EMPTY session while two others stream **1.7 s** (three fresh boots); six sessions **7.0 GB** PSS. The freeze is **98–99% renderer** (rendering every entry at once — `ChatView.tsx:764`, no virtualization), 0.1–0.3 s file read. Fixture now carries fenced code, diffs and tool cards (not prose). **Calibrated against Destin's real screen the same day** — he saw first messages immediately, then ~22 s unscrollable/stuttery. **Gate passed 2026-08-27 evening: Destin approved cards 1–3** (per-token streaming costs → paged history → park hidden views, in that order). Cycle-1 plan: `docs/active/handoffs/2026-08-27-perf-cycle-1-handoff.md`. Zero optimizations shipped yet. **Cycle 1 RUN 2026-08-28:** N1/N2/N3 built + pinned (`c5e1e2d3`, `4935e8d0`, `047da493` on `perf/optimization-pass`, unpushed); gate says REJECT — target metric −4.5% inside spread, PRIMARY guards clean, and the rig is blind to the per-token path by construction (it streams whole turns). **Destin's decision 2026-08-28: ship on the pinning tests — SHIPPED, youcoded PR #342, merge `97600ddd`** (the rig's per-token blind spot is the filed rig issue; card 2 done, cards 1 and 3 — paged history, park hidden views — are cycles 2 and 3, next); two rig defects filed (non-deterministic `native-chat` parity screen; flaky artifacts drawer).
  Leading suspect: `ChatView.tsx:695-707` keeps a ChatView **mounted for every open session** (`content-visibility: hidden`, deliberately not `display:none`, for resize perf) — so cost scales with total open sessions, which matches "worse with more sessions" exactly.
  The rig currently **understates** this: fixture transcripts are plain prose, while real conversations are full of code blocks (CodeMirror/lezer highlighting), diffs and tool cards; and no scenario runs longer than ~45s, so accumulation over hours is unmeasured. Closing those two gaps is a prerequisite for trusting any fix here.

- [ ] **Spikes when editing files, copying text, or navigating HTML in the artifact viewer** `bug` `#performance` `#artifacts` `#renderer` (added 2026-08-27, reported by Destin)
  Distinct surface from the transcript-replay freeze above, and **currently unmeasured** — the perf rig exercises no artifact viewer, no editor and no terminal, so these are code-level suspects, not measured findings. Flagged as such deliberately.
  - **Copy button walks the code block's AST whenever its message content changes.** `MarkdownContent.tsx:186-187` calls `hastText(node)` unmemoized inside the `pre` renderer — a full recursive subtree walk per code block, purely to populate `<CopyButton text=…>`. **Corrected 2026-08-27:** an earlier version of this entry claimed it re-walks on *every* re-render. It does not — `MarkdownContent` is `React.memo`'d on a plain `content: string` prop (`MarkdownContent.tsx:265`), so an unrelated re-render skips it entirely. What remains true: on a **resume** every message mounts fresh, so all N walks happen at once and feed the replay freeze; and **while streaming**, a growing bubble's `content` changes per token, so a bubble holding a large code block re-walks repeatedly. Scales with fence count, not with conversation length.
  - **HTML artifacts re-parse the whole document on any change.** `HtmlView.tsx:40` is `<iframe srcDoc={doc}>`; changing `doc` tears down and re-parses the document and re-runs its scripts. `DocxView.tsx:43` uses `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` with the same shape.
  - **Editing re-tokenizes** through CodeMirror/lezer, which is expected in isolation but lands on top of the two costs above.
  Prerequisite before ranking any fix here: extend the rig with an artifact/editor scenario (open an artifact, type into it, copy from a code block, navigate an HTML artifact) instrumented with BOTH the renderer long-task probe and the main-process IPC stall probe (`scripts/perf-lab/probe-ipc.mjs`).

- [ ] **The app's transcript mirror is LARGER than the original and writes back over it** `bug` `#performance` `#conversations` (added 2026-08-27, found by the perf lab)
  While measuring, the rig appended turns to a fixture transcript under `~/.claude/projects/<slug>/<id>.jsonl`, then truncated the file back after destroying the session. Between repeats the file grew back by exactly the streamed amount. The writer is the mirror at `~/YouCoded/Personal/Conversations/claude/transcripts/<project>/<id>.jsonl`, which was bigger than the original it mirrors (small: 7.4 MB mirror vs 4.7 MB original; medium 32.0 MB vs 29.2 MB). Unverified whether this is by-design takeover behaviour or a genuine second writer on Claude Code's own file (`conversations/takeover.ts:73` warns about exactly that shape). Owner of `conversations/` should look; the rig now boots fresh per repeat so it no longer trips on it.

- [ ] **Buddy-window chat (`buddy/BubbleFeed.tsx:347`) still forces a layout reflow per streamed token — the twin of cycle-1 N2, left in place on purpose** `bug` `#performance` `#renderer` `#buddy` (added 2026-08-27, found while fixing N2 in `ChatView.tsx`)
  Same shape as the main ChatView defect: the auto-scroll effect is keyed on `state.lastActivityAt`, which the reducer re-stamps on every delta, and `scrollToBottom` reads `scrollHeight` right after the commit — one forced document layout per token while a buddy window is open. NOT fixed in cycle 1 because (a) the rig measures no buddy window (handoff §7, coverage gap), so a change there would ship without a KEEP, and (b) unlike ChatView, BubbleFeed has NO ResizeObserver on its content wrapper to take over re-pinning, so dropping the dep alone would stop auto-scroll during streaming. Fix shape: port ChatView's `contentRef` ResizeObserver (`ChatView.tsx` "Watch the content wrapper's size" effect), then drop the timestamp dep; guard mirrors `desktop/tests/chatview-scroll-pin-deps.test.tsx`. Measure it once the rig has a buddy-window scenario.
- [ ] **The perf rig cannot see native per-token streaming — it streams whole turns through the CC transcript path** `bug` `#performance` `#tooling` (added 2026-08-27, found while running cycle 1)
  `scripts/perf-lab/scenario-workload.mjs` appends a user line + an assistant line per 150 ms tick, so the renderer sees ~7 renders/s per streamed-into session, every one of which appends timeline entries. The native harness streams hundreds of same-turn deltas per second that append NOTHING — the exact path cycle-1's N2/N3 fixes target — so their real-use effect is under-represented in the gate (now declared in the scenario's `MEASURES.blindTo`). Fix shape: a native-provider streaming variant (fake OpenAI-compatible endpoint or a scripted native model) driving `TRANSCRIPT_ASSISTANT_TEXT` deltas with a `partId`, reporting the same `switchPaintedBySize` and long-task clocks. Pairs with the buddy-window coverage gap above.

- [ ] **Perf rig: the `native-chat` parity screen is non-deterministic — it photographs a real local model's reply** `bug` `#performance` `#tooling` (added 2026-08-28, found by the cycle-1 gate)
  `scripts/perf-lab/run.mjs` captures `native-chat.png` after a real Qwen 0.5B session has replied, and `compare.mjs` fails any screen over 5% differing pixels. The reply text changes every run: two identical-code baselines (`…-2259-16ea12e` vs `…-2330-16ea12e`) differ by 6.88%, another pair by 4.61%, so the screen can REJECT an unchanged build. Fix shape: make the reply deterministic (fake/scripted native provider, or seed + temperature 0 and pin the prompt), or mask the bubble region before diffing. Until then treat a `native-chat` DIFF as noise unless the other four screens also move.
- [ ] **Perf rig: the artifacts phase's session-files drawer is flaky — 1 in ~9 repeats lists nothing for 30 s** `bug` `#performance` `#tooling` `#artifacts` (added 2026-08-28, found running cycle 1)
  Same boot, three repeats: repeat 3 of `…-2259-16ea12e-cycle1-baseline` opened the drawer and saw `[]` for 30 s although `artifacts:list-session` had just returned the registered files, aborting the whole 26-minute run (exit 2); repeat 2 of `…-0001-047da49-cycle1-n1n2n3` returned `undefined` numbers in 3 s WITHOUT aborting and the median silently covered it. Two shapes, one cause unknown — `scenario-artifacts.mjs:1022` documents the `useActiveProject` → `artifacts:list-projects-index` race but a 30 s wait should outlast a race. Candidates: the teardown's blind `clickTitle('Session Files')` toggle leaving the next repeat's drawer state inverted; the sidecar growing across repeats (resumed transcripts register artifacts each time). Fix shape: own boot per repeat like workload, and make an empty repeat FAIL validation rather than fall out of the median.

- [ ] **Community themes can inject arbitrary `@keyframes` — the sanitizer never looks at `animation`** `bug` `#performance` `#themes` `#marketplace` (added 2026-08-07)
  `sanitizeCSS` (`theme-validator.ts:152-174`) strips exactly five constructs — `@import`, external-protocol `url()`, `expression()`, `javascript:`, `-moz-binding` — and never inspects `animation`, `animation-iteration-count`, or `@keyframes`. The two lint passes (`lintCustomCss`, `lintCustomCssSelectors`) are `console.warn` only and check z-index and selector names. Meanwhile the blessed `KNOWN_THEME_HOOKS` targets (`.assistant-bubble`, `.header-bar`, `.input-bar-container`) are permanently-mounted chrome, and because the `[data-reduced-effects]` / `prefers-reduced-motion` rules are selector-specific, **Reduced Effects would not disable a theme's animation either**. So an installed theme can impose an uncapped ~30%-of-a-core perpetual cost on every user, with no switch that turns it off. No shipped theme does this today (`golden-sunbreak`, `meadow-mist`, `halftone-dimension` custom_css contain no `animation`) — this is a ceiling problem, not a live one. Second vector: `sanitize-rig-svg.ts:13` strips SMIL but keeps `class`/`style`, so a theme SVG can attach app-owned `comp-*` keyframes to arbitrarily many elements. Fix is probably a cap (force `steps()`, or reject `infinite`) rather than a ban, since animation is a legitimate theming tool.

- [ ] **The animation frame-budget cost ships to Android and remote browsers too, and is unmeasured there** `bug` `#performance` `#android` `#remote` (added 2026-08-07)
  Verified reach, not assumed: `scripts/build-web-ui.sh:19-26` copies `dist/renderer/*` verbatim into `app/src/main/assets/web`, the `bundleWebUi` Gradle task runs before every APK build, and CI does the same — so the identical `globals.css` (with `buddy.css` + `mascot.css` folded in) ships in the APK. `rg 'setFrameRate|preferredDisplayModeId|refreshRate'` across `app/src/main/kotlin` returns **zero hits**, so nothing caps frame rate; `WebViewHost.kt:61` sets `LAYER_TYPE_HARDWARE` and the WebView presents at the device's active refresh rate. Phones are commonly 120 Hz, always on battery, and Reduced Effects defaults off — with no fan noise to prompt a user to go find the toggle. Remote is the same story via `remote-server.ts:251` (serves the same `dist/renderer`), except the **client's** display governs, so a remote user on a 240 Hz monitor pays 240 fps while the host desktop is still presenting simultaneously. Mitigating: mascot rigs and companions are excluded from both surfaces (`Icons.tsx:281`), and Android's `PARTIAL_WAKE_LOCK` means a slept screen stops presenting. **Unmeasured:** the per-frame cost coefficient on mobile Chromium/ARM — the 29-33% figure is one Linux/amdgpu/180Hz machine. Measure before scoping any fix.

- [ ] **Unverified claim: "the marketplace grid pre-blurs ONE backdrop element"** `bug` `#docs` `#performance` (added 2026-08-07)
  `globals.css:1117` states the marketplace grid "avoids the same cost by pre-blurring ONE backdrop element", and the Bugs entry above about `FilesTab.tsx:393` repeats it as settled. A search during the 2026-08-07 animation audit could not find any rule implementing it: `MarketplaceScreen.tsx:281` does render `<WallpaperBackdrop/>`, but the theme-engine's `[data-wallpaper] .in-view .layer-surface` rule still matches the cards themselves (`MarketplaceCard.tsx:246`), which would mean N live blur layers, not one. Either the comment is wrong or the rule is missing — both are worth knowing, and the comment is currently load-bearing for a "don't worry about it" decision. Resolve it, then fix whichever side is wrong. Related and still genuinely open: the same question for `FilesTab.tsx:393` doc/folder cards.

- [x] **`bash scripts/ast-grep/check.sh` is RED on `master`** `bug` `#tooling` (added 2026-08-07, **FIXED 2026-08-06/09 — youcoded `feb76640`, merged via `8c62ca51`. check.sh re-verified green 2026-08-12 (5/5 fixture rules fire, zero real-source violations).**)
  **The entry's premise was wrong: the rule was RIGHT.** `tool-bounds-not-hand-rolled` was not over-broadened and never matched a "fragment-resolution note" — it was correctly firing on two genuine hand-rolled `[body truncated at 5MB]` strings in `web-fetch.ts:160,169`. The fix (`feb76640`, "WebFetch overstated what it showed") removed the hand-rolled strings from the tool so the cap is declared via bounds; no rule carve-out was needed. The only remaining regex-shaped text in the file lives inside `//` WHY comments, which the AST rule doesn't match.

- [ ] **Resumed native sessions with no *stored* title still show `Resuming…` until their next completed turn** `bug` `#native-runtime` `#renderer` (added 2026-08-06, residue of youcoded PR #286)
  PR #286 fixed the two structural halves of the stuck-pill bug: an already-titled session now gets its stored title re-pushed on resume (`native-resume-title.ts`), and a never-titled one can finally generate a title (the `'Resuming…'` placeholder no longer fools `hasTitle`). The remaining gap is the case where the store has **no** title at all — a conversation predating the title feeder, one where all 3 generation attempts failed (offline provider), or one where `getBinding` returned null. `reapplyStoredTitle` reads only `ConversationRecord.title`, whose sole native writer is the feeder's own `onTitle`, so it finds nothing and no-ops. Meanwhile the **Resume Browser row the user just clicked shows a real name** — derived from the conversation's first user message (`harness/session-store.ts:220-228`, overlaid by the store title only when it is real, `session-browser.ts:518`). So the row reads "help me refactor the auth module" and the pill it opens reads `Resuming…`. It self-heals at the next `turn-complete`, so it only bites someone who resumes purely to re-read a transcript.
  Fix = have the re-apply fall back to that derived title. **Held because it is a copy decision, not a mechanical one**: derived titles are raw first-message text, so planting one on the pill makes the pill look different from a generated title. Decide whether that is better or worse than the placeholder before implementing. Same gap exists CC-side when the topic file has been pruned.

- [ ] **Adopt the deferred ESLint rules — 79 renderer floating promises and 43 exhaustive-deps hits are still unguarded** `bug` `#tooling` `#renderer` (added 2026-08-06, follow-up to youcoded `51d859e4`)
  The new lint gate (`desktop/eslint.config.mjs`) enables only the 21 rules already at zero, so it fails on new defects and nothing else. The rules that still fire are listed at the bottom of that file with measured counts (all / `src/main` only) so adoption can be priced rather than re-measured: `no-unnecessary-condition` 631/224, `require-await` 152/93, `no-unnecessary-type-assertion` 138/41, `return-await` 111/106, **`no-floating-promises` (renderer) 79/0**, **`exhaustive-deps` 43/0**, `no-promise-executor-return` 26/24, `require-atomic-updates` 21/13, `no-cond-assign` 14/4, `no-base-to-string` 13/10.
  **`exhaustive-deps` is the highest-value one** — those are stale-closure bugs. Caveat from this session: both security-adjacent hits I spot-checked (ToolCard's "Always allow" decision, ResumeBrowser's skip-permissions default) turned out to be *correctly* hand-maintained, so expect a high false-positive rate and adopt file-by-file rather than in one sweep. Renderer `no-floating-promises` is real but each site needs a judgement call about the right recovery, which is why it was scoped to `src/main/**` where a rejection can kill the process. Drive a rule to zero in the same commit that enables it — a gate shipping with known violations trains everyone to ignore it.

- [ ] **`desktop/tests/` (347 files) is neither type-checked nor linted** `bug` `#tooling` (added 2026-08-06)
  `tsconfig.json`'s `include` is `src/**/*`, so `tsc --noEmit` never sees the test tree — vitest executes those files with esbuild stripping types without checking them. The new ESLint config is scoped to `src/**` for the same reason: type-aware rules need the files in a TS project. One tests tsconfig unlocks both at once. `scripts/verify.sh` already states this limitation in its header; this is the fix for it.

- [x] **`ToolCard` interpolates `unknown`-typed tool inputs straight into chat-visible strings** `bug` `#renderer` (added 2026-08-06, from the 2026-08-06 lint sweep; **FIXED 2026-08-12 — youcoded PR #295, merge `3dac8828`.** Turned out much bigger than the three named sites: the review found the same class INCLUDED live crashes — `basename(input.path as string)` in Grep/Glob/Read/Write/Edit branches, `.replace`/`.trimStart`/`.includes` on lie-cast strings — any of which took down the whole Chat ErrorBoundary pane. Every interpolated field in `friendlyToolDisplay` now goes through an `asString()` helper or a typeof guard; 21 tests, all red-first. Twin shape in `tool-views/ToolBody.tsx` + `AskUserQuestionCard` deep fields ticketed separately below.)
  `ToolCard.tsx:99/103/120` render `input.glob`, `input.type` and `input.subagent_type` into the tool card label/detail with no string cast, while the sibling lines in the same switch DO cast (`input.path as string`, `input.pattern as string`). These are `unknown` off the tool-input JSON, so a non-string value renders as `[object Object]` in the timeline. Low severity — the models emit strings in practice — but it is a three-line fix and the inconsistency with the adjacent lines is the tell. Caught by `@typescript-eslint/no-base-to-string`, which is in the deferred list above (13 hits total).

- [ ] **Android `SessionService.kt` dereferences `bootstrap!!` in ~18 IPC handlers** `bug` `#android` (added 2026-08-06, reachability UNVERIFIED)
  Roughly 18 handlers do `File(bootstrap!!.homeDir, …)`; if any is reachable before bootstrap completes it is a hard NPE. The team is already aware of the pattern in one spot — `SessionService.kt:2537/2577` carry comments about a sign-out window making "a re-read + `!!` throw". **I could not verify whether any handler is actually reachable pre-bootstrap**: there is no Android SDK on this machine (`ANDROID_HOME` unset, no `~/Android/Sdk`), so `./gradlew lint` and `./gradlew test` both fail at SDK resolution and the 18 Kotlin test files never ran. This came from `rg` pattern-matching only — establish reachability before treating it as a real bug or before touching anything.

- [ ] **Model favourites are localStorage-only, and they are now the picker's default view** `bug` `#renderer` `#sync` (added 2026-07-31, from youcoded#279)
  `ModelPicker` opens showing favourites and nothing else until you type, which is the design Destin picked — but the favourites live in `localStorage` under `youcoded-model-favorites`, so a second device opens the picker empty with no indication why. Needs a real synced channel (the Conversation Store already syncs per-conversation state; this is per-USER state, which has no lane yet). Low severity until someone actually runs two devices, which Destin does.

- [ ] **The `bg-inset` FIELD surface is invisible on `bg-inset` hosts — patched at three call sites, not fixed** `bug` `#renderer` `#ui-consistency` (added 2026-07-31, from youcoded#279)
  `FIELD_SURFACE` is `bg-inset`, and so is every card/sheet a field gets nested in, so the control ends up exactly the colour of its own background — it reads as a label, not something you can type in. Destin reported it once (the model picker's trigger); it was then found twice more on the same branch (close prompt editor, resume tag sheet) and each was patched with a `bg-well` override at the call site. `field.ts` predicted this in a comment — "inputs on `bg-inset/50` cards now sit closer to their background than before… the alternative (bg-well inside inset cards) was offered during review and not taken." Three occurrences in one branch says the default is wrong for nested hosts rather than that N call sites each need an override. Proper fix is a decision about the primitive, not a fourth patch.

- [x] **Resume Browser takes ~1s to open and ~280ms per search keystroke — the list is not windowed** `bug` `#renderer` `#performance` (added + shipped 2026-07-31, `f8ca631b`, merged in youcoded#279)
  Destin: "it definitely does feel slow… i've been wanting to optimize it for performance for a while." Measured, not estimated, at his real scale of ~1,642 rows: **948ms** from click to committed list (**37,920 DOM nodes**), **278ms** per search keystroke; against 225ms/546 nodes/48ms with 5 rows. `ResumeBrowser.tsx:1193` maps every row on every render with no virtualization, ~23 DOM nodes per card, and `renderSessionRow` is an unmemoized closure so all 1,642 subtrees reconcile on any state change.
  **File IO is NOT the cause and is already ruled out** — the whole of `listPastSessions()` measures **0.06s** against the real `~/.claude` tree (walk+stat 0.01, tail reads 0.01, head reads 0.03, store list 0.01). An earlier suggestion to skip the transcript scrape for store-covered rows would save ~10ms of a ~1000ms problem; don't.
  **Fix (2026-07-31): chunked reveal, not virtualization.** The list renders 50 items and an IntersectionObserver sentinel tops it up by 50 as the user scrolls. Matched before/after at 1,642 rows, median of 5 runs: open **804ms → 96ms** (script 425→72, style 194→13, layout 99→8), DOM nodes **37,920 → 1,585**, keystrokes **220/319/183ms → 64/83/100ms**. Open time is now **flat in conversation count** (90ms at 5 rows, 96ms at 1,642, 85ms at 4,000).
  Two pre-measurement hypotheses were **disproved** and should not be re-derived: `React.memo` on the row could never have fixed the OPEN (a first render has nothing to reuse — all the script and style cost still happens), and the filter pipeline never needed debouncing (keystroke cost shows no trend with session count). Remaining trade-off, accepted deliberately: the scrollbar is proportional to what is revealed, and scrolling through many hundreds of rows re-accumulates DOM. True windowing is the upgrade if deep scrolling ever becomes a real usage pattern.
  Full diagnosis, the two counting traps (subagent transcripts inflate the transcript count 2.2×; all 548 browsable transcripts DO have store records), and the reproducible harness: `docs/archive/handoffs/2026-07-31-resume-browser-load-time-handoff.md`.

- [ ] **Window-resize lag has a SECOND cause, unidentified — the inactive-session layout fix does not explain all of it** `bug` `#renderer` `#linux` `#perf` (added 2026-08-06)
  The identified cause is **fixed on master (`81c9562d`, 2026-08-06 — "perf(renderer): take inactive sessions out of layout during resize"; verified live at `ChatView.tsx:706`)**: `App.tsx` mounts a `ChatView` per open session and hid inactive ones with `visibility:hidden`, which does NOT leave the layout tree, so every resize tick re-laid-out all six of Destin's open conversations. Keying `content-visibility` on a new `sessionActive` prop measured 117 → 43 ms/step with long tasks 13 → 0, and made session switching *faster* than the behaviour it replaced (5.3 → 0.5 ms median; `display:none` would have cost 25.3 ms). Verified end-to-end in the workbench.
  **What that does not explain:** a dev window with ONE session open — zero inactive panes, so the fix is inert there — still lagged. The probe from that run showed 468 resize events, 1 long task (150 ms), and frame gaps of 230/153/94/45 ms. Big gaps with almost no blocking script means paint/raster/compositing, not layout or JS.
  **Suspects, none measured:** the 82 `backdrop-filter` sites + wallpaper layer (the earlier reduce-effects test does NOT clear these — it ran on the live app where the six-session layout cost would have masked any paint cost); 1.5× fractional scale on 2560×1600 @ 180 Hz (5.5 ms frame budget); and two always-mounted chrome paths that cost the same at one session as at six — `SessionStrip.tsx:741` (`new ResizeObserver(() => repack())`, unthrottled) and `HeaderBar.tsx` (recounted 2026-08-12: **3** `ResizeObserver`s at :121/:321/:384, not the 5-plus-MutationObserver previously written here; the cluster-reservation measure-then-write loop is still real).
  **Also unverified:** everything was measured on a Vite dev build, never the packaged app where the 1–2 s stall was actually reported. Next step is a Chromium trace with paint/raster categories over a resize sweep plus an effects-on/off A/B in the workbench; the harness for it is `scripts/resize-bench.mjs` (its header carries the probe snippet and how to read the output).

- [ ] **Audit the other repeated `.layer-surface` grids for the same per-tile blur cost** `bug` `#renderer` `#themes` (added 2026-07-31, follow-up to youcoded#277)
  #277 fixed the command drawer, where 20–40 tiles each carried their own `backdrop-filter` under `[data-wallpaper]` and Windows Electron dropped their paint (cards blinking in and out on halftone-dimension). The fix is scoped to `.command-drawer` on purpose. **Not yet checked: `FilesTab.tsx:393` doc/folder cards**, also `.layer-surface` in a grid — I never established what backdrop they sit over, so I don't know whether their blur is visible (keep it) or a no-op over an opaque parent (cancel it, as the drawer now does). No flicker reported there; the parent isn't `transform`-animating, so the likely symptom is wasted GPU rather than dropped paint. Same question applies to any future grid — the rule in `.claude/rules/react-renderer.md` → Overlays now states the test ("what's actually behind this tile?"). ~~Marketplace grid is already answered: it sits over `<WallpaperBackdrop />`, blur is visible, and it pre-blurs one element instead of N.~~ **That last claim is exactly what the "Unverified claim" entry above disputes — the two entries contradict each other, and the entry above has the evidence (`[data-wallpaper] .layer-surface` still matches the cards; the only carve-out is `.command-drawer`-scoped). Treat the marketplace grid as UNanswered until that entry resolves.**

- [ ] **Transcript storage has no long-term plan — GitHub will run out for everyone, and large transcripts are already unsyncable** `feature` `#sync` `#conversations` (added 2026-07-30, needs a design pass)
  Surfaced by the "Personal sync is large" notice on the Z13 and then measured, not estimated. **Three problems, one root:** conversation transcripts are append-only JSONL that grow without bound, and the sync system stores them as full copies in a git repo on GitHub.
  **(1) The ceiling is real and everyone hits it.** Z13's Personal space: **841 MB local / 652 MB on GitHub**, against GitHub's 1 GB soft limit and 5 GB hard limit. A single project space (`PAF 574`) is already **439 MB** on the same trajectory. This is not a power-user edge case — it is what any daily user reaches on a long enough timeline, and the failure mode at the limit is GitHub refusing pushes, i.e. sync stops for everyone on that account.
  **(2) There is NO pruning logic anywhere.** Nothing ages out, compacts, or tiers transcripts — not by size, not by date, not by device. `snapshot-retention.ts` prunes *Drive/iCloud backup snapshots* only; the sync spaces themselves grow monotonically forever. Confirmed: history compaction would NOT save this — measured on a throwaway clone, squashing away **all** 75,566 historical blob versions lands at **611 MB**, still over the 500 MiB warn threshold, because the bulk is *current content* (3,415 files / 1,892 MB uncompressed), not history. So the documented "manual compaction procedure" is not the answer and should not be reached for.
  **(3) Large transcripts are effectively unsyncable, and the UX for that lies.** `MAX_SYNC_FILE_BYTES` is 50 MB, so today everything rides; but the content is extremely concentrated — **68 transcripts (4% of files) hold 736 MB**, with nine over 20 MB and one at 43 MB — while all 1,673 conversation *records* total **1 MB**. Metadata is nearly free; transcripts are the entire cost. The obvious lever (lower the cap) is blocked by a UX truth problem: a record whose transcript is absent renders as **"Not synced to this device yet"** (`session-browser.ts:520` `fs.existsSync` probe → `notSyncedYet` → disabled row in `ResumeBrowser.tsx:670`). That wording is built for a *temporary* gap where waiting works. A size-excluded transcript would never arrive, so the message would name a false cause and imply a resolution that never comes — exactly what `docs/error-message-standards.md` forbids. Related asymmetry found in the same pass: the backend resume refusal ("This conversation hasn't synced to this device yet") exists **only** in the native lane (`ipc-handlers.ts:577`); the CC lane relies on the UI gate alone.
  **The goal to design toward: every transcript available on every device, always.** Destin's framing (2026-07-30) — GitHub may simply be the wrong permanent store for transcripts specifically, in which case options include a different backing store for transcript bytes (records keep riding git, since they're 1 MB), on-demand fetch from a peer or a blob store, or folding this into a future **YouCoded-managed cloud offered at cheap-ish rates** (which would also give the app a first-party answer to "where does my data live"). Whatever it becomes, it needs an honest not-here state and a fetch affordance, since the "yet" wording only survives if absence stays temporary. Not scoped yet — deliberately parked for a proper design pass.
  Measured during the 2026-07-27 corruption repair session; see `docs/archive/specs/2026-07-30-sync-corruption-self-heal-design.md` for the sibling work (that spec fixes silent sync failure, NOT this).

- [ ] Permission asks expire after 5 min and wedge the session silently — AskUserQuestion is the acute case `bug` `#permissions` `#desktop` `#android` (added 2026-07-30; **BUILT 2026-07-31 — youcoded PR #278, branch `feat/permission-ask-timeout`, worktree `worktrees/perm-timeout`, 20 commits, +1868/-150. PR is OPEN, not a draft, and has NEVER been reviewed (`reviews: []`, `updatedAt == createdAt`). `mergeable: CONFLICTING` — 663 commits behind, 8 conflicted files, the load-bearing two being `chat-reducer.ts` and `ToolCard.tsx`, where August's full-auto safety-stop work rebuilt the same permission render this branch extends. Also unexecutable at rebase: plan Tasks 7/8 edit `closeSocket()`, deleted from both platforms 2026-08-22 (`17931fc0`). The tier-1 magnitude question the spec calls open was SETTLED at 2h on 2026-07-31 and shipped that way.**)
  Design settled: `docs/active/specs/2026-07-30-permission-ask-timeout-design.md`. A chat-view permission ask dies at ~5 min with no indication anywhere while CC is still blocked. `install-hooks.js:126` registers the hook at `timeout: 300` and `relay-blocking.js:22` uses the same `300000` ms — nominally identical, but CC's clock starts first, so **CC always wins** and kills the hook with no decision (the spec's later reviews corrected the first draft's "they race" reading). Either way the socket closes, `chat-reducer.ts:1234` flips the card to `status:'failed'`, and nothing escalates: `useAttentionClassifier` is gated on `visible` (`ChatView.tsx:200`), so a background session is never classified at all.
  **Why AskUserQuestion is the one that bites:** CC's own `askUserQuestionTimeout` ("Question auto-continue timeout", options `["never","60s","5m","10m"]`) **defaults to `never`** — verified in the CC binary at `~/.local/share/claude/versions/2.1.220`. So CC waits indefinitely and our hook is the only clock; when it fires the session wedges permanently. It's also the only prompt still routed to chat in bypass mode (`main.ts:889` never auto-approves it), and the slowest to answer. Android is worse: its relay asset defaults to **120s**.
  Fix (per the spec, now through three review passes) = the app owns the clock: tier-1 app hold (magnitude open — 24h vs recommended 2h, Destin's call) < relay backstop < CC backstop, 30-min margins each (CC-wins is the bad outcome: hook killed with no decision, and AskUserQuestion then waits forever on its `never` default; a deny releasing the turn is now verified in the CC binary), plus a menu-present/menu-absent discriminator on `PERMISSION_EXPIRED` so answering in the terminal clears the card quietly instead of stamping it "expired", plus digit-rebind for single-select asks only. **Two traps found in review:** `Bootstrap.kt:992-1018` guards the whole write behind `if (!prRegistered)`, so Android existing installs would never receive the new value — changing the literal alone ships dead; and `relay-blocking.js:19-21` documents the *opposite* intent ("relay must not fire first"), so it needs a rewrite, not a renumber, or a future session reverts the margin. Also uncovered a live pre-existing hole: every stray-Enter guard (`hasPendingPermission`, `hasPendingInteraction`) goes false at expiry while CC's Ink menu is still on screen.

- [x] `McpManager` keys holders by session id, so a resumed session's release closes the connection its own re-acquire just took `bug` `#native-runtime` `#mcp` (added 2026-07-31, found in the native-MCP phase 1 final review)
  A resumed session reuses its session id. `NativeSessionHost.destroy()` releases the outgoing generation while `create()`/`resume()` acquires for the incoming one, under the same id — and `McpManager`'s holder set is keyed by that id, so it cannot tell the two generations apart. The shipped mitigation defers the close until the owning `acquire()` settles, then runs it: the incoming session is handed a live connection that closes one microtask later, and its tool calls return `"<server> is not connected."` for the rest of the session.
  That is a deliberate trade, not an oversight — the alternative (skip the close when an acquire is in flight) leaks the spawned subprocess, which three regression tests in `mcp-manager.test.ts` pin. This same bug was found and relocated three times during phase 1 before landing here; the reason it keeps moving is that the id is the wrong key.
  **Fix:** `acquire()` returns an opaque handle and `release()` takes it, so two generations of one session id are distinguishable. Comment recording the exact limitation is at `mcp-manager.ts`'s `release()`.

- [ ] Native MCP phase 2 — settings UI, the adopt flow, and IPC `feature` `#native-runtime` `#mcp` (added 2026-08-05, deferred by phase 1 which merged as youcoded#280)
  Phase 1 is **developer-operable only**: the only way to configure a server is hand-writing `~/.youcoded/mcp.json`. There is no settings UI, no way to add a server from inside the app, no adopt flow for servers already in `~/.claude.json`, and no IPC surface — so a non-technical user cannot use MCP at all today. Projection runs one way (YouCoded → Claude Code); a marketplace MCP visible in Claude Code is still invisible to native sessions.
  Carries one known Low from the phase 1 final review, unreachable until this ships: `mcp-registry.ts` `upsert()` filters with the strict `fromStored` predicate, so the first `upsert()` would permanently DELETE a hand-edited entry whose id is empty or contains `__`, where `remove()` preserves it. `upsert()` has no production caller today, which is the only reason it is harmless.
  Also deferred: folding the plugin-manifest scan into the registry, so plugin-bundled and user-configured servers share one ownership rule instead of two code paths in `mcp-reconciler.ts`.

- [x] Ten atomic-write call sites still use a FIXED `<file>.tmp`, which is not atomic against a concurrent writer `bug` `#desktop` `#reliability` (added 2026-08-05, found while merging native-MCP phase 1; **FIXED 2026-08-12 — youcoded PR #296, merge `0a325b99`, plus workspace guard commit `e9ca14b`.** All eleven sites (the ten plus `github-client.ts` and `project-manager.ts` which the original count missed) now use pid-suffixed temp names; the review caught that pid names turn crash orphans PERMANENT, so cas-write and the two user-tree writers gained `sweepStaleTmp` (transcript-mirror's pattern) + error-path unlinks; `update-installer.ts`'s `.partial` deliberately kept fixed (load-bearing for crash recovery). The wished-for ast-grep rule EXISTS now (`atomic-tmp-name-per-process`, fires 13/13 on the old shapes, silent on fixed source). Two parked follow-ups: the `*.tmp`-in-DEFAULT_IGNORES product decision (own entry above) and optionally tightening the sweep match to `^base(\.\d+(\.\d+)?)?\.tmp$`.)
  Write-temp-then-rename protects against a crash mid-write. It does NOT protect against a second writer: both processes write the same temp path, the first rename consumes it, and the second throws `ENOENT` renaming a file that no longer exists. This is reachable in normal use — `~/.claude/` is SHARED between the installed app and a dev instance (`run-dev.sh` isolates userData and ports, not the home dir), so two YouCoded processes writing the same config file is the ordinary case, not a corner one.
  `skill-config-store.ts` was fixed on the native-MCP branch because it blocked that merge (its parallel-worker failure is the same two-writer shape). The remaining sites, verified by `rg` over `src/main` (re-verified 2026-08-12): `claude-code-registry.ts:119`, `marketplace-config-store.ts:41`, `marketplace-auth-store.ts:84`, `announcement-service.ts:33`, `artifacts/cas-write.ts:75`, `changelog-service.ts:50`, `sync-spaces/space-manager.ts:86`, `integration-installer.ts:93`, `ipc-handlers.ts:3717` (was :3576) — **plus two sites the original count missed: `github-client.ts:217` and `artifacts/project-manager.ts:67-68`**, so eleven total, not ten.
  The fix is mechanical and the convention already exists in this codebase — `mcp-reconciler.ts`, `hook-reconciler.ts`, `saved-folders.ts` and `transcript-mirror.ts` all use `${path}.${process.pid}.tmp`. Worth an ast-grep rule so the fixed-`.tmp` shape can't come back.

- [x] `window-all-closed` is the app's ONLY quit hook — Cmd+Q, dock quit and SIGTERM skip every teardown `bug` `#desktop` `#lifecycle` (added 2026-07-30, found in the native-MCP phase 1 Task 4 review)
  `main.ts` registers exactly one quit-related listener: `app.on('window-all-closed')` (`main.ts:1915-1942`), which calls `ipc-handlers.ts`'s `cleanup()`. There is **no** `before-quit`, `will-quit`, `quit` or `SIGTERM` handler anywhere in the file — verified by a repo-wide search during the Task 4 review, not inferred. So any quit route that does not go through window-all-closed first runs none of the teardown: macOS Cmd+Q and dock-quit call `app.quit()` directly (firing `before-quit`/`will-quit`/`quit`), and an OS-shutdown/logout SIGTERM bypasses Electron's quit events entirely on every platform.
  **What leaks:** `sessionManager.destroyAll()`, `hookRelay.stop()`, and — as of the native-MCP branch — `mcpManager.destroyAll()`, whose whole purpose is keeping a stdio MCP server's spawned subprocess from outliving the app. An MCP subprocess surviving a Cmd+Q is a real user-facing bug, not a theoretical one, and it is the exact failure the MCP teardown step was written to prevent.
  Pre-existing and app-wide, so deliberately NOT patched inside the MCP branch — the MCP teardown correctly hooks in beside the others. Fix is a `before-quit`/`will-quit` handler (idempotent with the existing one, since both can fire) plus a SIGTERM handler; `native-session-host.ts`'s `destroyAll()` carries a comment recording the exact scope limit so a future reader does not mistake the hook for full coverage.

- [ ] Buddy chat's input bar never consults the PTY gate at all — a typed message can land in a live Ink menu `bug` `#buddy` `#pty-io` (added 2026-07-31, found by the permission-ask-timeout final review)
  `components/buddy/BuddyChat.tsx:178` (path corrected 2026-08-12) renders `<InputBar sessionId={viewedSession} compact />` without `getSessionState`, `onSendBlocked`, or `onToast`. `InputBar.sendMessage`'s gate check needs `getSessionState` to resolve a session, so with it absent the whole guarded block — refusal, toast, and "Send anyway" affordance — never executes in buddy chat. A message typed there while a permission/AskUserQuestion/plan menu is live goes straight to the PTY, and its trailing `\r` confirms the highlighted option. Same class as youcoded#110, which the gate was built to close on the main input bar. Pre-existing and untouched by the timeout branch, but that branch makes `awaiting-approval` states long-lived (a 2h hold instead of a 5-min expiry), which widens the window this is exposed in. Fix is wiring the three props; the harder question is what the buddy overlay's refusal UI should look like at 320px.

- [ ] `HookRelay` can drop a permission expiry entirely in one narrow ordering `bug` `#permissions` `#desktop` (added 2026-07-31, found by the permission-ask-timeout final review)
  If the tier-1 hold fires while the socket is already `destroyed` but its `'close'` event has not yet dispatched, `respond()` deletes the pending entry and returns false (so the hold correctly does not claim an auto-deny), and then the `'close'` handler's `wasOpen` guard finds the entry gone and also stays silent — zero expiry events for that request. The card keeps rendering as a live ask with a dead socket. It degrades honestly rather than wedging: clicking any button gets `delivered === false`, which dispatches `PERMISSION_EXPIRED` with `reason: 'delivery-failed'` and resolves the card with accurate copy. Narrow, and not introduced by the timeout branch, but the cleanest fix is for desktop's `respond()` to emit `'delivery-failed'` itself on the not-delivered path the way Android's does, instead of leaving that recovery only in the renderer.

- [x] `HookRelay.closeSocket()` has zero call sites `bug` `#permissions` `#desktop` (added 2026-07-31, found by the permission-ask-timeout final review) **SHIPPED 2026-08-22 — merged `17931fc0` (branch fix/roadmap-easy-knockouts; plan: `docs/archive/plans/2026-08-22-roadmap-easy-knockout-batch.md`)**
  Dead code, and knip does not flag it because it is an exported class method. It is symmetric with Android's `EventBridge.closeSocket()`, which also has no callers — so either both are a deliberate API surface for a cross-path cleanup that was never wired, or both should go. The timeout branch kept it correct (it clears its hold timer) rather than deleting it mid-feature.

- [ ] Plan-approval buttons send arrows+Enter in ONE write — the shape measured broken on CC 2.1.220 `bug` `#desktop` `#pty-io` (added 2026-07-30, found during the permission-ask-timeout spec's third review)
  `ToolCard.tsx:472-473` (`PlanApprovalButtons`) sends `DOWN.repeat(optionIndex) + '\r'` as a single `sendInput` — exactly what `.claude/rules/pty-io.md` forbids and what `ink-select-parser.ts:345-351` measured on CC 2.1.220 (2026-07-26, the Resume Session bug root cause): CC drops arrows sharing a write with the Enter and confirms the HIGHLIGHTED option. If that measurement holds here, every plan-approval click commits option 1 regardless of which button was pressed. Needs a quick dev-instance check to confirm, then the `input`/`submitInput` two-write split. Independent of the permission-timeout redesign — fixable on its own.

- [x] Desktop app burns CPU continuously at idle — measure, attribute, fix `bug` `#perf` `#desktop` (added 2026-07-30, **fixed 2026-07-30 — youcoded PR #275; PR #274 closed, premise disproven**)
  Root cause was **not** the lease/heartbeat lead (renderer JS 99% idle, `pty:output` 0 bytes). It is a platform property: **on a high-refresh display, ANY smoothly-animating element makes Chromium present a frame per refresh at ~1.5–1.9ms of CPU each — ~29% of one core at 180Hz — regardless of element size or property.** Not app-specific: the identical pulsing div measured 29.8% in YouCoded, **33.7% in a bare Electron window with zero app code, and 26.7% in plain Google Chrome**. Cost is per-FRAME, not per-element (8 divs ≈ 1 div; an 8px layer-promoted transform animation still cost 31.9%).
  **Correction trail:** PR #274 shipped `will-change` layer promotion claiming 12× (43% → 3.6%); re-measurement disproved it (29.8% → 27.8%) — the original "promoted" reading was taken against a freshly-restored window that was not yet presenting (same artifact as the acknowledged bogus 0 fps reading). An early steps() test was likewise contaminated by probe-window occlusion. Lesson: **window presentation state is part of any perf experiment — re-run the baseline in the same window state before trusting an A/B.**
  The levers that actually work, both shipped in **PR #275**: (1) **fewer frames** — `steps()` timing on decorative animations (smooth 28.75% → `steps(8)` 9.33%): `.animate-pulse`/breathe/challenge-pulse at steps(8) (visually indistinguishable), `.animate-spin` at steps(12) (deliberate 12-tick spinner look). This is the per-animation form of Destin's "cap it to 30fps" suggestion — which was directionally right; there is no app-global knob (Electron `setFrameRate` is offscreen-only, Chromium switches only remove caps). (2) **zero frames** — Reduced Effects now actually stops CSS animations (`data-reduced-effects` previously had ZERO css rules gating on it; 48.78% → 2.44%), and `.flowing-word` is finite (plays twice, settles — its `background-position` paint is main-thread, immune to both promotion and steps()). Original 35.6% repro scenario: now **11.0%** with the animation running, ~2.5% with none.
  Also corrected from the handoff: the 104–133% live-app readings were **not idle** (the measuring session ran inside the app alongside 6 other streaming CC sessions ≈ 99% of a core); hidden windows were always fine (Chromium throttles them, 3.9% minimized) — the problem is exclusively visible-and-idle. 180Hz machines pay 3× a 60Hz laptop.
  Guards: `desktop/tests/animation-frame-budget.test.ts` (pins every quantization + bans new inline smooth-infinite animations in components/), `desktop/tests/reduced-effects-animations.test.ts`, `scripts/measure-idle-cpu.mjs` (flags SMOOTH animations vs `frame-budgeted: steps(N)`; refuses to sample the packaged app). Write-up incl. full correction history: docs/archive/investigations/2026-07-30-idle-cpu-burn.md.
  Follow-ups: ~~`model-load-sweep` animates `left`~~ and ~~mascot/buddy SVG keyframes~~ **both closed 2026-08-07** (branch `perf/transcript-hover-frames`) — the sweep now uses `translateX` (no layout pass), and the mascot's five rig loops plus its 33ms limb interval now pause on `visibilitychange` rather than being quantized, since `steps()` on character motion reads as juddering. `buddy-breathe` is deliberately NOT fixed: the buddy window is `alwaysOnTop`, so `visibilitychange` never fires, and pausing breathing makes the character look dead — recorded as an accepted cost in `ANIMATION_EXCEPTIONS` (`animation-frame-budget.test.ts`); ~~three full-rate rAF loops~~ (all fixed in PR #275 — BrailleSpinner/ThemeEffects on intervals, MascotRig dual-rate with rAF only while dragging; rig themes were presenting 180 sway frames/sec since idleSway never lets springs park); a session that never initializes still shows a permanent "Initializing session…" overlay (UX bug, no longer a CPU one); and whether this machine's per-frame cost is inflated by the missing `DC_DISABLE_IPS` amdgpu flag (measure after the kernel-param fix, or at 60Hz).

- [ ] verify.sh related-tests fail in symlinked-node_modules worktrees — Vite "Denied ID …?inline" `bug` `#tooling` (added 2026-07-30; **WORKAROUND PROVEN 2026-08-12: replace the symlink with a hardlink farm — `cp -al youcoded/desktop/node_modules <worktree>/desktop/node_modules` — realpaths then stay inside the worktree root and Vite stops denying; verified in the ToolCard PR #295 worktree where the symlink failed 5 test files and `cp -al` passed everything. A real fix in vitest.config.ts `server.fs.allow` would still be better.**)
  On Linux, worktrees share the main checkout's `desktop/node_modules` via a symlink (mirroring the Windows junction convention). Vitest resolves `highlight.js/styles/github-dark.css?inline` through the symlink to its REAL path under `youcoded/desktop/node_modules`, which sits outside the worktree's Vite `server.fs` allow-list → "Denied ID", failing every test file that imports theme-context — while the identical file passes on the main checkout. Confirmed environmental 2026-07-30 (fails with the working diff stashed). Bites any worktree session whose diff touches a file whose related tests import theme-context; CI is unaffected (real node_modules). Likely fix: add the workspace root (or `searchForWorkspaceRoot`) to `server.fs.allow` in `desktop/vitest.config.ts` — note verify.sh auto-escalates to the full suite when vitest.config.ts changes, so land it as its own tiny PR.

- [x] `.flowing-word` keyword gradient burns ~40% of a core whenever a keyword is on screen `bug` `#perf` `#desktop` (added 2026-07-30, **fixed 2026-07-30 — youcoded PR #275**)
  `.flowing-word` (used by `FlowingKeywords.tsx` + `UserMessage.tsx`) animated `background-position` on an infinite loop — main-thread painted, so **no rendering trick helps**: plain 43.8%, `will-change: background-position` 43.0%, forced layer via `will-change: transform` 65.9% (vs 66.1% unpromoted), `steps(24)` still ~40%. Fixed by making it **finite**: plays twice on appearance (~6s of flow, preserving the draw-the-eye purpose), then settles to the static start gradient — screenshot-verified legible. Cost after settling: zero. Reduced Effects stops it entirely. Guarded by `animation-frame-budget.test.ts` (`infinite` on this rule fails the suite). The fancier alternative (transform-driven gradient overlay behind `background-clip: text`) is no longer needed for perf; only worth revisiting if Destin wants the endless-flow look back.

- [x] Sync transport converts git failures into success — a crash-corrupted repo syncs "green" forever `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-30, **MERGED 2026-07-30 — youcoded PR #276, merge 5a787879**; spec + plan archived to `docs/archive/`)
  Found live on the Z13: three hard freezes (Jul 27–29, the EC-wedge machine) left 16 zero-byte loose objects in the Personal space's `sync.git`; `refs/heads/main` pointed at one of them, so every git op failed `fatal: bad object HEAD` — and **sync was dead for three days while the panel showed green and `lastSync` advanced every cycle** (3,381 files staged, never pushed). Two defects: (1) `git-transport.ts` `git()` never throws and `push()`/`pull()` map failures to `{pushed:false}`/`{updated:false}` — indistinguishable from "nothing to do" — so the engine emits phantom `synced` events that stamp `recordSyncSuccess` (verified by reproducing the transport's exact command sequence against the corrupt repo: commit exits 128 → silent benign return). Only auth failures deliberately surface; every other mode falls into the trap `classifyGitAuthFailure`'s own WHY comment warns about. (2) Zero-byte objects are *poison*: git checks existence by filename, so `add -A` keeps "succeeding" and never rewrites them from the intact worktree — corruption never self-heals. Related third defect, same session (screenshot): the devices-list **self** row reads legacy `.sync-marker` (absent on GitHub-era installs) → "last seen 22 hours ago" on a running machine, while peer rows ride the live SyncHub map; `syncInProgress` stats the legacy `.sync-lock` identically. Approved design (all four parts): benign-allowlist inversion (`classifyGitFailure` — non-zero exit ⇒ error unless allowlisted; corrupt patterns throw coded `repo-corrupt`), two-tier auto-repair (surgical zero-byte purge + reset to `origin/main`, escalate to backup-aside + refetch; once per space per launch; worktree never touched), notice/danger surfacing via existing panel plumbing, and the self-row rewire onto `lastSyncFor` evidence. Sequencing: land PR #248 (same legacy-state family, open since 07-24) first, then honesty before the row rewire — the row is only as truthful as the events feeding it. Z13's own repo to be repaired by hand in the same session (local history keepable — `ca2f6a0c` verified intact; pending app quit).

- [ ] Sync recency counts loop iterations, not contact with the remote `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-30, from the PR #276 whole-branch review)
  `recordSyncSuccess` stamps `lastSync` on any *completed* cycle, and offline is silent by design — so an offline `push()` fails, the recovery `pull()`'s fetch fails silently, the retry push fails, `isNetworkFailureStderr` returns true, nothing throws, and the engine emits `synced` anyway. A device offline for three days polls every 120s and stamps recency every cycle; PR #276 newly surfaces that value on the devices-list **self** row, which then reads "Synced just now" while offline. Not a regression (these semantics predate the branch and the settings row has consumed them since 2026-07-22) — but it is the one place #276's honesty thesis doesn't reach, and #276 corrected `getSelfLastSyncEpochMs`'s docstring rather than the behavior. Fix: stamp recency only when the cycle actually reached the remote (fetch/push exit 0), so "Last synced" means contact. Fold in the related semantics question while there: `getSelfLastSyncEpochMs` takes the **max** across spaces, so one healthy space and two broken ones still reports "just synced". Drift risk to close at the same time — self recency is now derived twice with the same rule, in `service.getSelfLastSyncEpochMs` and `SyncPanel.tsx:380-381`; they agree today by coincidence, not construction.

- [x] Renderer maps neither `repo-corrupt` nor `repo-repair-failed`, and its fallback copy is wrong for one of them `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-30, from the PR #276 whole-branch review; **FIXED 2026-08-12 — youcoded PR #299, merge `a8b0a290`**: both codes now have explicit mappings with restart-based copy whose every claim the review traced to code — repair writes only under `.youcoded/`, `healedSpaces` is in-memory so restart genuinely retries, and the offline-corrupt corner (separate entry below) surfaces different copy via a different path so nothing overpromises.)
  `summarizeSpaceSyncError` (`sync-space-error-summary.ts`) special-cases only `'github-auth'`, so both error codes PR #276 introduced fall to the generic tail: *"Sync hit an unexpected problem. It will keep retrying automatically."* For `repo-corrupt` that is merely vague. For `repo-repair-failed` it is **reassuring and wrong within the launch** — the sync does keep retrying, but the heal is guarded to one attempt per space per launch and will not run again. Accurate copy is closer to "Sync couldn't repair itself — restart the app to try again." Renderer work was explicitly out of #276's scope; this is the highest-priority of its follow-ups. Both codes are exported constants (`REPO_CORRUPT_ERROR_CODE`, `REPO_REPAIR_FAILED_ERROR_CODE`) — match on those, never on prose.

- [ ] A corrupt sync repo that is *also* offline never heals, though Tier 2 is offline-capable `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-30, from the PR #276 whole-branch review)
  `hasRemote()` runs `git remote get-url origin` and returns `code === 0` with no corruption guard, so a repo damaged badly enough that git refuses to open it (zero-byte `HEAD`, truncated `config`) reads as "no remote". `engine.syncSpace` then takes the `ensureProvisioned` branch **before** any guarded op runs. Online this is only wasted REST work — `setRemote` fails, `pull()`'s `add -A` then throws `repo-corrupt` and the heal proceeds. Offline or signed out, `ensureProvisioned` throws first ("Not connected to GitHub…"), the corruption is never classified, and `repair()` never runs — even though Tier 2 (move aside + re-init) needs no network at all. The user sees an auth/network error for a corrupt repo, every cycle, until they reconnect. Fix: `throwIfCorrupt` inside `hasRemote()`, or a corruption probe before the `ensureProvisioned` branch.

- [x] `repair()` leaves no trace of which tier ran `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-30, from the PR #276 whole-branch review; **FIXED 2026-08-12 — youcoded PR #299, merge `a8b0a290`**: `repair()` returns + logs a `RepairOutcome` {tier, zeroByteObjectsDeleted, tier1Failure, backupPath} via the subsystem's injected logFn; git/fs sequence verified byte-identical. Known residual asymmetry, deliberate: a Tier-2 *failure* exits before the tier-2 log line — the engine's "Sync self-repair failed" error event still carries the cause.)
  On crash-prone hardware "did the heal fire, and which one" is the first question a debug session asks, and nothing on disk answers it: `deleteZeroByteObjects`'s return count is discarded, Tier 1 and Tier 2 emit the **identical** notice, and the only durable artifact is the `.broken-*` directory, which `pruneBrokenBackups` reduces to one. If a user later reports "my sync history changed", there is no record. `service.ts` already has a `log` in scope at engine construction — one line per tier closes it. Smaller items in the same file while there: `tests/sync-spaces-repair.test.ts`'s `cleanup()` is not in a `finally`/`afterEach`, so a failing assertion leaks a temp git repo; `brokenBackupName` has one-second resolution, so two repairs of the same space within one second `ENOTEMPTY` the rename (non-destructive — surfaces as `repo-repair-failed`); and `getSelfLastSyncEpochMs` does a `readdir` plus one uncached `readFileSync` + `JSON.parse` **per space** on every 10s status tick (`SpaceManager.read()` is uncached), which wants a single read + max.

- [ ] Every `tsc`-emitted file ships inside `app.asar`, tests included `bug` `#build` (added 2026-07-29, measured while proving the UI workbench tree-shakes)
  `npm run build` runs `tsc` over the whole `src/` tree into `dist/`, then `vite build` produces the bundle the renderer actually loads (`dist/renderer/assets/`). **Correction 2026-08-12: the electron-builder config is `desktop/electron-builder.yml`, not a `package.json` `build` key — and it DOES have a `files:` allowlist; it's just `dist/**/*`, so the fix is narrowing that yml entry (or narrowing `tsconfig` `include` from `src/**/*` to `src/main/`), not adding a missing field.** `electron-builder` packs all of `dist/` — measured on the 1.2.4 Linux build (re-measured 2026-08-12: now **47**): **44 `.test.js` files** (`dist/main/__tests__/*`, `dist/main/analytics-service.test.js`, …) plus **19 `dist/renderer/dev/workbench/*.js`** files, including `fixture-loader.test.js`. None of it is reachable: `index.html` loads only `assets/`, main loads `dist/main/main.js`, and a grep of the entry chunk for `dev/workbench` returns 0. So this is installer weight and a slightly larger attack surface for anyone unpacking the asar, not executable dead code — which is why it is a `bug` and not a release blocker. Fix is a `build.files` allowlist (or a `tsc` output narrowed to `src/main/`), then re-measure the asar file count. Pre-dates the workbench; the workbench made it 19 files worse and is what surfaced it.

- [ ] Android `EventBridge` maps session ids with no gate at all `bug` `#android-runtime` (added 2026-07-26, found while fixing the desktop wrong-transcript bug)
  `EventBridge.kt:96-98` writes `sessionIdMap[mobileSessionId] = claudeSessionId` on **every** hook event — no `hook_event_name` check, no `source` check, no equivalent of desktop's `resolveMappingAction`. That is strictly looser than desktop's behavior *before* youcoded PR #257: a subagent or tool hook carrying a child session id repoints the mapping, which is exactly the poisoning the desktop gate exists to prevent. Exposure differs — Android reads `transcript_path` straight off the hook payload (`EventBridge.kt:101-104`) rather than reconstructing `<slug>/<id>.jsonl` from the mapping, so the desktop symptom (an unrelated conversation replaying into chat view) may not reproduce identically. Port `resolveMappingAction`'s semantics (first-sighting adopt, same-id ignore, SessionStart-only remap, refuse `startup` on an already-mapped session) and verify on a device before assuming parity. Deliberately NOT changed in #257 — the desktop fix was verified against real process state, and porting blind to a platform I couldn't test would have been guessing.
- [ ] `CLAUDE_DESKTOP_SESSION_ID` leaks into every PTY descendant `bug` `#ipc` (added 2026-07-26, root cause behind the wrong-transcript bug)
  Verified live on the Z13: PID 126721 (a `run-dev.sh` shell spawned from a Bash tool) carried `CLAUDE_DESKTOP_SESSION_ID=ba1ad72e…` and `CLAUDE_DESKTOP_PIPE=/tmp/claude-desktop-hooks-5879.sock` — both inherited verbatim from the CC session that spawned it (PID 7125). So **any** `claude` invoked from inside a session (Bash tool, script, background job) announces its hooks to the built app's relay under the *parent's* desktop session id. That is what let a foreign `SessionStart` repoint a live chat view. `pty-worker.js:241-257` already scrubs CC's own identity vars at spawn and `run-dev.sh:151-153` unsets both, so the hazard class is known — but there is no clean scrub for Bash-tool children, because the session's own hooks need those vars and the tool runs as a child of the same CC process. youcoded PR #257 guards the *consequence* (`source`-gated remap) rather than the leak. A real fix likely means a per-invocation nonce the relay can validate, so an inherited id alone isn't sufficient to claim a desktop session. Worth doing before something else keys off that env var.
- [ ] Transcript replay has no size ceiling `bug` `#chat-reducer` (added 2026-07-26, blast radius of the wrong-transcript bug)
  `TranscriptWatcher.startWatching` begins at `offset: 0`, so any (re)point at an existing JSONL re-emits the entire file as live transcript events. In the 2026-07-26 incident that meant **44 MB / 15,554 lines** parsed and dispatched into the chat reducer in one burst — and the same path also drove a conversation-store `lastActive` upsert and a mirror write for a conversation nobody had opened. Even when the remap is *correct* (a legitimate `/clear` or in-session `/resume`), nothing bounds the work. Worth a cap or a chunked/streamed replay with a progress state.
