Related surface worth auditing at the same time: `App.tsx:2337` seeds chat on resume via `session.loadHistory(claudeSessionId, projectSlug, 10, false)`, and `HistoryExpandButton` (`ChatView.tsx:54-62`) expands it further from `resumeInfo` — both inject history keyed by an explicitly-passed id rather than by the watcher, so they are a second, independent way for a wrong id to put the wrong conversation on screen.
- [ ] Conversation-store title and topic file disagree for the same conversation `bug` `#conversations` (added 2026-07-26, observed while diagnosing the wrong-transcript bug)
  Observed on `ff2e7ceb-c564-4e7f-81ff-59b2085d0409`: the store record (`~/YouCoded/Personal/Conversations/claude/<id>.json`) read `"title": "Fix Ink Menu Keystrokes"` while `~/.claude/topics/topic-<id>` read `Resume Session Menu Broken`. **Re-checked 2026-08-12: the cited pair now AGREES (both "Fix Ink Menu Keystrokes") with no code change — the drift converged on its own, so a fresh repro is needed before any fix.** Both are live sources for the Resume Browser's name precedence (topic file > conversation-index topic > derived-from-first-user-message, per `youcoded/docs/conversations.md`). **Not confirmed to be a defect** — the auto-title hook rewrites the topic file as a conversation's subject drifts, and `store-core.ts` merge rules treat a non-`'Untitled'` title as local truth, so the store legitimately holds an older title. The open question is whether the store is ever *meant* to follow a retitle, and which one a user should see. Investigate before assuming a fix; the risk of "fixing" it wrongly is a title fan-out that clobbers good titles across devices.
- [x] Sound presets cannot be auditioned without assigning them `bug` `#ui` (added 2026-07-26, **SHIPPED 2026-07-26** on `feat/menu-internals-tranche-1`, unmerged)
  `PresetSelector` (`SettingsPanel.tsx:340-351`) fires `onSelect(p.id); playPreview(p.id)` in the same handler, so **the only way to hear a stock sound is to make it your notification sound.** Shopping through the library means clobbering the setting 15 times and then remembering which one you wanted. Compounding it: all **15** presets (`utils/sounds.ts:133-149`) render as `px-2 py-1 rounded text-[10px]` chips in a `flex-wrap`, **twice** — once per category — so the list is both unreadable and duplicated. Fix is in the menu-internals spec §4: one library listed once, a K3 segmented control picking *which slot you are filling* (Needs attention / Response ready), a Radio to assign, and a separate `▶` icon button to audition. Optional polish in the same edit: `SoundPreset` gains a `desc` field and the tone signatures that already exist as trailing comments on `STOCK_PRESETS` ("C5 → E5", "A4 double tap") lift into it — strings unchanged; without it the 15 rows are hard to tell apart. Small and self-contained; does not need the rest of the design system to land first.
  **Outcome (2026-07-26):** shipped, but NOT as specced. Destin reviewed the first fix — Radio to assign, separate `▶` to audition — and reversed it: with one shared list behind a category toggle, assigning IS how you listen, so a second control was just another thing to aim at. Selecting a sound now plays it, and the whole tile is the hit target. `desc` shipped. The guard test inverted accordingly (`tests/sound-preview.test.ts` asserts selecting DOES preview). Also fixed in the same pass: the popup could not scroll at all, which turned out to be a `SettingsPopup` defect affecting Session Defaults too — the trigger for the whole D1 shell rework.
- [x] Settings "Backup & Sync" row freezes on launch-time warnings for the whole app run `bug` `#sync` `#ui` (added 2026-07-26, reported by Destin from his live app, **SHIPPED 2026-07-26 — youcoded PR #254, merge `6645db83`**)
  Symptom: the Settings row reads red **"Sync Failing"** with a `2` badge while the Backup & Sync popup two clicks away reads green **"All synced"** and shows no Warnings section — and `~/.claude/.sync-warnings.json` does not exist on disk. Root cause is a refresh gap, not a disagreement between the two derivations (both correctly route through `deriveSyncBoxState`/`deriveSettingsRowState`). `SyncSection` (`SyncPanel.tsx:272`) is mounted from **app launch**, not from settings-open — `DesktopSettings` renders unconditionally inside the always-mounted, translate-hidden drawer (`SettingsPanel.tsx:242,270`) — and it calls `getStatus()` exactly once, 350 ms in. Nothing ever refetches `status.warnings` after that: its `status:data` handler deliberately patches only `lastSyncEpoch`/`syncInProgress`/`backupMeta`/`lastSyncByDevice` (`SyncPanel.tsx:296-315`), and the only other caller — the popup's `refreshStatus` → `onRefresh` — auto-fires solely when the LEGACY `toolkit-state/.sync-marker` epoch advances, a file that does not exist on a spaces-only install, so `advanced` is false forever. Meanwhile `SyncService.runHealthCheck()` runs ~35 s after launch (the `rclone lsd` probe is slow — Z13 log: start `12:40:06`, `Auto-detected sync backend: drive` at `12:40:41`) and rewrites the warnings file, sweeping every stale health code. So the row reads the file as the PREVIOUS session left it and then keeps showing that snapshot for the rest of the app's uptime, while every fresh-reading surface (StatusBar pill off the 10 s `status:data` push, the popup off its own `getStatus()`) correctly shows nothing. The 07-25 22:06 launch logged no auto-detect line → `autoDetectBackend()` returned null → the phantom pair is almost certainly `PERSONAL_NOT_CONFIGURED` + `OFFLINE`, both danger, both health codes, both swept at 12:40:41 — i.e. the ROADMAP entry below (PR #248) is the SOURCE of the bogus warnings and this entry is why they outlive their own fix by a full app session. Fix: have `SyncSection` take warnings from the authoritative 10 s push (`statusData.syncWarnings`, already read straight off `.sync-warnings.json` by `buildStatusData`, `ipc-handlers.ts:1884`) instead of its frozen mount-time snapshot — App.tsx's gear danger-dot already does exactly this (`App.tsx:1823-1845`). Landing #248 alone does NOT close this: any transient warning (a real OFFLINE at launch) still pins the row red until restart.
- [ ] Native turn teardown leaves an unhandled promise rejection when a turn is aborted mid-stream `bug` `#native-runtime` (added 2026-07-25)
  Surfaced while rebasing Plan C, but **the defect is on master, not on that branch** — attribution was proven, not assumed: checking out master's `harness-session.ts` verbatim and adding a single `await Promise.resolve()` at the top of `turnLoop` reproduces it exactly, with no other change. Plan C's `await this.maybeCompact(...)` sits in that same position, so it shifts the microtask timing enough to make a latent race observable; `maybeCompact` itself is innocent (it returns `'none'` immediately for a small history, and a bare `await Promise.resolve()` in its place reproduces the rejection identically). Repro: `npx vitest run tests/harness-session.test.ts -t serialize` — the existing "send() while a turn is in flight rejects (callers must serialize)" test, which builds a never-closing `ReadableStream`, leaves turn 1 in flight, then calls `interrupt()`. Vitest reports `Errors 1` / `{ type: 'Unhandled Rejection', message: undefined, stacks: [] }` while all assertions still pass. **The rejection reason is literally `undefined`**, so there is no stack and no message to trace. Investigation ruled out: the SDK's terminal promises (swallowing `usage`/`finishReason`/`text`/`totalUsage`/`steps`/`response`/… in the abort branch does NOT fix it), any userland `new Promise` executor or `Promise.reject` call (a Promise-subclass wrapper that traces every `undefined` rejection never fires), and `async_hooks` promise-creation capture (the rejected promise isn't the one the init hook sees). That points at native stream/iterator machinery — most likely the cancellation path when `iterator.return()` tears down a reader that has a permanently-pending `read()`. Note the `'stall'` branch already swallows four promises with the comment *"Cancelling the reader can reject the terminal promises with nothing awaiting them"*, while the `'aborted'` branch swallows only two — that asymmetry is suspicious and worth revisiting even though widening it alone did not fix this instance. Low user impact (an `undefined` rejection carries no error detail to mask, and every assertion passes) but it dirties the suite's error count and could hide a *real* rejection later, so it should not ride along indefinitely.
- [x] "No sync configured" fires on a machine whose GitHub sync is healthy `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-24, **MERGED 2026-07-30 — youcoded PR #248, merge 0a5b15b9**)
  Found by Destin on a fresh macOS install running `1.3.0-beta.9`: red "Sync Failing" StatusBar chip + non-dismissible "No sync configured — your backups aren't set up", with GitHub sync set up, connected and green two clicks away. Root cause: `SyncService.runHealthCheck()` only reads the LEGACY additional-backup backends (`storage_backends` in `toolkit-state/config.json` — Drive/iCloud/rclone). Sync spaces keeps its own state at `toolkit-state/sync-spaces.json` and is invisible to that read, so zero legacy backends = "no sync" regardless of the primary system. **Clean installs are exactly the affected population, which is why this survived to beta.9:** `autoDetectBackend()` masks it on old machines — its `rclone lsd gdrive:Claude/Backup/` probe still exits 0 on a box that used the legacy backups years ago, silently skipping the warning without enabling any backend (verified on the Z13: `storage_backends` is `[]`, rclone exits 0, no warnings file on disk). New machines have no rclone and no `~/Library/…/Claude/Backup`, so they take the warning. Fix gates the warning on the primary system, rewords `PERSONAL_NOT_CONFIGURED` off "connect a cloud provider", and renames `PERSONAL_STALE` to name the Drive/iCloud markers it actually tracks. Health codes are re-cleared every launch, so affected installs self-heal on the next start. Flip to `[x]` on merge.
- [x] Artifact tracker writes RELATIVE paths into `absolutePath` (documented canonical-absolute) `bug` `#artifacts` (added 2026-07-23, **MERGED 2026-08-13 — youcoded master 3213c0af**)
  **Shipped.** Plan: `docs/archive/plans/2026-08-12-artifact-relative-path-misclassification.md`. Root cause confirmed as predicted below — the NATIVE harness `Write`/`Edit`/`Read` accept a relative `file_path` and resolve it with `path.resolve(ctx.cwd, p)` themselves, but the transcript event carries the RAW arg, and `resolveTrackedPath` had no branch for it. Four parts: classifier fix; an `isAbsoluteRecorded` guard at every site that builds a path from a record (**five, not the four the plan first counted** — `renameArtifact` was an unguarded `fs.rename`); a pure one-time repair that re-runs stored paths through the fixed classifier and merges collisions; and wiring gated on `reclassified === 0` with a `.pre-migration.bak` written first. Dry-run against a copy of the real 2,855-record sidecar: 48 reclassified, 13 merged, six invariants held, idempotent second pass. Full desktop suite 5,070 green; Android 171/171. **Known gap shipping with it: Android never gets the repair** — see the entry below.
  Found auditing the real `.youcoded/artifacts.json`: ≥7 external records (re-counted 2026-08-12: now **15**, still accumulating — new ones from the Aug work) have a non-absolute `absolutePath` — `docs/MAP.md`, `flappy-bird/styles.css`, `youcoded/desktop/src/renderer/state/chat-reducer.ts`, etc. The `ArtifactRecord.absolutePath` field is documented "canonical, set when kind='external'" (`shared/artifacts/types.ts`), and `visible-artifacts.ts`/`import-file` canonicalize it as absolute. A relative value only "resolves" by luck against the current cwd — `fs.access('docs/MAP.md')` passes when the app's cwd happens to be the workspace root, and fails everywhere else, so these records flip between present and orphaned by cwd. Something in the tracker (App.tsx artifact-tracker path, likely a Write/Edit tool-use whose path arrives project-relative) is storing the raw path instead of resolving it against the project root first. Latent regardless of the Files-tab work; surfaced by it. (found 2026-07-23)
- [ ] Android never receives the artifact-path repair, and its symptom is SILENT `bug` `#android` `#artifacts` (added 2026-08-13)
  Follow-up to the relative-`absolutePath` fix above, which is desktop-only. The plan originally justified that with "both platforms read the same synced sidecar" — **that is false, verified 2026-08-13**: `.youcoded/` is in `DEFAULT_IGNORES` (`sync-spaces/guards.ts:29`), which `git-transport.ts:333` writes into each hidden repo's `info/exclude`, and `project-manager.ts:67` also adds it to the project's own `.gitignore`. The sidecar is per-device and never synced by any transport in this tree. So an Android device keeps its damaged records indefinitely. Worse, the Android symptom is *quieter* than desktop's: `artifacts:check-existence` is a stub that always reports nothing missing (`SessionService.kt:3619-3622`), so a damaged record renders as an ordinary file and then opens blank — no "no longer on disk" label to explain it. The safety half already shipped (Task 3's guard makes `artifacts:get` return a clean orphan instead of reading whatever sits at the app's cwd); the repair half is absent. Needs either a Kotlin migration twin or an honest empty-state that says the record's stored location is invalid.
- [ ] Artifact records that escape the project root with `..` are refused, never repaired `bug` `#artifacts` (added 2026-08-13)
  Deliberate scope cut in the relative-path fix. `resolveP` is `path.resolve(cwd, p)`, so `../other/notes.md` really did write outside the project; those records keep a relative `absolutePath`, which now consistently orphans them at all five resolution sites rather than resolving against the process cwd. Consistent and safe, but they are permanently unopenable, and they are also the reason the `renameArtifact` guard is permanently load-bearing rather than transitional. Folding `..` segments against the root in pure string code would let them resolve to their real absolute location.
  **Verified 2026-08-22 (easy-knockout triage): still current, but NOT the easy fix it reads as — deliberately skipped from that batch.** The fold can't live in `resolveP` (harness tool guard — different layer, no artifact record in scope). A real repair either adds a `resolveRecordedPath` helper next to `isAbsoluteRecorded` in `write-authorization.ts` (all five refusal sites convert through it), or better, drops the `..` exclusion in `resolve-tracked-path.ts:97-101` so escapees classify external with a folded absolute path and `migrateRelativeExternals` repairs existing records. Either way it must update the tests that PIN the refusal (`migrate-relative-externals.test.ts:71-77` "leaves a .. escape external", `artifact-store.test.ts:499-530` rename guard, `write-authorization.test.ts`, `projects-index.test.ts`), navigate two sibling risks (silent over-reclassification — the entry two below; a fold landing outside any project root re-arms the exact escape `mustStayInRoot=false` externals path was guarded against), remember the `.youcoded/` sidecar never syncs (desktop-only repair leaves peers damaged), and Android has the guard half only (`ProjectManager.kt:177-180`) with NO Kotlin `resolveTrackedPath`/`migrateRelativeExternals` twin — so the repair needs a third implementation there (ties into the line-411 "Android never receives the artifact-path repair" entry). Design pass first.
- [ ] Nothing detects an artifact repair that silently over-reclassifies `chore` `#artifacts` (added 2026-08-13)
  On first launch after the relative-path fix ships, ~48 records on `youcoded-dev` become newly VISIBLE in Project View (externals are pin-gated by `visible-artifacts.ts:62`, internals are not). That is the intended fix, but if the classifier ever reclassified a shape the dry-run did not contain, the symptom is phantom in-project files, not an error — nothing would surface it but Destin noticing unfamiliar entries. `.youcoded/artifacts.json.pre-migration.bak` is the recovery path. Also unlogged: a repair that fails once is memoized for the whole process (it does now `console.error`, but nothing surfaces that to the user).
- [ ] Opening a session REPLAYS its whole transcript and re-records every file it ever touched `bug` `#artifacts` `#transcript-watcher` (added 2026-08-13)
  **This is almost certainly the real "unbounded sidecar growth" mechanism**, and it was found by accident while verifying the relative-path fix — Destin opened a 3-hour-old session and saw files in its drawer he had never touched in it. `TranscriptWatcher` registers a session with `offset: 0` (`transcript-watcher.ts:403`), so the first watch of a transcript in a given process reads it from byte 0 and re-emits every historical `tool_use` event as if new. The renderer's artifact tracker then calls `appendVersion` for each one, stamped with TODAY's date. Hard evidence from the live sidecar: launching a dev instance wrote **30 version events in a 0.2s window across 3 sessions** (21 `edit`, 7 `read`, 2 `create`), and `flappy-bird/play.html` now carries **two** `create` events — the real one and a replay duplicate. `read` is partly shielded (`App.tsx` dedupes the first read per doc per session) but `create`/`edit` have no dedup — `appendVersion` explicitly has none of its own. So every app launch that opens an old session inflates that session's history permanently. Fix direction: seek to EOF when first registering an already-existing transcript (a session's past events are already in the sidecar), or make `appendVersion` idempotent on (path, sessionId, type, source-event). Note the sidecar is per-device and unsynced, so this inflates independently on every machine.
- [x] Kotlin `detectOrphan` is dead code with test-only callers `chore` `#android` (added 2026-08-13) **SHIPPED 2026-08-22 — merged `17931fc0` (branch fix/roadmap-easy-knockouts; plan: `docs/archive/plans/2026-08-22-roadmap-easy-knockout-batch.md`)**
  Desktop twin already removed (`project-manager.ts:84-88`). `ProjectManager.kt:118-130` survives with only test callers — `rg detectOrphan app/src` to confirm before pruning.
- [ ] Remote: "+ Add file" uploads the file to the host, then the import fails `bug` `#remote` `#project-view` (added 2026-07-23)
  In remote mode `dialog.openFile()` maps to `pickAndUploadFiles()` (`remote-shim.ts:1013`) — it uploads the picked file to the desktop host and returns a host path. The renderer then calls `artifacts:import-file`, which `remote-server.ts` does NOT bridge (its only `artifacts:*` case is `list-projects-index`), so the import fails after the upload already happened. **Not a regression** — `includeExternal` was equally unbridged, so the button was already broken this way; but it now means a filesystem mutation rather than a sidecar pin, so it belongs with the existing "which namespaces are safe to expose over a password-auth'd but NOT TLS-encrypted channel" scoping decision under the remote-bridging entry. Either bridge it deliberately or hide the button in remote mode. (found 2026-07-23 auditing the Files-tab merge)
- [ ] `tests/ipc-handlers.test.ts` flakes at import time under full-suite runs `bug` `#tests` (added 2026-07-23)
  Fails intermittently inside `SkillConfigStore.migrate` → `skill-provider.ts:600` (was :597) → `main.ts:183` during `npm test`, and passes both in isolation and on a clean full re-run. Reproduced twice across one session on an untouched import chain. Looks like a tmp-dir/ordering race in the migration, not a real regression — but an intermittently-red suite trains people to re-run instead of read. (found 2026-07-23 during the Files-tab merge)
- [ ] Takeover holder can't detect lease loss while the SyncHub is down `bug` `#sync` `#leases` (added 2026-07-23)
  Found during the M2 dev repro (instrumented, confirmed from logs): lease *presence* has two transports (hub + the `~/YouCoded/Personal/Leases/` file fallback) but loss-detection lives only in the hub renew branch. A holder in file-fallback mode keeps optimistically renewing forever — after a force-takeover it never interrupts, never flushes, never shows the moved pill, and keeps rewriting the lease file (two installs ping-pong it). Pre-existing, provider-agnostic (CC + native). The misleading "isn't responding" dialog copy is being fixed on the M2 branch (PR #212); this entry is the deeper holder-side gap.

- [ ] Same-machine takeover handoff without the hub (lease-file signaling) `idea` `#sync` `#leases` (added 2026-07-23)
  Two installs sharing `~/YouCoded` (dev instance + built app — the sanctioned dogfood config) can see each other's lease files but can't deliver a takeover request when the hub is down (the request has exactly one transport: the SyncHub socket). A file-based request signal (holder watches its lease file's dir) would make same-machine handoff work hub-less. Nice-to-have; the hub path is primary.

- [x] Remote WS set-tag/set-note don't broadcast `SESSION_META_CHANGED` on success `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-23) **SHIPPED 2026-08-22 — merged `17931fc0` (branch fix/roadmap-easy-knockouts; plan: `docs/archive/plans/2026-08-22-roadmap-easy-knockout-batch.md`)**
  Found in M2 final review (youcoded PR #212): the desktop IPC surface's meta-write path broadcasts `SESSION_META_CHANGED` after a successful persist (the M2 broadcast-after-persist fix), but the remote WS `set-tag`/`set-note` handlers persist without the matching broadcast — a second remote client (or the same session viewed from another device) goes stale until its next full refresh. Pre-existing gap, not introduced by M2; M2 only fixed the desktop-IPC honesty half. **Partial adjacent work landed since (re-checked 2026-08-12): both handlers now call `emitConversationMetaChanged()` (the chatsearch signal) — but still no `remoteServer.broadcast({type: SESSION_META_CHANGED})`, which exists only on the IPC path (`ipc-handlers.ts:3067, 3089`).**

- [ ] `NativeSessionHost` needs a `quiescing` refuse-flag to close the one-macrotask takeover race `idea` `#native-runtime` `#sync` (added 2026-07-23)
  Found in M2 final review: `quiesce()` (cross-device takeover teardown) clears the send queue synchronously, then awaits one macrotask before aborting — deliberate, so a `send()` issued in the same tick can finish its deferred dispatch. But a `send()` that arrives DURING that macrotask window is not refused; it runs one full uninterrupted turn before the handoff proceeds. A `quiescing` flag set before the macrotask await (checked by `send()`, refusing honestly like the existing queue-full case) would close the window. Not a correctness bug — the flush still happens after — just a narrower race than the design intends.

- [x] `SESSION_CREATE` native-resume split-refusal branch has no direct test `bug` `#tests` `#native-runtime` (added 2026-07-23) **SHIPPED 2026-08-22 — merged `17931fc0` (branch fix/roadmap-easy-knockouts; plan: `docs/archive/plans/2026-08-22-roadmap-easy-knockout-batch.md`)**
  Found in M2 final review: the native resume path's split-refusal branch (the three distinct cwd-resolution refusal messages, see `docs/conversations.md` → "Native provider participation") has no IPC-harness test exercising it directly — it existed before M2 and M2 didn't add coverage. `session-meta-parity.test.ts` (M2, real-store IPC harness) now makes a direct test possible where none existed before; write one as a follow-up rather than relying on incidental coverage.

- [ ] `sync-spaces-engine.test.ts` "debounces file changes into one sync" — **recurring macOS CI failure. Possibly a real sync watcher bug, not a test defect** `bug` `#tests` `#ci` `#sync` (added 2026-07-22 as a flake; ESCALATED 2026-07-23; **DE-ESCALATED 2026-08-12 — "macOS CI is RED for every PR" is no longer true: only 22 of the last 60 Desktop CI master runs failed, the debounce case passed in the latest red run, and it last failed 2026-08-06 (run 31079675247). Still real, still recurring, still unexplained.** Separately: the macOS reds of 2026-08-10/11 — runs 31526973244, 31531850229, 31539413917 — are a DIFFERENT test, `sync-warning-self-clear.test.ts > skips the file write when nothing changed`, which deserves its own entry, added below.)
  Seen once on youcoded PR #213 (run 29983190137, macOS, commit `5850e281` — a branch that touches nothing in sync-spaces): `vi.waitFor(() => expect(t.pushes.length).toBe(1))` timed out with 0 pushes. Same macOS fs-event/timing flake family as the closed `subagent-watcher` and `sync-warnings-lifecycle` entries — and the SAME FILE as the now-CLOSED `[x]` "sync-spaces-engine macOS CI flake — fs.watch test still blows the 5s vitest timeout" entry further down (ROADMAP :326, PR #180/#181 history), which prescribed a per-test `testTimeout` — that fix shipped and closed the entry, but did NOT cure this. Treat these as one ledger, and note that entry called the failure mode exactly: *"trains people to re-run red macOS builds without reading them — which is how a real failure gets merged."* Passed on rerun (job 89132331505). **RECURRED 2026-07-23** on the same PR's final head `bebbe276` (job 89313264909, macOS, 60030ms timeout on the same `debounces file changes into one sync` case) — again on a branch touching nothing in sync-spaces, and again green on rerun. Per the flake rule (file once, fix on recurrence) this is now due. **RECURRED A THIRD TIME 2026-07-23** on youcoded PR #243 (job 89320861447, a COMMENT-ONLY diff), which is what finally pinned the diagnosis:

  **The prescribed timeout fix is already applied and is NOT the answer — do not re-apply it.** `sync-spaces-engine.test.ts` already carries both knobs (`WAIT_MS = 60_000` bounding the `vi.waitFor` poll, and `vi.setConfig({ testTimeout: 120_000 })` bounding the test, added in the #180/#181 round). The failing run polls the full 60s and observes **0 pushes** — not a slow push, no push at all. A budget that is already 60s/120s cannot be "too tight"; the watcher event is never delivered, so raising any timeout can never fix it.

  **ESCALATED 2026-07-23 — no longer intermittent.** On #243 it failed on the first run AND on the rerun (jobs 89320861447, 89323296359), both at the identical ~2m30s / 60s-poll signature, 293 test files passing and only this one failing. Every prior occurrence went green on rerun; this one does not. Treat macOS CI as effectively red for every PR until it is fixed — #243 was merged over it deliberately (comment-only diff, and the same failure already exists on master via `bebbe276`), but that is not a repeatable escape hatch.

  **PARTIALLY WALKED BACK 2026-07-25 — it went green on rerun again, so "no longer intermittent" is too strong.** Observed on `origin/master` at `95959cff` (Desktop CI run 30154294363): macOS failed the same `debounces file changes into one sync` case with `AssertionError: expected +0 to be 1`, Windows and Ubuntu green (303 passed / 1 failed) — then `gh run rerun --failed` came back **success** with no code change. It also failed on master at `60d56a67` on 2026-07-23 (run 30048860023), so it does reproduce on master, not just on PR branches. Two corrections to the escalation above: the failure is **not** deterministic (this rerun disproves "every rerun stays red"), and the fast-fail signature differs from the one the diagnosis was pinned on — this run asserted `+0 to be 1` quickly rather than polling the full 60s, so it is not obviously the same 0-pushes-after-60s shape. Everything else in this entry stands: it is real, it recurs on branches that touch nothing in sync-spaces, and the timeout knobs are not the answer. Treat the intermittency as evidence about the mechanism (a race that usually loses under load), not as a reason to downgrade it.

  **This may be a REAL macOS bug, not a test defect — do not "fix" it by weakening the test.** If the hypothesis below holds, files written immediately after a space is added are silently missed in production on macOS too, which is a data-loss-shaped bug in sync, not a CI annoyance. Confirm the production path before touching the test; a test tweak that makes this green while `addSpace` still resolves early would hide the real defect.

  Real hypothesis to chase: a **watcher-arming race**. The test calls `await engine.addSpace(space)` and then immediately `writeFileSync`s two files. On macOS, FSEvents registration is not synchronous with the `addSpace` promise resolving, so writes landing in that window are missed entirely and no debounce ever fires — which matches "0 pushes, forever." ~~Fix direction is to make `addSpace` resolve only once the watcher is genuinely armed~~ **THE PRESCRIPTION IS ALREADY THE CODE (checked 2026-08-12): `engine.ts:105-108` has awaited `watcher.once('ready')` since `c898c046` (2026-07-08) — i.e. since BEFORE this entry was filed. So chokidar's `ready` is evidently not the same as FSEvents being genuinely armed, and the next investigation needs a new hypothesis (e.g. a post-`ready` FSEvents settling window), NOT this one re-applied.** Cost so far: a re-run lap on three separate heads.

- [x] `sync-warning-self-clear.test.ts` "skips the file write when nothing changed" flakes — **NOT macOS-only** `bug` `#tests` `#ci` `#sync` (added 2026-08-12, found while re-verifying the debounce entry above; escalated 2026-08-13; **FIXED 2026-08-15 — youcoded PR #317, merge `c4fdc19c`**)
  **Root cause (2026-08-15), and it was a real production bug wearing a test flake's clothes:** `atomicWrite` in `sync-state.ts` named its temp file `<target>.tmp.<pid>` — the SAME name for every write in the process. Two overlapping writes to `.sync-warnings.json` both wrote that one temp path; the first `rename` moved it away and the second threw ENOENT. The overlap in the suite was the previous test's fake-timer-fired 60 s health check still doing real fs I/O after `stop()` (which only clears the interval); in the app the same collision is reachable when the 60 s health check overlaps a push-failure warning write. Fix: `<target>.tmp.<pid>.<seq>` (a per-call counter), and the leaking test now awaits its in-flight checks after `stop()`. 25/25 in a local loop; green on all three OSes in the PR run.
  Three consecutive macOS reds on master 2026-08-10/11 (Desktop CI runs 31526973244, 31531850229, 31539413917) are all this one case — `runHealthCheck — resolved warnings clear themselves > skips the file write when nothing changed` — with 404 other files passing. The test guards the PR #292 "rewrite only when the set actually changes" behavior.
  **2026-08-13 — reproduced on LINUX, locally, on unmodified master: 1 failure in 6 consecutive single-file runs.** That kills the "macOS-hostile clock/fs assumptions" hypothesis this entry previously steered toward — it is platform-independent and just rarer elsewhere. The failure is concrete, not a timeout: `Error: ENOENT: no such file or directory, rename '/tmp/sync-warning-self-clear-<pid>-<ts>/.claude/.sync-warnings.json.tmp.<pid>' -> '.../.sync-warnings.json'` thrown from `atomicWrite` (`src/main/sync-state.ts:226`) via `writeWarnings` (`:366`) ← `SyncService.runHealthCheck` (`sync-service.ts:1233`). So the tmp file is gone by the time `rename` runs, or its directory is — suspect the test's own cleanup racing a still-running periodic health check (PR #292 made `runHealthCheck` re-run every 60s and the suite drives timers), not the atomic-write helper. Next step: check whether the test awaits `stop()` before removing its temp home. Found incidentally during the dismiss-ends-turn full-suite run; that branch touches nothing in sync.

- [x] `session-meta-native-refusal.test.ts` flaked locally under parallel workers (`/tmp` race) `bug` `#tests` (added 2026-07-23, **MOOT — the file was deleted 2026-07-22 in youcoded `c3db2742`, one day before this entry was filed; its successor `session-meta-parity.test.ts` already uses per-run `mkdtempSync` dirs, the prescribed fix. Verified 2026-08-12.**)
  Seen once during git-surface work (Linux, full-suite parallel run): failed on first run, clean on rerun and standalone — implementer diagnosed a pre-existing `/tmp` path race between vitest workers. One strike; if it recurs, give the test a worker-unique temp dir (`mkdtemp`) like the git fixtures use.

- [x] `UnsavedChangesDialog` Escape also fires the app ESC cascade underneath `bug` `#renderer` (added 2026-07-22, **FIXED 2026-08-12 — youcoded PR #298, merge `c7899ffe`**: raw listener replaced with `useEscClose(true, onCancel)`; test proves one Esc closes only the dialog, second Esc pops the layer beneath, and `defaultPrevented` still suppresses the PTY passthrough. Review confirmed the old double-fire was order-INdependent (stopPropagation can't block same-target capture listeners) and the fix also made Android hardware-back dismiss the dialog, which the raw listener never did.)
  Found while fixing the identical bug in the git-surface discard dialog (branch `feat/git-surface`, commit `b2301eb8`): the dialog registered its own raw capture-phase `keydown` listener and called `stopPropagation()`, but `EscCloseProvider` (`use-esc-close.tsx`) ALSO listens capture-phase on the same `window` target — and same-target listeners are immune to `stopPropagation()` (only `stopImmediatePropagation()` stops them). One Escape press therefore cancelled the dialog AND popped the drawer's ESC stack beneath it.
- [x] `UnsavedChangesDialog` still hand-rolls its scrim instead of the Scrim/OverlayPanel layer system `bug` `#renderer` `#ui-consistency` (added 2026-08-12, found by the PR #298 review; **FIXED same day — youcoded PR #305, merge `d686530e`**: migrated to `<Scrim layer={3}>` + `<OverlayPanel layer={3}>` mirroring DiscardConfirmDialog (deliberately no `destructive` ring — headline action is Save), AND the overlay-layer-authority guard was widened with a repo-wide red-first `bg-black/\d+` scan so the class can't recur — it found zero other offenders. **Destin: eyeball the dialog once** — backdrop went from cold black to theme-tinted `--scrim-heavy`, panel gained the theme rounding/shadow. Review parity-verified click-outside, Esc stack, and layer ordering; noted non-blocking: no focus trap (pre-existing), and the dialog is host-local stacking like its sibling.)

- [x] Git surface: conflicted (unmerged) files read as clean `bug` `#git` `#renderer` (added 2026-07-22, **FIXED 2026-08-12 — youcoded PR #304, merge `87dd28b1`**: `u ` lines parsed into a `'conflicted'` kind (fixture captured from a real merge-conflict repo — the review independently reproduced its blob hashes), amber "Conflict" word in the footer + badge on the review card, `staged:false` matching git's own commit refusal. The review proved discard-on-unmerged is safe (was previously a silent no-op LIE — `{ok:true}` for a file it never touched), and a follow-up hardening disables "Include in commit" on conflicted files so a non-technical user can't stage conflict markers. **Destin: eyeball the amber Conflict badge/footer word.** Residuals filed as their own entries: core.quotePath paths, TerminalRightSlot keying, rewrite staleness.)
  `parsePorcelainV2` skipped `u XY …` lines entirely, so a mid-merge conflicted file showed no footer counts and no review card.

- [x] Git surface: "Show more" pagination vs `git:changed` refresh can drop/duplicate log entries `bug` `#git` (added 2026-07-22, **FIXED 2026-08-12 — youcoded PR #304, merge `87dd28b1`. Fixed by dedupe-by-sha, NOT the clear-on-refresh this entry sketched**: refresh rides the view's own stage/unstage `git:changed` events, so clearing would collapse the user's opened history on every "Include in commit" tick; dedupe (page-one wins, `--skip` from the deduped count) kills both the duplicate-React-key and skip-gap failure modes. Known bounded residual on history REWRITE mid-pagination — own entry above.)

- [x] Git surface: stale discard-error banner after close+reopen during an in-flight discard `bug` `#git` (added 2026-07-22, **FIXED 2026-08-12 — youcoded PR #304, merge `87dd28b1`**: `runGuardedDiscard` with a monotonic token bumped in `closeGitReview`; superseded results dropped, error passthrough unchanged. Chat view is fully safe (per-session keyed drawers); the terminal-view unkeyed-drawer cousin is its own entry above.)

- [ ] Android `layoutInsets` flow is emitted into but never collected — REPORTED, UNCONFIRMED `bug` `#android` `#renderer` (added 2026-07-22)
  **Status: reported but NOT confirmed** — found by grep during the 2026-07-22 dead-code sweep, never observed at runtime, and deliberately left in place rather than deleted. Treat the impact claim as unverified until someone checks it on a device.
  What was seen: `SessionService.kt` declares `_layoutInsets`/`layoutInsets` (+ the `LayoutInsets` data class) and the `ui:action`/`layout-update` handler emits into it on every layout report from React, but **`rg layoutInsets` finds no collector anywhere in `app/`** — only the declaration, the emit, and a comment. `ChatScreen.kt:14` explicitly names "the layoutInsets / screenMode plumbing they fed" as part of what Tier 2 removed along with the native Termux `TerminalView` Compose block, which is consistent with the consumer having been deleted and the producer left behind. Same vestigial shape as the `viewModeRequest` flow, which WAS removed in youcoded#207 (`82552cee`) after confirming React owns chat↔terminal switching entirely.
  Why it wasn't just deleted with its twin: it is ~5 lines, harmless while nothing reads it (a `MutableSharedFlow(replay = 1)` that nobody collects), and **planned Android soft-keyboard/inset work may want exactly this plumbing** as a starting point — deleting it would be the wrong call if that lands soon. So the open question is a decision, not a diagnosis: **delete it as dead, or wire it up as the foundation for keyboard-inset handling.** If the answer is delete, it's a 5-line removal plus dropping the now-empty `layout-update` branch — but note the `"ui:action"` case itself must survive, because falling through to the unknown-message branch logs a warning and returns an error to the WebView on every layout report.

- [ ] `artifacts:save` bypasses write-guard in both directions `bug` `#native-runtime` `#artifacts` `#hooks` (added 2026-07-20)
  `hook-scripts/write-guard.sh` is registered as a Claude Code `PreToolUse` hook on `Write|Edit` (`scripts/install-hooks.js:200`). It is a same-machine concurrency lock, not a permission guard: it reads `~/.claude/.write-registry.json` and blocks (exit 2) only when a *different, still-alive* Claude PID last wrote the file. Its whitelist explicitly covers `CLAUDE.md`, `settings.json`, and `mcp.json` (`write-guard.sh:31-33`). But **`artifacts:save` calls `fs.promises.writeFile` directly in the Electron main process** (`ipc-handlers.ts:3113-3114`, `:3140-3141`) — no hook dispatch, no registry read, **and no registry write**. So the break is symmetric: a user editing in the artifact pane is not subject to the guard, *and* the agent's next write to that file sees no ownership conflict because nothing recorded the pane's write. ~~Today this is nearly harmless because the pane can only edit `md`/`markdown`/`txt` (`ActiveArtifactView.tsx:48`)~~ **THE MITIGATION IS GONE (re-checked 2026-08-12): the D4 code-editor work lifted the allowlist — `ActiveArtifactView.tsx:105` now says "ANY text file is editable — the old md/markdown/txt allowlist is gone." The condition this entry called "the moment it becomes live" has already happened; re-triage upward.** (Path corrections: the hook installer is `desktop/scripts/install-hooks.js:190/205`, and the raw write is now at `ipc-handlers.ts:3716-3717`.) **Sequencing caution:** per CLAUDE.md, until the youcoded-core deprecation release ships, write-guard fixes must land in BOTH the youcoded-core copy and the app's bundled copies — so coupling the artifact save path to registry participation mid-deprecation is the thing to avoid; wait for the deprecation to complete, or scope the fix to the app's copy only and note it. (found 2026-07-20 in the Tier 1 code-editor consequence review — spec §12.2)

- [ ] Artifact sidecar `versions[]` grows unbounded, and each save rewrites the whole file `bug` `#artifacts` `#perf` (added 2026-07-20)
  `appendVersion` (`artifact-store.ts:58`) pushes to `versions[]` (`:108`) with **no cap, no eviction, no pruning, and no comment acknowledging it** — unlike `permission-store.ts:25-26`, which at least documents its unbounded growth as intentional and names the UI that will prune it. Size alone is minor (~250–300 bytes per event once `JSON.stringify(next, null, 2)` pretty-prints it at `artifact-store.ts:37`; 500 edits of one file ≈ 125–150KB). **The write pattern is the real cost:** `appendVersion` does a full read-parse-mutate-serialize-CAS-write of the *entire* sidecar on every save, with up to `MAX_RETRIES = 5` attempts (`artifact-store.ts:46`) — so cost per save is O(total sidecar size) across all artifacts in the project, not O(this file). Mitigating asymmetry today: the *discovered*-file save branch deliberately skips the sidecar entirely (`ipc-handlers.ts:3131-3133`, so editing a stray doc never silently creates a `.youcoded/` dir), so growth only hits already-tracked files. The Tier 1 code-editor workstream makes this materially worse on two axes at once — more tracked records AND more events per record — but capping/compacting `versions[]` changes the sidecar's on-disk contract and needs its own migration story, so it is filed here rather than scheduled into that PR. Decide whether old events are dropped, coalesced by session, or moved to a rotated file. (found 2026-07-20 in the Tier 1 code-editor consequence review — spec §12.8)

- [x] Halftone Dimension's bubble `custom_css` targets selectors that don't exist `bug` `#themes` (added 2026-07-20; FIXED 2026-07-22 — wecoded-themes #20 (merged) fixes the pack, youcoded #197 (merged `694fc94b`) adds `lintCustomCssSelectors()` so an inert selector warns instead of shipping silently. Confirmed halftone is the ONLY offender: the other six packs use no bubble `custom_css` at all. The `:nth-child(odd)` cyan alternation was DROPPED, not renamed — `.assistant-bubble` is the only child of its flex wrapper, so `:nth-child(odd)` always matches and a straight rename would have painted every assistant bubble cyan)
  `wecoded-themes/themes/halftone-dimension/manifest.json`'s `custom_css` styles `.chat-bubble.user` and `.chat-bubble.assistant` — the live renderer uses **`.user-bubble`** (`UserMessage.tsx:72`) and **`.assistant-bubble`** (`AssistantTurnBubble.tsx:362`). So the pack's user-bubble glow, its 1.5px accent border, and the assistant border-left (incl. the `:nth-child(odd)` cyan alternation) are **all dead in the shipping app** — the theme renders without the bubble treatment its previews imply. Fix is a one-line selector rename in the manifest, but check the other six community themes for the same mistake before shipping it, and confirm against the bubble-style variants at `globals.css:1051,1060,1066` which already hook the real class names. Note the pack also has an internal inconsistency worth fixing in the same pass: token `accent` is `#E51F48` while every `custom_css` glow hardcodes `#e8234a`. (found 2026-07-20 while building the session-switch animation mockup, which reproduced the pack faithfully and surfaced that the rules never applied)

- [ ] Sync dead-ends on any machine without `gh` — the setup panel must not require a terminal `bug` `#sync` `#onboarding` (added 2026-07-20)
  Enabling sync on a stock macOS install fails with no usable signal: `gh` is absent (Xcode CLT ships `git`, not `gh`), `provisionGithubRemote` throws ENOENT, and the user sees "Setting up…" then a green "All synced" over an empty space (the two `#sync` bugs below). Destin hit this on the beta.8 VM 2026-07-20 and only got past it by hand-installing the release tarball and running `gh auth login` in Terminal — **exactly the terminal step the product exists to avoid.** The same trap awaits stock Windows and most Linux installs. **The auth half is already solved and should not be rebuilt:** `github-auth.ts` runs GitHub's OAuth *device flow* in-app (its header: "Non-developers can't be expected to open a terminal and run `gh auth login`"), mints a token, and pipes it to `gh auth login --with-token` — so the app already holds a valid token and merely needs `gh` to *exist* to store it. Two fix shapes; the second is the one worth doing:
  **(A) Stopgap — teach `prerequisite-installer.ts` about `gh`.** That module already does user-local, sudo-free installs with PATH registration + absolute-path fallback (built for Claude Code); `gh` ships prebuilt tarballs for darwin x64/arm64, linux and win32, so this is a new entry in an existing pattern, not new machinery. Fastest path to "the panel does it for you", and it's the minimum Destin asked for.
  **(B) Real fix — drop the `gh` dependency entirely.** `gh` is used for exactly two things: `repo create`/`repo view` (`space-manager.ts:40,46`), and acting as the git credential store for push/pull (`git-transport.ts` has NO credential logic — it relies on the helper `gh auth login` configures). The app already has the token, so both collapse into things it can do itself: repo create/view via the REST API using the same `fetch` the device flow already uses, and push/pull auth via `GIT_ASKPASS` or an inline `credential.helper` in the per-call env the transport already builds alongside `GIT_DIR`/`GIT_WORK_TREE`. That deletes a ~13 MB third-party binary, an install step on every platform, and the whole "gh not installed / not authed" failure class. **Constraint that must survive: token hygiene** (`github-auth.ts` — token never logged, never in an error string, never over the remote socket, with a test enforcing it). An askpass/credential-helper env var keeps it in process memory; **do NOT embed it in the remote URL**, which would write the token into `.git/config` on disk. Sequencing: (A) is about a day, (B) is a design pass — but shipping (A) alone leaves every user carrying a CLI they never asked for, so treat (A) as a release unblocker and (B) as the plan. (found 2026-07-20 dogfooding beta.8 on the macOS VM) **Update 2026-07-22:** investigated + planned — (A) already shipped on master (`f13dea52`, postdates beta.8); (B) approved and expanded into a shared github-client. Plan: docs/archive/plans/2026-07-22-sync-setup-overhaul.md (Phase 2). **Update 2026-07-22 (later): (B) SHIPPED — youcoded PR #201, merge `998d6fb0`.** github-client (token custody in safeStorage/userData, REST repo provisioning, per-invocation inline credential.helper — token env-only, coded `github-auth` errors → Reconnect CTA, combined github:status, gh now fully optional). Remaining before flipping this [x]: the fresh-VM verification through the FIRST REAL PUSH with no gh installed (needs Destin's macOS VM + a build ≥ `647bd242`) — it now exercises the whole gh-free path, and the old `gh auth setup-git` question is moot on that path (credentials ride the transport, not git's global helper). **Update 2026-07-22 (Phase 3 SHIPPED — youcoded #202 `95895a6b` + #203 `647bd242`):** every remaining gh consumer converted (marketplace/theme publishing via shared `github-fork-publish`, bug reports, PR lookups — all work gh-free now, plus the skill publisher's Windows ~32 KB argv bug died with the conversion); Connected accounts page inside the Account popup with `github:disconnect` (first-ever GitHub sign-out surface); `repo`-scope disclosure in the connect modal; "Sign in to YouCoded" naming contract across all sign-in surfaces; Backup & Sync settings row now derives from BOTH sync systems (`deriveSettingsRowState` — was legacy-backends-only, read "Not configured" over live green sync). The whole overhaul is code-complete; ONLY the VM pass gates this entry.

- [x] Remote connect crashed the whole screen on a phone — "undefined is not a function (near '…theme of themeEntries…')" `bug` `#remote` (added 2026-07-20, fixed 2026-07-20 — youcoded merge `492ff880`)
  Regression from `f17d00cb`. That merge made unbridged channels answer `{ok:false,unsupported:true}` instead of dropping; the shim then RESOLVED with that payload ("so nothing crashes"), which was backwards — callers expect the channel's real return *shape*. `marketplace-context.tsx:172` does `theme.marketplace.list().catch(() => [])`; the `.catch` never fired, the object survived `themes || []`, and `for (const theme of themeEntries)` threw, blanking the screen. Fix: shim REJECTS unsupported channels (restores the contract every `.catch(() => [])` was written against — they used to reject on the 30 s timeout; now they reject in ms), plus `Array.isArray` guards on all five list setters in marketplace-context. The prior test literally asserted "still resolves … so the caller does not crash" — it encoded the bug; replaced. Found because Destin pushed back on a wrong "production-bundle" hypothesis, and switching the dev server back to Vite un-minified `themeEntries`.

- [x] Remote logging could crash the main process (EPIPE) + warn-spam on unbridged channels `bug` `#remote` (added 2026-07-20, fixed 2026-07-20 — youcoded merge `9c31808e`)
  A closed stdout/stderr throws EPIPE; with no `error` listener that's an uncaught exception that kills the main process — the app died over a log line ("A JavaScript error occurred in the main process / write EPIPE"). Guarded in `main.ts` at import time (reproduced through the exact `2>&1 | head` pipeline: unguarded exits 1, guarded exits 0). Second bug compounding it: the `default:` case warned on EVERY unhandled request, and `useAttentionClassifier` polls the unbridged `terminal:get-screen-text` once a second → a log line per second. Now deduped per channel. Both pinned in `tests/remote-server.test.ts`. (Surfaced by a crash that only happened because a dev launch was mistakenly piped into `head` — the EPIPE path was latent in production regardless.)

- [ ] Remote first-connect is slow — the real bottleneck is NOT yet fixed `bug` `#remote` `#perf` (added 2026-07-20)
  A first connect sits on a white screen for seconds. Investigated 2026-07-20. **Tier 1 shipped (youcoded merge `0cbb72ba`) but did NOT move the needle on LAN** — Destin confirmed on his phone that first-paint feels unchanged. Honest correction: the Tier 1 analysis optimized *bytes* without ever measuring *time*. Over LAN the entire critical path (HTML + CSS + 552 kB compressed entry chunk) lands in **~120 ms** — measured via CDP against the dev server. Compression is real (2.05 MB → 494 kB, 4.2×) and still worth having on **cellular / Tailscale-over-WAN**, but it was never the LAN bottleneck. The seconds of white are spent AFTER the bytes arrive.
  **What Tier 1 actually shipped (keep):** brotli/gzip with `Vary`, `immutable` on content-hashed `assets/` (`no-cache` on `index.html`, else clients strand on a stale build), brotli **q5 not q11** (measured 38 ms vs 4,719 ms on the entry chunk — q11 would have stalled the first request ~5 s), compressed bytes cached per (asset, encoding), plus an inline boot skeleton. Pinned by `tests/remote-static-policy.test.ts`. Note the skeleton paints dark+spinner on cold cache (verified via CDP screenshots) but is only on screen for tens of ms once bytes are local — so it does little on LAN.
  **The likely real bottleneck (unproven — next session should MEASURE on the phone, not emulate):** parsing/executing the **1,969 kB single-chunk bundle on phone-class CPU**, plus the WebSocket connect+auth gate that blocks `App` from mounting (`index.tsx` Root: no `App` until `connected || hasConnectedOnce`). Emulating a 6× CPU throttle in headless Chrome did NOT reproduce the sustained white (React still mounted <100 ms there), which means the emulation is not representative — get a real trace off the device (Safari Web Inspector timeline, or `chrome://inspect` if Android) before spending more effort. **Do not repeat the Tier-1 mistake of optimizing a number without confirming it maps to wall-clock the user feels.**
  **Tier 2 — ~2.5 s of scripted dead time (independent of the above; pay it even on a warm connect).** `remote-server.ts` `replayBuffers()` awaits `requestSnapshot()` (2000 ms internal timeout) then a hardcoded `setTimeout(…, 500)`; comment at `:610-612` concedes the worst case. Snapshot timeout costs the 2 s AND returns `degraded: true`. Fix: client ACK `chat:hydrate` instead of guessing; stop serializing snapshot-then-PTY. Also `:624-627` replays hook events **one WS frame per event**, up to 10,000/session — batch them. NOTE: this delay is post-auth, so it lengthens time-to-first-CHAT, not necessarily time-to-first-PAINT — measure which one Destin is actually seeing before assuming.
  **Tier 3 — payload + bundle.** `serializeChatState` (`chat-types.ts:545`) is **unbounded**: full `timeline`, every `toolCall`, all `seenUuids` for every session in one frame. PTY buffers 4 MB/session. Bundle: no `manualChunks` (`PdfView`/`DocxView`/`XlsxView` already lazy, but `SettingsPanel`/`MarketplaceScreen`/`GameLobby` are not — splitting these is the most direct lever on parse time IF that's the bottleneck); `MarketplaceProvider` mounts at app root firing 7 IPC calls; `theme-context.tsx:246-255` awaits `theme:read-file` in a **serial `for` loop**; three `fetch`es in `skill-provider.ts` (`:713, :729, :751`) have no timeout; `skills:list` called three times on mount.
  Ruled out: the `isFirstRun === null` blank gate (`App.tsx:2474`) — `remote-shim.ts:1288` stubs `firstRun.getState()` to resolve immediately. (found 2026-07-20 investigating slow first connect)

- [ ] Concurrent sidecar *creation* can still clobber a record — CAS is off on the create path `bug` `#artifacts` (added 2026-07-22)
  Sibling to the same-millisecond ABA lost-update fixed in youcoded PR #198. That PR made every sidecar *update* strictly advance its CAS token, which closes the update race on both platforms. **Creation is still unguarded:** `casWrite` skips the CAS check entirely when `expectedUpdatedAt === null` (`cas-write.ts` — the `if (extractUpdatedAt)` branch), so two `appendVersion` calls that both find no sidecar each write a fresh page-one and the second silently overwrites the first. Both report `committed: true`; one artifact record is lost with no error. Window is narrow — it needs the FIRST-EVER two artifact writes in a project to land concurrently, since after that the file exists and the shipped fix covers it — but the failure is invisible and permanent.
  **Why it was not fixed in #198:** the obvious fix (require the file to NOT exist when expected is null, i.e. O_EXCL semantics) would break corruption recovery, which *deliberately* passes null to overwrite a damaged sidecar (`artifact-store.ts` `appendVersion`, the `'corrupted' in current` branch). A correct fix has to distinguish "creating fresh" from "deliberately replacing something broken" — e.g. a third expected-state value, or an explicit `overwriteCorrupt` flag — and needs its own tests. Mirror in `app/src/main/kotlin/com/youcoded/app/artifacts/ArtifactStore.kt`, which shares the CAS model. (found 2026-07-22 while fixing the update-path race)

- [x] `artifact-store.test.ts` "retries on CAS conflict up to MAX_RETRIES" is flaky `bug` `#tests` (added 2026-07-20; FIXED 2026-07-22, youcoded PR #198 `79ac5802` — **it was NOT flake: a real data-loss bug in the CAS retry path**, fixed in `artifact-store.ts` (+43) and mirrored in Android `ArtifactStore.kt`. The suspicion recorded here was right — an intermittent failure in a compare-and-swap path was losing one of three concurrent appends, not merely failing to retry. Independently re-confirmed during the 2026-07-22 theme-layer consequence sweep, where it reproduced 5/5 locally on clean master while CI stayed green — i.e. environment-dependent, which is why the suite had tolerated it)
  Fails roughly 1 in 3 full-suite runs with `expected length 3 but got 2` at `tests/artifacts/artifact-store.test.ts:116` — the third CAS retry doesn't land. **Confirmed pre-existing**, not caused by the Tier 1 remote-perf merge: reproduced 3/8 runs on `4cc745ec`, which predates it. A flaky test in the suite is corrosive — it trains sessions to re-run until green, which is exactly how a real regression gets waved through. Likely a timing/mtime-resolution assumption in the retry loop rather than a genuine race in `appendVersion`. (found 2026-07-20 verifying the Tier 1 merge)
  **Mechanism (PR #198):** `updatedAt` is the CAS comparand and has millisecond resolution, so two writers inside the SAME millisecond left the on-disk token byte-identical to the value the other writer had already read — its CAS then passed on a value that had changed underneath it (classic ABA) and its stale copy clobbered the first record. Both callers got `committed: true` and one artifact silently vanished. Measured **149/300** same-millisecond pairs. That is why the failing assertion is `toHaveLength(3)` — a LOST RECORD, not the "third CAS retry doesn't land" this entry originally guessed — and why it fails MORE on a fast idle machine and LESS under load, the opposite of the fixed-budget family it was filed alongside. Fix: `writeSidecar` now advances `updatedAt` past the token the writer read, so every commit strictly increases it and a stale reader can never match; placed there so all five sidecar writers inherit it, and mirrored in `app/.../ArtifactStore.kt`, which shares the CAS model and writes the same file. Pinned by a 60-iteration concurrency loop + a direct strictly-advances assertion (both verified red with the fix reverted). Creation-path sibling still open, see the `#artifacts` entry above.

- [ ] Most `window.claude` channels are not bridged to remote access (~100 of them) `bug` `#remote` (added 2026-07-20)
  `remote-server.ts`'s message `switch` had **no `default:` case**, so any channel it doesn't implement was silently dropped — and `remote-shim.ts`'s `invoke()` registers a pending promise with a 30s timer, so an unimplemented channel presented as a **30-second hang then a rejection naming nothing**. Both discovered symptoms looked like unrelated feature bugs: remote Project View was permanently empty (no `artifacts:*` cases at all, and `ProjectView`'s `listProjectsIndex().then()` has no `.catch()`), and the game lobby showed *signed out* while the host app was signed in (no `account:*` cases; `account-context` calls `reloadFromStore()` as `void`, so `signedIn` stayed at its `useState(false)` default). Note the identity there is the **marketplace account**, not `gh auth` as some comments imply.
  **Partly fixed 2026-07-20** (youcoded merge `f17d00cb`): added `artifacts:list-projects-index`, `account:signed-in`, `account:user`, and a `default:` case that responds immediately with `{ok:false, unsupported:true}` naming the channel. The shim maps the channel to a plain-language feature name and `RemoteUnsupportedNotice` surfaces *"X isn't available via remote access yet"* — deduped by **feature, not channel**, which is load-bearing because `useAttentionClassifier` polls an unbridged channel every second and a notice per response would pin a toast on screen permanently. Pinned by `tests/remote-shim-unsupported.test.ts` + `tests/remote-server.test.ts`.
  **Still unbridged** (now failing fast and visibly instead of hanging): all of `social:*` (16 — friends, presence, challenges, so remote games sign in but the lobby stays empty), the rest of `artifacts:*` and `project:*` (so Project View lists projects but its tabs are thin), `theme:*`, `theme-marketplace:*`, `marketplace:*`, `integrations:*`, `dialog:*`, `dev:*`, plus `terminal:get-screen-text` (polled every second by the attention classifier), `commands:list`, `performance:*`, `analytics:*`, `app:restart`, `platform:get`, `session:set-flag`, `update:changelog`. (`appearance:*` came OFF this list — re-checked 2026-08-12, `appearance:get`/`appearance:set` are now bridged.) **Concrete consequence, measured 2026-08-25:** because neither `artifacts:get` NOR `artifacts:read-binary` is bridged, the artifact pane cannot open **any** file over remote access on a desktop host — not a big image, not a 4 KB markdown note. Both fall to the `default:` case. This makes every size- or preview-related refinement for remote unreachable until the bridge exists (that is why the ask-before-a-large-download design in `docs/archive/specs/2026-08-25-artifact-pane-size-limits-design.md` §4.6 was cancelled rather than built). (The stale `remote-shim.ts` comment claiming binary viewers work for remote browsers was corrected in youcoded `73e2defe` — it now names this gap instead.)
  **This is deliberately NOT a mechanical "add the missing cases" task.** Bridging the *mutating* ones — `artifacts:delete-project`, `account:delete`, `account:sign-out`, `dialog:open-folder`, `marketplace:install` — exposes host-side filesystem and account mutation over a channel that is password-authenticated but **not TLS-encrypted** (see `desktop/CLAUDE.md` → Remote Access). Needs a scoping decision on which namespaces are safe to expose and whether any should be read-only, not a drive-by. Also outstanding: 6 methods present in `preload.ts` but missing from the shim entirely (`remote.installTailscale`, `remote.authTailscale`, `detach.detachLive`, `detach.dragWindowMove`, `buddy.captureDesktop`, `buddy.onAttachFile`) — those fail as `undefined` TypeErrors at the call site, not as timeouts, and `buddy.onAttachFile` is a subscribe helper so callers crash on mount. (found 2026-07-20 debugging empty Project View + signed-out games on a phone)

- [x] Enabling remote access in Settings does nothing until the app restarts `bug` `#remote` (added 2026-07-20, fixed 2026-07-20 — youcoded merge `756ee273`)
  `REMOTE_SET_CONFIG` (`ipc-handlers.ts:1311-1320`) sets `remoteConfig.enabled = true` and saves, but never starts the server — `remoteServer.start()` runs exactly once at boot (`main.ts:1325`), when `enabled` was still false. So a user flips the toggle, the UI shows it on, and nothing is listening; the browser gets ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED with no hint that a restart is required. Affects the built app, not just dev. Fix is either starting/stopping the server from the handler, or telling the user a restart is needed — the current silent no-op is the worst of both. (found 2026-07-20 while trying to reach the remote-hydration runtime checkpoint)
  **Fixed 2026-07-20** (`756ee273`): the handler starts on enable and stops on disable. Making `start()` runtime-callable exposed four states unreachable when it only ran at boot, all fixed in the same change — non-idempotent re-subscription of the SessionManager/HookRelay listeners, `listen()` errors with no handler at all (the promise never settled; the new test *times out* at 5003ms against the old code rather than failing), `loadTokens()` being constructor-only while `stop()` clears the map, and an uncancelled upload-cleanup interval. Failures roll `enabled` back, persist it, and surface the real OS error under the toggle. Open follow-up: `stop()` still clears in-memory tokens, so toggling off de-pairs every device rather than pausing — pre-existing, preserved deliberately, cheap to change if that reads wrong.

- [ ] `isTouchDevice()` returns false on remote browsers, including phones `bug` `#remote` (added 2026-07-20)
  `remote-shim.ts:389` sets `__PLATFORM__` from the host's `auth:ok` payload, and `remote-server.ts:439/486` send `platform: 'desktop'`. So a phone browser on remote access reports `'desktop'` — a value not even in the `Platform` union (`platform.ts:1`) — and `isTouchDevice()` (`'android' || 'browser'`) is false. Three call sites depend on it, all in `TerminalView.tsx` (94, 413, 416), including `useRawBytes`, which selects the terminal byte path: a phone on remote access is running the desktop path with touch adaptations off. Root cause is the shim overwriting a **device** fact with a **host** fact; the `preservePlatform` flag already exists for the Android-connecting-to-desktop case, so the shape of the fix is known. The same trap bit the idle-blur fix (merge `2f8132cf`), which had to feature-detect `(pointer: coarse)` rather than trust the platform string. (found 2026-07-20 fixing soft-keyboard behavior on Chrome/Android)

- [ ] Verify the mobile keyboard fix on the packaged Android app `task` `#android` (added 2026-07-20)
  Merge `2f8132cf` changed the viewport meta from `interactive-widget=overlays-content` to `resizes-visual` (`index.html`) and reworked the chrome-glass cutout to follow `--vvp-offset` via `--bottom-chrome-total` (`globals.css`). Verified on Chrome for Android over remote access only. The packaged app's WebView reads both, and the working theory for why it was previously unaffected — that WebView ignores `interactive-widget` and resizes the visual viewport regardless — is reasoning, not a measurement. If that theory is wrong, this traded a browser bug for an app regression. Needs a debug APK with a keyboard open/close check in chat view. Deliberately deferred at Destin's call on 2026-07-20.

- [ ] Finish the remote-hydration work — ordering + view-state parity `task` `#remote` (added 2026-07-20)
  Merge `2f8132cf` shipped only the first of three planned commits (collision-proof message ids + empty-snapshot rejection). Still open, specced in `docs/active/plans/2026-07-20-remote-hydration-pr-spec.md`: (2) sequence hydration ahead of live events by buffering push events in the shim between `auth:ok` and `chat:hydrate`, with a bounded timeout, and delete the 500ms wall-clock guess in `replayBuffers` — note this must NOT gate first paint, because Android's `LocalBridgeServer` never sends `chat:hydrate` and would hang forever; (3) carry `activeSessionId` + `viewModes` in the snapshot and drop the Android-only condition on the `switch-view` broadcast (`App.tsx:2248-2258`), which is what actually makes remote match desktop. Destin reported connect "significantly better" after (1) alone, so (2) may be smaller than specced — the open question is whether remote still lands on a different session/view than the desktop window, which decides whether (2) or (3) matters more.

- [x] Sync reports green "All synced" on a device that has never synced once `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-20, fixed 2026-07-22 — youcoded merge `efab7e7d`)
  `SyncPanel.tsx:894` derives status as a fallback ladder — `enabling → !enabled → errorMsg → spacesSyncing → 'synced'`. The green `'synced'` state is the bare `else`: it asserts only "enabled, nothing erroring right now, nothing in flight", never that a push or pull has **ever** succeeded. A device that has never contacted the remote lands on green. Observed 2026-07-20 on a fresh Intel macOS VM running `1.3.0-beta.8`: panel read `All synced · GitHub · instant sync on · 1 Device`, while `youcoded-sync-personal` held 3110 blobs, 2 device records (`destinsZ13` linux, `GalaxyBook` win32 — the VM absent), 1 `ProjectSync/` entry and 3074 conversations. Every commit that day was authored by `destinsZ13`; the VM had neither pushed nor pulled. Root cause of the *sync* failure was mundane — `gh` isn't installed on a stock macOS (Xcode CLT ships `git`, not `gh`) — but the reporting failure is the bug: **`space-manager.ts:53` already throws the correct plain-language `'GitHub CLI (gh) is not installed — sync needs it to create your private repos'`, and it never reached the UI.** Per `docs/sync-spaces.md`, enable-path provisioning failures don't reject `enable()`; they arrive as broadcast error events, so that channel is where it was lost — determine whether the event is never emitted, emitted before the panel mounts and dropped from `recentEvents`, or emitted and then superseded. Compounding the false confidence: `instant sync on` came from the SyncHub socket, which authenticates against the account and needs no `gh` at all, so the one subsystem that *could* connect did. Two fixes, both needed: (1) gate green on evidence of a completed sync (e.g. `lastSyncEpoch`/per-space success) and give never-synced-yet its own non-green state, and (2) make the `gh`-missing error survive to the panel. Directly contradicts docs/error-message-standards.md — a status that says "synced" when nothing has ever synced is the misleading-message failure mode applied to success rather than error. Related: `#32` (misleading error messages audit). (found 2026-07-20 dogfooding beta.8 on the macOS VM) **Update 2026-07-22:** loss mechanism confirmed = emitted-then-superseded: the no-remote space emitted a phantom `synced` ~1s after the error, which `latestUnresolvedError` treated as resolution; poll re-emitted it forever and `ensureRemote` was never retried. **Fixed 2026-07-22** (youcoded merge `efab7e7d`): remote-less spaces never emit `synced`; provisioning retries every cycle via `ensureProvisioned` (self-healing, error re-emitted verbatim until cured); green is evidence-gated via pure `deriveSyncBoxState` (all active spaces provisioned + Personal `lastSyncAt` non-null, persisted in `sync-spaces.json`); enable preflights `github:status` into the connect modal and resumes after connect. Plan: docs/archive/plans/2026-07-22-sync-setup-overhaul.md (Phase 1 — shipped).

- [x] First-sync hydration has no progress state — "Setting up…" names the wrong operation and promises the wrong duration `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-20, fixed 2026-07-22 — youcoded merge `efab7e7d`; the "minimum viable" distinct hydrating phase shipped — real percentages via `git fetch --progress` deferred to overhaul Phase 2+)
  Enabling sync on a new device shows exactly one state for the entire first sync: `Setting up… / Creating your private repositories — a few seconds.` (`SyncPanel.tsx:905,929`). Both halves are wrong for the step that actually takes the time. Repo provisioning IS a few seconds; the **pull that follows** is the long pole — on Destin's account that's 3110 blobs / 3074 conversations over a fresh unrelated-histories merge, and it is completely unnarrated. The user watches a card claiming "a few seconds" for minutes and reasonably concludes it has stalled (reported verbatim 2026-07-20: *"'setting up' just makes it feel like it stalled"*). **This is not a copy fix — the data does not exist.** `enabling` is a local `useState` boolean in the panel (`SyncPanel.tsx:432`) flipped around the `enable()` call, so it tracks "we called enable", not progress; and `SpaceSyncEvent` (`sync-spaces/types.ts`) has no progress-shaped variant at all — `synced` carries only `{pushed, updated}` booleans, with no file/byte counts or phase. Doing this properly means emitting phase + progress from the engine/transport (git pull is the only place the counts exist) and adding a `hydrating` state to the ladder, which is the same ladder that needs a never-synced-yet state per the entry above — **do these two together, they are one state-machine fix.** Also seen in the same screenshot: three contradictory surfaces at once — sidebar `Backup & Sync · Not configured`, panel `Setting up…`, and a red `No sync configured / Set up sync` warning card — which is the same root cause (status derived from disconnected local flags rather than one source of truth). Minimum viable version if the full progress plumbing is too big: a distinct "Downloading your synced data — this can take a few minutes on first sync" phase entered once provisioning returns, even without a percentage. (found 2026-07-20 dogfooding beta.8 on the macOS VM) **Update 2026-07-22:** folded into the one-state-machine fix with the entry above. Plan: docs/archive/plans/2026-07-22-sync-setup-overhaul.md (Phase 1).

- [ ] Queued-message polish: attachment edits + fast-typist refill design call `task` `#renderer` `#native-runtime` (added 2026-07-22)
  Two accepted-for-now limits from M1's queue work (PR #204). (1) Editing a queued message that had attachments refills the composer with the raw space-joined file paths as text and does not re-attach the files — narrow but visibly wrong; fix by storing the display split (text + attachment list) in `queuedMessages` instead of the joined string. (2) Design call for Destin: if the user is actively typing in the instant an edit-refill lands (sub-ms IPC window), the refill currently wins; the alternative (typing wins) would silently destroy the queued message, so the safe order shipped — decide whether to append/merge instead. Also: the docked strip is renderer-local, so an app reload loses the strip display while the host queue still drains correctly; rehydrating the strip from the host queue on reload is the nicety that closes it.

- [ ] `sendChatMessage` overloads reject a non-literal provider argument `task` `#renderer` `#native-runtime` (added 2026-07-22)

- [x] Tighten `PRESENCE_STALE_MS` once the Android app-level ping release is widespread `task` `#social` `#worker` (added 2026-07-22, done 2026-07-22 — wecoded-marketplace PR #55)
  The heartbeat-liveness fix (wecoded-marketplace PR #54 + youcoded PR #209 — the stuck-"Online" ghost-socket bug, `docs/archive/investigations/2026-07-22-presence-stuck-online.md`) shipped with a 60-min rollout threshold; Destin called it down to 10 min the same evening (PR #55, deployed). Accepted cost at beta scale: Android builds without the app-level ping (unshipped in any APK) flap offline/online ~every 10–15 min until they update — the v1.3 APK carries the ping. Android Doze deferring pings on an idle phone reads as "away" and is treated as correct behavior.

- [ ] `PresenceClient.kt` has no JVM test harness — ping loop and state machine unverifiable off-device `task` `#android` `#social` (added 2026-07-22)
  The class is bound to `android.os.Handler(Looper.getMainLooper())` and the repo has no Robolectric dep, so the 30s app-level ping added in youcoded PR #209 (and the whole reconnect state machine, unlike desktop's fully-tested `reconnecting-ws.ts` twin) is verified only by CI compile + the worker-side "a pinging socket is never evicted" contract test. Either add Robolectric or extract the timing/state logic behind an injectable clock/scheduler like the desktop engine.

- [x] Desktop vitest flake: `ipc-handlers.test.ts` import-race on shared `/tmp/youcoded-vitest-home` `bug` `#tests` (added 2026-07-23, **FIXED 2026-08-05 — youcoded `f05b2711`: `SkillConfigStore.save()` now writes a per-writer temp name (`.${pid}.${seq}.tmp`) before the rename, so the exact ENOENT (`youcoded-skills.json.tmp` → `youcoded-skills.json`) can no longer collide. Verified on master 2026-08-12.** Note neither roadmap-proposed fix was taken — the shared `TEST_HOME` in `vitest.config.ts:30` is still one dir for all workers, so the BROADER shared-temp-home class lives on as the separate "Pre-existing vitest flake — parallel workers race on the shared temp home" entry above.)
  Intermittent (~50% in a worktree, also reproduced on clean master): collection of `tests/ipc-handlers.test.ts` failed with ENOENT renaming `youcoded-skills.json.tmp` → `youcoded-skills.json` under `/tmp/youcoded-vitest-home`. Import of `main.ts` triggers `SkillConfigStore.migrate → save` as a module side effect, and parallel vitest workers share the same temp HOME, so two files importing main-process modules raced the atomic-rename.

- [ ] Presence: Android "service alive" vs "user present" — decide what Online means on mobile `task` `#android` `#social` (added 2026-07-23)
  Desktop now gates presence on OS suspend (youcoded PR #211) and 10-min user idleness (PR #215) so Online ≈ a human at the machine. Android has no equivalent: the presence socket lives in `SessionService` (a foreground service that outlives the UI), so once the v1.3 ping ships, a phone with a long-running session would read Online with the screen off and the app backgrounded. Decide the semantic (screen-on? app-foreground? recent-touch?) and implement — candidates: gate `setDesired` on Activity lifecycle (foreground only), or a `PowerManager.isInteractive` + recency check mirroring the desktop idle gate. Related: the accepted-as-correct Doze behavior noted in the threshold entry above.

- [ ] Presence: periodic snapshot reconciliation — the last structural self-heal gap `task` `#social` `#worker` (added 2026-07-23, deferred at Destin's call — "fine for now")
  After the connect-time snapshot, a client's roster lives on deltas alone; a `user-left`/`user-joined` frame lost on a socket that survives (the `safeSend` silent-drop case, or the multi-device early-user-left edge where an alive-but-silent device resumes pinging without re-announcing) leaves stale presence until the next reconnect or friend-mutation poke. Textbook fix, cheap at this scale: have `PresenceRoom`'s existing 5-min alarm re-send `sendSnapshot` to every connected user, so ANY drift self-heals within ≤5 min. Closes the whole missed-delta class instead of the two known instances. Context: final audit in `docs/archive/investigations/2026-07-22-presence-stuck-online.md`.
  Found in M1 final review (youcoded PR #204): the overload pair on `sendChatMessage` (`native-send.ts`) only accepts literal `'native'`/`'claude'` — a future caller passing a `provider` variable (the reserved "Try again" retry caller noted in `native-send.ts:2-5` and `ChatView.tsx:757-759`) fails overload resolution. No live caller today; fix when the retry affordance lands.

- [x] StopButton's attentionState gate has no ChatView-level integration test `task` `#renderer` `#native-runtime` (added 2026-07-22, **RESOLVED — youcoded `e0bfb92b` moved the stop button into InputBar and `e4e6c85e` gave the gate a derived selector; `InputBar.test.tsx:221-237` now drives real reducer actions (`SESSION_INIT` → `USER_PROMPT` → `ATTENTION_STATE_CHANGED: 'stuck'`) through a provider tree and asserts the button disappears — the assertion this entry asked for, reached by a different route. Verified 2026-08-12.**)
  M1 shipped the stop button with unit tests on the extracted `StopButton` component; the guarantee that it never renders for a dead/errored session lived only in ChatView's `thinkingArea && attentionState === 'ok'` branch structure (M1 final review judged this a reasonable trade — mounting ChatView's provider tree is heavy).

- [x] Dependabot rollout across the workspace `task` `#ci` `#tooling` (added 2026-07-23, **DONE 2026-07-23**)
  Weekly grouped dependency-update PRs across youcoded (npm desktop + partykit, Gradle, GitHub Actions), wecoded-marketplace (worker npm + Actions), and wecoded-themes (npm + Actions). **Part A closed two CI gaps first:** youcoded `desktop-ci`/`android-ci` now check every PR via `pull_request` (was a branch-name allowlist that `dependabot/**` matched none of), and marketplace gained `worker-ci.yml` — worker code previously had NO pre-merge check (tests ran only inside the post-merge deploy job, against production), and it caught **three breaking bumps on day one**. **Tiered ignore policy:** `node-pty` ignored entirely (its post-install patch exits 0 when its target line is gone → silently drops the macOS spawn-helper fix with green CI), `electron`/`koffi`/`@vscode/ripgrep` majors ignored (green tests don't prove a *packaged* build works); no auto-merge or branch protection. PRs merged 2026-07-23: youcoded #216 + #217 + #238, marketplace #56 + #57, themes #21. **V1–V5 all verified**, including V1 — the one assumption unverifiable before the first run: Dependabot's `dependabot/**` PRs DO get a check rollup.
