  **SHIPPED 2026-08-27** — youcoded#343, merge `3b759931` (branch and worktree removed); skill side wecoded-marketplace#68 (merge `314617f8`), which teaches the assistant to write the reference block. Spec `docs/archive/specs/2026-08-10-chatsearch-session-references-design.md`, header spec `docs/archive/specs/2026-08-26-conversation-preview-header-design.md`, plan `docs/archive/plans/2026-08-25-chatsearch-session-references-plan.md`, handoff `docs/archive/handoffs/2026-08-10-chatsearch-state-of-play.md`. **What shipped:** search results become rows with Preview and Resume; a read-only preview panel with bounded paging, a Resume options popover, and a tag/note sheet; two IPC channels on all five surfaces with an Android stub; and the **reference block** — a fenced ```conversations block in the assistant's own message rendering as a card of those same rows. **How the trigger problem was solved:** it was not a tool. The app cannot hand Claude Code one, so the block is a renderer/parser trick over text, which works on both lanes with no plugin change. **Destin's calls at the 2026-08-27 gate:** the Referenced-conversations list stays (titles only); search results stay collapsed by default and scroll after ten; `show` does NOT get its own turn segment; repeat searches do NOT collapse into one card. **Carried:** the skill change reaches existing installs only once bundled-plugin upgrades ship (the entry below); `DeliverablesCard` and the reference card still re-express one shell — extract it when `feat/send-user-file-card` lands.

- [x] **Status bar: every number says what it counts** — Code Changes reads "No changes" after a native model rewrote twenty files; Session Cost is `--` in exactly the sessions that spend real money; In/Out/Cached mean session-totals in one runtime and last-turn-only in the other `feature` `#renderer` `#native-runtime` `#ux` (added 2026-08-26)
  Spec `docs/active/specs/2026-08-25-status-bar-session-relevance-design.md`, plan `docs/active/plans/2026-08-26-status-bar-session-relevance.md`, branch `feat/statusbar-session-relevance`, worktree `worktrees/statusbar-relevance`. Spec rewritten 2026-08-26 away from its first draft: the three-"kinds"-of-session taxonomy and its per-widget relevance table are GONE, replaced by two mechanical rules — a chip with no value hides instead of printing `--`, and what belongs to the other runtime does not render. Newly *working* in native sessions rather than hidden: Session Cost (priced per turn at the model that ran it — `CatalogModel.pricing` keeps only `{in,out}` today and that is the whole gap) and Code Changes (derived from `structuredPatch`, and it must walk `subagentSegments` or it undercounts hardest on the biggest sessions). Accepted cost, stated not mitigated: switching to a local model *because* you are near your 5-hour limit removes the number you were watching. Git Branch is explicitly NOT a relevance decision here — it is a missing feed, tracked separately. **SHIPPED 2026-08-27 — youcoded PR #340, merge `7d515480`.** Spec and plan archived to `docs/archive/`. Destin signed off the UI on a review deck (`docs/active/design/2026-08-26-statusbar-relevance/`), approving 6 of 9 points and declining 3 (a reserved second bar row, a "Free" chip in local sessions, a specialists chip). Beyond the spec, the work also fixed two pre-existing `/usage` defects it uncovered: subscription percentages rendered 100x too high in EVERY session type (42% drew as "4200%" with every bar pinned red), and the context row labelled REMAINING context as "used" and coloured it backwards (90% free drew as a red, nearly-full bar). It also closed a hole where `/usage` typed the literal text "/usage" at the model for anyone who had never run Claude Code. Two accepted gaps are tracked as their own bugs above: the chip runs ~25% low on a long compacting session, and a mid-turn model swap re-prices the whole turn.

Verified 2026-08-26: item 1 (Bash `workdir`) and item 4 (`<cwd-rules>` block) unbuilt — no grep hits; `prompt-assembly.ts:81` emits only `Working directory: <cwd>`. Item 2 shipped in a DIFFERENT shape on 2026-08-11 — `harness/tools/guards.ts`'s `shellCwdMissHint`/`workspaceRootMissHint` name the other cwd when a path misses, instead of rejecting relative paths. **Residual hazard the hints do not cover:** they fire only on a MISS, so when the same relative name exists under both the workspace root and a moved shell cwd, Read and Bash silently open different files. **Blocked on Destin:** reject relative paths outright (breaks nothing today, changes every model's habits) or keep accepting them with hints (leaves the both-files-exist case open)?

- [ ] Native sessions have no bypass permission mode — the Skip Permissions toggle is hidden and hardcoded off `feature` `#native-runtime` `#permissions` (added 2026-08-26)
  `NativePermissionMode` on master is `'ask' | 'auto-edit' | 'full-auto'` — there is no `'bypass'`, `App.tsx` hardcodes `skipPermissions: false` for native creates, and the toggle is hidden in three places. It was never wired, not broken. Shape: add `'bypass'`, seed it from the create/resume toggle, un-hide the toggle, extend the chip cycle and display map, keep the secret hard-deny, drop the `external_directory` ask under bypass. Four-surface IPC parity applies. Distinct from the full-auto external-read work, which only lifts the external ask for three read tools inside `full-auto`.

- [ ] Port Claude Code's `rm`-target analyzer as the non-overridable floor under native permissions `feature` `#native-runtime` `#permissions` (added 2026-08-26)
  Native's destructive deny-list matches `rm *` by pattern, which a remembered grant can beat. CC instead runs a target analyzer below the permission system that no rule can auto-allow — six message variants in the family (workspace directory, critical system directory, target not statically resolvable, glob traversing non-enumerable directories, `$UNSET` expanding to `/`, cd-before-removal). Pure logic, own test suite, platform-independent.

  The model request payload (system prompt, tool schemas) is never persisted, so a resume cannot reproduce the request that produced a turn, and persistence is persist-alongside with no fsync. `git grep "fsync|wireRecord|wire-log" origin/master -- desktop/src/main/harness` → 0 hits. Ground truth for eval scoring, and a prerequisite for faithful resume and for agent memory below.

Shape: chatsearch as a native tool; a bounded agent-maintained index file plus on-demand topic files; a pre-compaction flush wired into `maybeCompact`. Sequenced AFTER the eval CI gate (behaviour change needs eval coverage) and AFTER the wire log (needs the durable record underneath).

- [ ] Workspace guardrails from the 2026-07-28 session retrospective — three proposals plus five authoring papercuts, all still unapplied `feature` `#docs` `#tooling` (added 2026-08-26; Proposals 1 and 2 shipped 2026-07-28, 3/4/5 never did)
  Re-verified 2026-08-26, 29 days on. **(3) Spec counts are anchored or dated** — extend the `verify:` anchor convention so a count in a spec is either anchored or written "78 sites *(measured 2026-07-26)*", plus a CLAUDE.md line saying specs are snapshots whose numbers are stale by construction. Four wrong beliefs in one session came from bare numbers. **(4) Staging hygiene** — stage explicit paths, never `git add -A`/`.`/`<dir>/`; and before pushing to master run `git log --oneline origin/master..master` and confirm every commit is yours. Both motivating incidents recurred: the untracked `conversation-triage.mjs` bug above exists BECAUSE a directory add swept another session's file into `51c9531e`. **(5) `run-dev.sh --list` shows what is running** — a line per live instance (worktree, offset, profile, PID) so a session can pick a free offset without `ss -ltnp` forensics. **Plus five authoring papercuts** for `docs/PITFALLS.md`.

- [ ] Dependency upgrade projects — five majors that are real work, not bumps `task` `#tooling` `#ci` (added 2026-08-26)
  **All 8 open youcoded dependabot PRs have at least one failing check as of 2026-08-26; none is mergeable as-is.** Routine-but-red: #323 (desktop-minor-patch, 18 pkgs) and wecoded-themes #26. **Projects:** #242 TypeScript 5.9.3→**7.0.2** (all 4 checks fail, skips 6.x); #270 knip 5.88→6.32 (config-schema change, and `knip` is a `verify.sh` gate); #271 jsdom 29→30 (renderer test suite); #237 Android Gradle Plugin 8.7→9.3.1 (needs Gradle+JDK alignment); #236 Compose BOM 2024.12→2026.06 (~18 months of API drift); #316 gradle-minor-patch (**mislabelled — it carries Kotlin 2.1.0→2.4.10**); wecoded-marketplace #61 vitest 2.1.9→4.1.10 (two majors). - [ ] **Git Branch chip is missing in native sessions** — Claude Code's statusline is its only feed, so a native coder session in a repo shows nothing `feature` `#renderer` `#native-runtime` `#ux` (added 2026-08-25, Destin — split out of the status-bar session-relevance work, which deliberately scoped it OUT)
  Verified 2026-08-25, not inferred. The chip renders from `StatusData.gitBranch`, whose only writer is `buildStatusData` in `ipc-handlers.ts` (~:2023): it walks `sessionIdMap` (desktopId → **Claude Code** session id) and reads `~/.claude/.gitbranch-<claudeId>`, a file written by `statusline.sh`. A native session has no Claude Code id and runs no statusline, so it never gets a map entry, `gitBranch` stays null, and `StatusBar.tsx`'s `{show('git-branch') && gitBranch && …}` gate silently drops the chip. **This is a missing feed, not a correct hide** — a native session working in a git repo has exactly the same need, which is why the session-relevance work classes Git Branch as "hidden for the wrong reason" and does NOT mark it inapplicable-by-design.
  **Cheaper than it looks — the app already reads the branch.** `git-types.ts`'s `GitFileStatusResult` carries `branch: string | null`, served by the existing `git:file-status` handler; today it needs a file path (`relPath`) because it's built for the file footer (`useGitFileStatus.ts`). The `git:watch` / `git:unwatch` / `git:changed` trio already exists too, and the `.git` watcher behind it already fires on commits, checkouts and staging — i.e. the live-update path a branch chip needs is built and shipping. Likely shape: a branch-only read over the session's cwd (a new thin call, or `relPath: null` on the existing one) plus a subscription to `git:changed`, feeding the SAME chip so both runtimes render identically. **Check before building:** the git surface degrades to unsupported on Android/remote (`useGitFileStatus`'s header says every call rejects there), so the chip must keep hiding cleanly on those, exactly as it does today.
  **Sequencing:** this is renderer + main work with no harness dependency, so it does not have to wait on the status-bar relevance change — but landing it AFTER was going to let a "relevance table" flip Git Branch in one edit — but **the relevance spec was rewritten 2026-08-26 and no longer has that table**; it runs on two mechanical rules ("a chip with no value hides"; "what belongs to the other runtime does not render"), and Git Branch already hides via rule 1. So this is a pure feed fix and nothing in that spec needs editing when it lands. **An approved design for this already exists and was never built:** `docs/active/specs/2026-07-23-statusbar-git-branch-popup-design.md` (status `active` since 2026-07-23, zero implementation — `StatusBar.tsx:1415` is still a static span). Its §3 designs exactly the git-subsystem swap described above, plus ahead/behind, dirty count and PR status on click, with Android parity scoped. Read it before scoping this item; demote it to `draft` if this supersedes it.

- [x] Show progress while a tool call's arguments stream (the "silent Write" blind spot) `feature` `#native-runtime` `#renderer` `#ux` (added 2026-08-12, Destin; **SHIPPED 2026-08-13 — youcoded `0ccb576c`**)
  When a native model generates a large tool call — a Write whose input is a whole file — the app shows only the pulsing thinking indicator until the arguments finish. Reproduced live 2026-08-12 (dev session `c08e0b47`, DeepSeek v4-flash flappy-bird ask): ~4 min of reasoning, one line of visible text, then multi-minute silence while the Write args streamed; Destin read it as a hang, and reports this shape recurring. The stall watchdog is rightly quiet (data IS flowing) — the gap is presentational.
  **Verified feasible:** ai@7 emits `tool-input-start` / `tool-input-delta` / `tool-input-end` stream parts (present in `node_modules/ai/dist/index.d.ts`); the consume loop's switch (`harness-session.ts` ~1789) currently handles only the completed `tool-call` part, so the deltas are dropped on the floor today.
  **Shape to brainstorm, not decided:** the emit surface is FROZEN (`native-runtime.md`), so progress should ride the existing `assistant-thinking` heartbeat the way `promptProcessing` already does (display-only, never persisted) — e.g. `{ toolArgProgress: { toolName, chars } }` → ThinkingIndicator renders "Writing a file — 12 KB so far". Open questions: per-tool naming (Write has a file_path only after enough JSON has streamed to parse it), throttling (per-chunk emit would spam the IPC), and whether Bash/Edit want it too or only file-writing tools.
  **Update 2026-08-12 (same day):** Destin widened the goal — minimize generic-spinner time overall, preferring visible tool liveness or streaming text; an EARLY TOOL CARD (rendered at `tool-input-start`, detail filled as args stream) is now on the table alongside the progress line. Full investigation handoff with everything verified this session (adapter emission chain, watchdog feeding, OpenRouter probe result): `docs/archive/handoffs/2026-08-12-tool-streaming-visibility.md`. Probe preserved at `scripts/tool-arg-stream-probe.mjs`.
  **SPECCED 2026-08-12:** `docs/archive/specs/2026-08-12-tool-arg-streaming-visibility.md` — the early tool card won. A normal `ToolCard` appears at `tool-input-start` in its running state, detail line `preparing… N chars`, expanded body "Still preparing tool call…", and it transitions IN PLACE to the real card because `tool-input-start` and the completed `tool-call` carry the identical id (verified in the installed `provider-utils` dist) and `TRANSCRIPT_TOOL_USE` is already idempotent by `toolUseId`. Progress rides `assistant-thinking` as `toolPreparing` (dropped by `session-store.ts:93`, so nothing partial is persisted); `preparing` is a boolean on a `running` entry, never a fifth `ToolCallStatus`; `endTurn` DELETES preparing cards rather than failing them. No partial-JSON parsing of `file_path` — dropped as a truncation-failure class for information the finished card shows moments later. Native-only; CC and Android unchanged.
  **SHIPPED 2026-08-13** (youcoded merge `0ccb576c`, plan `docs/archive/plans/2026-08-12-preparing-tool-cards.md`). Built as specced. Two things the plan did not foresee, both now in the code: (1) teaching `endTurn` to prune an emptied preparing group means it returns `assistantTurns`, which silently clobbered the two callers that build that map themselves — `TRANSCRIPT_TURN_COMPLETE` (usage/model stamping) and `TRANSCRIPT_INTERRUPT` (`stopReason`, whose "Interrupted" footer went blank) — so `endTurn` now takes their map as a third argument instead of spreading over it; (2) `injectPlanSegment` needs `beforeGroupId` now that placement runs first, or an ExitPlanMode plan renders *below* its own tool-group. Group placement for both the preparing and real paths is one shared helper (`placeToolInCurrentGroup`) precisely so they cannot drift apart — if they place differently, the real tool-use adds a second card instead of superseding. Verified live by Destin on a native session. Carried open item, not a blocker: whether llama.cpp's `--jinja` grammar-constrained path emits a first tool-call delta carrying the function name — a provider that never emits `tool-input-start` simply falls through to the old behavior.

- [ ] Tell the USER when context files were truncated, not just the model `feature` `#native-runtime` `#harness` `#ux` (added 2026-08-11, Destin)
  Every truncation the harness performs currently announces itself **to the model and nowhere else**. `fitProjectInstructions` outlines an over-budget `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` and appends a notice the model reads; `fitInjection` does the same for skill bodies, path-triggered rules, and nested instruction files; `mcpBudgetSizing` drops whole MCP servers from a session and records them in `HarnessSession.droppedMcpServers`, which **has no UI reader at all** (its own JSDoc says "a later task wires the actual surface"). So a user on a small local model can be running a session where most of their project's rules are outlined rather than present, several skills are cut, and entire MCP servers are unattached — with nothing on screen saying so. The failure is silent in exactly the way that produces "why is it ignoring my CLAUDE.md".
  **Wanted:** a session-start notice naming what was cut and by how much — the file(s), how much survived, and (for MCP) which servers were dropped. Destin's framing: make any context truncation transparent, with a message at the beginning of a session.
  **Design questions, not yet decided:** where it lives (a dismissible banner in the timeline, a status-bar chip, or an `(i)` on the existing context surface — the accessibility standard in `CLAUDE.md` argues for an explainer popup, not raw numbers); whether it fires per session or only when something actually got cut (the latter, or it becomes noise); whether it is per-file or one aggregate line; and whether it needs to survive resume (the notice is a fact about the session's prompt, which IS rebuilt on resume, so probably yes). Note the budget is fixed at session start and `setBinding` does NOT re-apply it — so switching to a smaller model mid-session does NOT re-truncate, and the notice must not imply otherwise.
  **Depends on nothing.** `fitProjectInstructions` already returns `{ text, truncated }` and `fitInjection` returns the same shape; both are currently discarded by their callers. `droppedMcpServers` is already tracked. This is a plumbing + UI task, not a new mechanism. Context on the truncation rework that surfaced it: youcoded PR #289 (project instructions are now outlined, not tail-cut).
  **DESIGN APPROVED 2026-08-17 — broadened from "truncation notice" to "session context transparency".** Destin: the mechanism should be a broader context-transparency display — at the start of EVERY session (local or cloud), a panel showing the full accounting of what the assistant began with: system prompt, CLAUDE.md (as truncated), skill/tool lists, with clickable outlinks to the real files, clear visual hierarchy, and a "Manage Assistant Settings" button (stubbed — will open the Assistant-settings panel, see below). Any trimmed surface gets a "Full vs supplied" diff. Mockup approved in the workbench compare view (tabbed layout, "B"), on branch `feat/context-truncation-notice` in worktree `worktrees/context-truncation`. Handoff: `docs/active/handoffs/2026-08-17-session-context-panel-handoff.md`. Backend still open: the real `native.onSessionContext` channel wiring `fitProjectInstructions`/`fitInjection`/`droppedMcpServers` facts (currently `MOCK_ONLY`).
  **Related:** the "Manage Assistant Settings" stub's destination is the Assistant-settings consolidated panel — `docs/active/design/2026-08-17-assistant-settings-panel-design.md` (concurrent design, same day).

- [ ] Move Write's and Edit's staleness checks from mtime to a content hash `feature` `#native-runtime` `#harness` (added 2026-08-10)
  Both tools currently compare `fs.statSync(abs).mtimeMs` against the mtime recorded at read time (`write.ts`, `edit.ts`) — deliberately made consistent with each other during the 2026-08-10 harness-tool-honesty work (Write previously had no freshness check at all; see `docs/archive/investigations/2026-08-10-review-claims-verified.md` claim 2). mtime is the wrong long-term primitive: a `touch`/checkout can bump it with unchanged bytes (false-positive rejection), and some filesystems' clock resolution can miss a true same-second change (false-negative — a real external edit not caught). `docs/active/investigations/2026-08-10-harness-mutation-safety-prior-art.md` §1 documents two real precedents for the fix — Gemini CLI's Edit tool hashes with SHA-256 at read time and compares before applying; OpenCode V2's `FileMutation.writeIfUnchanged` does a raw byte-array comparison under a per-path mutex. Move BOTH tools together, not one at a time — an in-code WHY comment in `write.ts` already flags this as deferred-not-forgotten. Re-archive the mutation-safety doc once this ships.

- [x] **Workbench comparison view — 3 candidates side by side with round tracking** `feature` `#workbench` (added + shipped 2026-07-31, on `feat/session-launch-ux`)
  `?mode=workbench&view=compare`. Three candidate designs for one surface rendered simultaneously against the same mock backend, with a pick recorded per round so a design narrows over passes: pick one of three → Claude authors three more from it → pick again. Replaces the query-param switcher loop (`utils/design-variant.ts`, added and deleted three times), which meant flipping between candidates one at a time from memory AND editing the production component to hold them all. Candidates live in `dev/workbench/compare/registry.tsx` and are built from the REAL primitives and tokens — the only new code in a candidate is the arrangement under comparison, which is what makes a winner paste back into production unchanged. Two constraints found building it: a real `Dialog` can't be a candidate (fixed-position, all three would stack centre-screen — hence the `panel` frame), and picks live in localStorage because HMR reloads constantly.

  **Status 2026-07-28 (final) — THE KIT IS COMPLETE. All 12 roles + D1 shipped to `feat/menu-internals-tranche-1` (24 commits, UNMERGED, no PR). Destin reviewed and approved in a dev instance.** K5 and K9 were expected to need a fourth tranche behind a copy pass; the copy pass ran the same day and **turned out to be structural, not editorial** — seven of Remote Access's eleven setup branches carried over verbatim and all three danger zones already had a consequence sentence satisfying `docs/error-message-standards.md`. Five copy decisions (worksheet: `docs/archive/specs/2026-07-28-k5-k9-copy-pass.md`) and they landed in the same tranche. **One real bug fixed on the way:** `{setupError || 'Setup failed'}` replaced a missing reason with a hardcoded guess, leaving the user two words and no next step — now the real error + Retry when there is one, and the two-action fallback when there is not. That gave `ErrorState` its first call sites (it had none BY DECISION), so its exemption in `primitive-adoption.test.ts` came off and `error-message-standards.md` was corrected — it still told a future session to remove an entry that is now gone. **D1 chrome residue closed 2026-07-28:** six views (not the three first reported — that count came from memory rather than a search) still painted their own dialog header, close, back chevron and scroll body. Five converted; `SettingsPanel` and `SyncPanel` own their Dialogs so their views just pass `title`/`headerActions` up, and `ThemeScreen`'s `editingSlug` was lifted to its host the way K12 lifted `showInfo`. `SubViewHeader` deleted, three back-chevron reimplementations gone. **The setup wizard keeps its header by design** — its title derives from state that belongs inside it, and the real fix (the wizard owning its own `<Dialog>`) turns it from replacing the sync panel into stacking over it, which is a UX call. Guarded by `tests/dialog-chrome-authority.test.ts`. **Merged 2026-07-28 as PR #264** (26 commits, 3482 tests, all four CI jobs green). Docs archived to `docs/archive/`. Follow-ups tracked separately: the setup wizard owning its own `<Dialog>` (a UX call — it would stack over the sync panel rather than replace it), and the three counted backlogs the guards now hold. Three debts the guards surfaced are recorded as per-file COUNTS that may shrink and never grow — 9 hardcoded error fallbacks (that IS the v1.3.1 audit), 5 centred coloured status lines on non-settings surfaces, ~20 surviving `#DD4444` (change 17's unfinished business).

  **Status 2026-07-28 — tranches 1, 2/D1 and 3 are DONE and pushed to `feat/menu-internals-tranche-1` (23 commits, UNMERGED, no PR). Destin reviewed and approved in a dev instance.** Shipped: K1, K2, K3, K4, K6, K7, K8, K10, K12 and D1. **Remaining: K5 (status strip) and K9 (danger zone), both gated on a copy pass** — K5 turns Remote's eight setup branches into eight status messages and K9 needs a consequence sentence per danger zone, and `docs/error-message-standards.md` binds both, so those are Destin's words to write rather than Claude's to invent. K11 (footer) folded into K9: its only real dialog footer is ContextPopup's, and moving that warning out of the footer IS K9's "callout and control stay together" fix. Plan: `docs/archive/plans/2026-07-28-menu-internals-tranche-3.md`. **The spec's ledger overstated what was left, for the fourth time (spec §0b holds the first three).** K12 was listed as "retires 5 mechanisms" but `SettingsExplainer` was already the one renderer with four hosts sharing one payload; the real remainder was that it predates `<Dialog>` and hand-rolled a header, a scroll body and an Esc handler. K11 had almost nothing independent left. K4 and K6 were undercounted in the other direction — K4 by two tones the spec cannot express (a `bg-green-500/10` success block, and `bg-red-500/10` at two border opacities around text already using the `destructive` token), K6 by two glyphs and by conflating three different problems under "the last bare ✕". Standing lesson, now proven twice: **read for the shape, not for the recipes someone already wrote down** — and that applies to the guard as hard as to the survey, since the first K4 guard required the tint before `border` and silently scored `Button.tsx` as zero.

  **Status 2026-07-26 — tranche 1 and tranche 2/D1 are DONE and pushed to `feat/menu-internals-tranche-1` (18 commits, UNMERGED, no PR).** Tranche 1: one section-label recipe (~90 sites, six orderings), decorative dividers deleted, the Sound bug, `SegmentedTabs` and `LoadingState` adoption. Tranche 2/D1: built `<Dialog>`, migrated **40 dialogs across 22 files**, deleted `SettingsPopup.tsx`. **Remaining in tranche 2: K2 rows** (decided — one component, two densities by role: `nav` `text-sm`/`2xs` for rows that navigate, `item` `text-xs`/`3xs` for rows being scanned; the five shapes K2 retires were about description PLACEMENT, not size, so the win survives). Then bookkeeping + PR, then tranche 3. **D1 needed three amendments once it met the code (spec §0b):** `SettingsPopup` was NOT "already correct" — it left the scroll body to callers and 2 of its 7 got it wrong, producing dialogs that clipped with no way to scroll; the `340/420/560/820` ladder was circular (fitted to arbitrary old values — `lg 560` was a width nothing had ever used) and was replaced by content-derived sizes `prompt 340 / panel 420 / document 600`, computed from reading measure now that the app is known to be entirely monospace (0.6em advance); and `max-h-[80vh]` was the wrong unit (≈700px on a laptop, ≈1730px on a 4K display) — the cap is now 1.4× each size's own width, clamped by `calc(100vh - 6rem)`. Two of my own errors are recorded there too, both the same shape (a negative asserted from a partial read, then written somewhere durable): `SyncPanel`/`QuickChips` exempted as "anchored popovers" when their style objects showed them centered, and an adoption guard scoped to `components/*.tsx` that could not see `App.tsx` or any subdirectory, hiding five more dialogs.

C declined — it re-opens change 50 (headerless drawer) and the 2026-07-23 git-branch popup spec, and its case doesn't clear that bar. Accepted cost of A, explicitly: 6 modal-on-modal sites and up to 2 stacked scrims remain. Tranche 1 plan: `docs/archive/plans/2026-07-26-menu-internals-tranche-1.md` — five D2-independent tasks (K1 labels, K8 dividers, the Sound audition fix, K3 SegmentedTabs adoption, K10 LoadingState adoption), each with a source-text guard test in the established `*-authority.test.ts` style. Tranche 2 = D1 shell + K2/K6/K7 rows; tranche 3 = per-menu restructures (K4/K5/K9/K11/K12), gated on a copy pass. **Re-measurement against post-tranche-8 master corrected three numbers in the original draft** (spec §0): D4 type tokens **already shipped** in tranche 6 (`text-[10px]` 313→1) so the "prerequisite" tranche is done; `SegmentedTabs` now has 2 call sites, so the headline "zero call sites" finding is **stale** — though tranche 8's own audit replaced it with a stronger one (`FirstRunView` declared a LOCAL `ProgressBar` that *shadowed* the shared import, so grepping the name matched while the primitive had no real consumers; now guarded by `tests/primitive-adoption.test.ts`); and K1 is **78 sites, not ~30** — the original grep matched one Tailwind class ordering and missed a 49-site variant that renders identically. Standing rule this produced: audit a multi-class recipe on the *set* of classes, never the string.
  Spec: `docs/archive/specs/2026-07-26-menu-internals-design-system.md`. Artifact (interactive, 5 themes, verbatim class strings): https://claude.ai/code/artifact/7e886cfc-9957-4e5f-918d-3d83bd77e0a6. **Extends the 2026-07-16 UI-consistency spec, which locked *controls* across 8 tranches but never touched dialog anatomy or menu internals.** Destin's criterion (2026-07-25): *"clear/consistent menu structure across most menus (some unique elements are okay where they make sense for unique features, but elements performing similar functions should share design)"*. A first pass proposed three *shell* designs and was course-corrected — chrome discipline alone leaves every menu looking the same on the inside. The kit is 12 functional roles with one design each, measured against source: section label **4 shapes → 1**; setting row **5 → 1** (the single rule that kills all five: the description always lives in the left column under the title — Buddy's popup currently uses two different placements within itself); choice group **7 recipes → 3 with a decidable threshold rule** (4 radii, 4 text sizes, 3 inactive treatments today); callout 3 geometries → 1 shape/3 tones; status strip (Remote's 8 ad-hoc setup branches → 1); item list 3 → 1; field+action (kills the button-styled-as-a-field project-folder picker); divider **4 idioms → 0** (`space-y-5` + labels already separate); danger zone 3 bespoke → 1, callout and control kept together; empty/loading/error → the existing `ui/states.tsx` family, **which unblocks held change 33**; footer 3 conventions → 1 rule; explainer **5 mechanisms → 1** (all five already consume the same `{intro, sections}` payload). Four controls stay unique because the *function* is (theme tile grid, volume slider, QR pairing block, `kbd` chip) plus two inherited documented exceptions. Two findings that outlive whatever ships: **`ui/SegmentedTabs.tsx` has ZERO call sites** — built as change 45, never adopted, with seven hand-rolled choice groups sitting beside it (any primitive shipped without migrating its call sites in the same tranche lands here); and **`ProgressBar` + `UsageCard.tsx:79-80` already render context-remaining** while `ContextPopup.tsx:135` renders the same metric a second way as a `text-3xl` billboard. K1–K12 are shell-agnostic and can be approved independently. **Blocking decision is D2** — the navigation model: A drawer+modals (6 modal-on-modal sites, up to 2 stacked scrims, small migration) / B one frame with pages (0 stacked, +1 click to Remote→Devices, medium) / C screen+anchored popovers (0 stacked, largest migration, and it re-opens two shipped decisions — change 50's headerless drawer and the 2026-07-23 git-branch popup spec). They compose: A ⊂ B, and B is what C degrades to below 640px. D4 (type tokens, change 35, tranche 6) is a prerequisite — every recipe is written in `text-2xs/3xs/4xs`.
- [ ] Android toolchain migration: Kotlin 2.4 + AGP 9 + compose-bom 2026.06 `chore` `#android` `#build` (added 2026-07-25, from the dependabot triage — blocks 3 open PRs)
  Three youcoded dependabot PRs (#250, #237, #236) are **one coupled migration, not three bumps** — each fails on the next link in the chain, so they can only land together. Gradle 9.6.1 (#234) is already merged and was the first link; these are the rest. Failure modes read off the actual CI logs 2026-07-24/25:
  - **#237 AGP 8.7.0 → 9.3.1.** First failed with *"Minimum supported Gradle version is 9.5.0. Current version is 8.11.1"* — that's what merging #234 fixed. Rebased onto Gradle 9 it now fails **deeper**: *"An exception occurred applying plugin request [id: 'org.jetbrains.kotlin.android'] > Cannot add extension with name 'kotlin', as there is an extension already registered with that name."* AGP 9 registers its own `kotlin` extension, so it collides with the separately-applied Kotlin plugin at 2.1.0. **AGP 9 therefore requires the Kotlin bump in #244** — it cannot go in alone.
  - **#250 root gradle group (8 updates; superseded #244, which dependabot closed once #218 landed the safe subset).** Carries Kotlin 2.1.0 → 2.4.10, which fails at `app/build.gradle.kts:117`: *"Using 'jvmTarget: String' is an error. Please migrate to the compilerOptions DSL."* A real source change, not a version pin. (Its safe minor/patch subset — commons-compress, xz, zstd-jni, commonmark, Java-WebSocket, coroutines-test, mockito — already landed separately via #218, so #250 is now exactly the risky half — Kotlin 2.4.10, camera 1.6.1, activity/lifecycle — and still fails on the same jvmTarget line.)
  - **#236 compose-bom 2024.12.01 → 2026.06.01.** Fails `:app:lintVitalAnalyzeReleaseTest` with *"Unexpected failure during lint analysis of MainActivity.kt (this is a bug in lint or one of the libraries it depends on)"* — a ~19-month BOM jump against AGP 8's bundled lint. Plausibly resolves once AGP 9 brings a matching lint, which is another reason to sequence it with the other two rather than debug it alone.
  **Suggested order once someone picks this up:** migrate `app/build.gradle.kts:117` off the deprecated `jvmTarget: String` to the `compilerOptions` DSL first (it's the only hand-written change), then land Kotlin 2.4.10 + AGP 9.3.1 together, then re-run compose-bom. Android CI (`./gradlew test` + `assembleDebug` + `assembleReleaseTest`) is the gate and has been honest about every step so far.
  **Separately blocked on a compileSdk decision:** okhttp/mockwebserver 5.4.0 (#235, and the closed /app twins #219/#222) fail `checkDebugAarMetadata` — *"requires libraries and applications that depend on it to compile against version 36 or later of the Android APIs."* That's a compileSdk 36 bump, its own call, not part of this chain.
- [ ] Worker test-infra migration: vitest 4 + vitest-pool-workers 0.18 + wrangler 4 `chore` `#marketplace` `#worker` (added 2026-07-24, from the dependabot triage — blocks 3 open PRs)
  Three marketplace dependabot PRs (#60, #61, #63) all fail `npm ci` with ERESOLVE and **none of them can be merged as a dependency bump** — together they're one coordinated migration. Verified by building it in a scratch worktree 2026-07-24, not inferred from the peer ranges.
  **What's actually blocking.** #61 takes vitest 2.1.9 → 4.1.10 alone, but `@cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers@0.5.41` peer-wants `vitest 2.0.x - 2.1.x`. #60 takes pool-workers → 0.18.8, which peer-wants `vitest ^4.1.0`. So they're complementary halves — **but installing both together still doesn't work**: pool-workers 0.18.8 **deletes the `@cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers/config` subpath export entirely**, and `worker/vitest.config.ts:1` imports `defineWorkersConfig` from exactly that path. 0.18.8's root export is `cloudflarePool` / `cloudflareTest` / `readD1Migrations` — `defineWorkersConfig` no longer exists at all, so `npm test` dies at config load with `Missing "./config" specifier`. It also drags in `wrangler@4.114.0` transitively while `worker/package.json` pins `^3.80.0`, leaving two wranglers in the tree.
  **So the real unit of work is:** rewrite `worker/vitest.config.ts` to the vitest-4 pool API (the package ships a codemod at `@cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers/codemods/vitest-v3-to-v4`), bump wrangler 3 → 4, and re-verify the D1 migration harness (`readD1Migrations` + the `TEST_MIGRATIONS` binding + `environment: "test"`) still works. Doing the wrangler 4 bump here also unblocks **#63** (`@cloudflare/workers-types` 4 → 5, which fails because `wrangler@3.114.17` peerOptional-wants `^4.20250408.0`) — one migration clears all three PRs.
  **Why it deserves care rather than a quick bump:** `worker-deploy.yml` runs the test suite, then applies D1 migrations, then deploys to production on every push to master. A silently-degraded test harness doesn't just go red — it stops gating a live deploy.
- [ ] Resume-on-startup ("Welcome back") — rebuild on the Conversation Store `feature` `#conversations` `#renderer` (added 2026-07-24, from a branch audit — milestone TBD, Destin's call)
  **The want:** on cold start, if YouCoded had sessions live in the strip when it last shut down — window close, crash, OS kill, anything that wasn't an explicit per-session X — present a "Welcome back" screen listing them with per-session checkboxes and a one-click Resume-all, plus a Start-fresh escape. Originally specced 2026-05-01 (`docs/archive/specs/2026-05-01-resume-active-sessions-on-startup-design.md`); the UX in Task 6 of the archived plan is still the intended shape.
  **The 2026-05-01 attempt is dead and its architecture must NOT be revived.** `feat/resume-on-startup` (3 commits, Tasks 1–2 of 10, never PR'd, deleted 2026-07-24) persisted a second JSON file at `~/.claude/youcoded-active-sessions.json`. That predates the Conversation Store: master's records already carry a superset of its fields (`title`, `lastActive`, `projectName`, `originalPath`, `transcriptRef`, `device`, `lastUsedModel`) plus flags, convergent merges, healing, and sync. Reviving it means a second unsynced source of truth for conversation activity, and it's provider-blind — post-M2 a resume needs to know `'claude'` vs `'native'` and, for native, a model binding (`nativeHost.resume(id, cwd, binding)`). The archived plan carries a SUPERSEDED banner explaining this.
  **What's actually missing on master is small:** a durable *"this was open in the strip at shutdown"* marker. That's session-set membership, not conversation metadata, and `store-core.ts` already has `FlagState` + `setFlag`. Sketch: set the flag on first turn, clear it on explicit X-close, leave it alone on quit/crash; at startup list flagged records and hand them to the existing provider-aware resume path, which already handles leases, takeover, and the native model picker. Reuses `session-browser.ts`'s live-session exclusion rather than reinventing it.
  **Open design question that blocks it — device scoping.** Conversation records SYNC, so a naive boolean flag means the sessions left open on the Z13 pop up as a "Welcome back" screen on the Mac. Needs to key on the per-INSTALL `getDeviceIdentity(userData)` id — NOT the per-machine one (`docs/conversations.md` → leases, and the "Do NOT swap the lease id" invariant). Same family as the v1.3.1 cross-device native resume deferral; decide deliberately before building.
- [ ] No in-app way to resolve a sync conflict copy `feature` `#sync` (added 2026-07-24, Destin during the beta.9 dogfood — milestone TBD, his call)
  Today the entire conflict story is one transient amber line in the Backup & Sync panel: *"Some files had conflicting edits — the other device's copy was kept alongside yours (look for "(from …)" files)"* (`SyncPanel.tsx:1215`). It is keyed off a per-boot sync event (`visibleSpaceEvents.some(e => e.type === 'conflict')`), so it vanishes on restart whether or not anything was resolved; it names no file, offers no action, and sends the user to a file manager to hunt for `notes (from Laptop, 2026-07-03).md` — the copy `git-transport.ts` writes via `conflictCopyName` (`guards.ts:69`) when both devices edited one file.
  **Scope note:** conversation *records* do NOT need this — `foldConflictCopies` (`store-core.ts:256`) heals them automatically and the copies are pruned. What needs a resolver is user-visible FILES: synced project folders and Personal files. Whatever the UI is, it must (a) find copies by disk scan rather than a live event, so the state survives restarts and shows a real count; (b) offer per-file keep-mine / keep-theirs / keep-both with a preview for text; (c) route the resolution back through the sync engine as an ordinary synced change (delete/replace propagates), never a silent local edit; (d) stay honest about binary and large files, where a preview isn't possible.
- [x] Project View: merge Artifacts + All files into one Files tab `feature` `#project-view` (added 2026-07-23, **SHIPPED 2026-07-24 — youcoded PR #247, merge `b0f990b9`**)
  Destin's ask: the Artifacts tab looked like a subset of All files, so having both made no sense. **It wasn't a subset** — four things live only on the tracked side: external pins (`kind: 'external'`), version history (discovered files get `versions: []`, `project-file-discovery.ts:122`), deleted/orphan records, and gated roots (a home-dir project skips the disk scan entirely but still lists artifacts, `ipc-handlers.ts:3016`). So this is a merge, not a deletion.
  Shape: one `Files` tab (segments 4→3) = the on-disk `Project Files` walk only, in-folder artifacts **undifferentiated** (the disk is the truth). `+ Add file` becomes a native-picker **Move/Copy import** into the browsed folder (new `artifacts:import-file`; verify-before-unlink so a cross-filesystem move can't eat the source; Replace/Keep both/Skip asked once per batch, disclosed-collisions gate so an undisclosed collision falls back to keep-both; self-import guard so Move+Replace onto a file's own path can't delete it).
  **DESCOPED after Destin tested the branch (2026-07-23):** the design also added an `External Artifacts` section (sidecar records outside the folder, via a flipped `visible-artifacts.ts` rule 4 that dropped the pin requirement). Against his real sidecar it was ~95% incidental noise (163 scratchpad temps, 37 other-device Windows paths, 19 `.claude/` internals; `manualIncludes`=0). Removed; rule 4 reverted to "externals hidden unless pinned". Externals live in the Session Drawer per session — their correct home. The section was Claude's idea and it did not earn its place; the lesson is in the spec amendment banner.
  **"Show deleted" leaves Project View but STAYS in the session drawer** — `VersionEvent` has no content field (`shared/artifacts/types.ts:14`), so a deleted record is a tombstone, not a recovery path; but seeing everything Claude did in a session, deletions included, is the drawer's whole purpose. The flag is synced cross-device (`theme-context.tsx:526`), so a naive cleanup would drop a real preference — pinned by a guard test.
  **Desktop-only in practice:** every `artifacts:*` channel on Android is a `not-implemented-on-mobile` stub (`SessionService.kt:3585-3601`) — mobile Project View is v2, so the shared renderer shows the tab with no data behind it. Ships with an Android stub + a Move gate only. Rider, off the critical path: Android's picker renames every selection to `<timestamp>.<ext>` (`MainActivity.kt:56-64`), so chat attachments are all named `1784838134583.png` — fixed via `OpenableColumns.DISPLAY_NAME` in its own revertible commit.
  Spec: docs/active/specs/2026-07-23-project-view-file-merge-design.md · Plan: docs/active/plans/2026-07-23-project-view-file-merge-plan.md

- [ ] Session-switch motion — pill reveal + bubble pop `feature` `#renderer` (added 2026-07-20, **draft PR youcoded#192 — do NOT merge as-is**; re-checked 2026-08-26 — still open/draft/untouched, worktree `worktrees/session-switch-animation`, 1 ahead / **1076 behind**. **New blocker that may invalidate the DESIGN, not just the code:** `81c9562d` (2026-08-06) put `contentVisibility: sessionActive ? 'visible' : 'hidden'` on the ChatView root, taking a non-selected pane's subtree out of rendering — so its entries cannot be intersecting and the observer strips `in-view`, which is exactly what both animation selectors and the `--switch-i` stagger depend on. Resolve that before touching the merge conflict. The handoff's "nothing in the suite renders ChatView" is now half-false: three master tests do; SessionStrip still has none.)
  Destin's ask: clicking between conversation tabs "feels jumpy/stuttery" — content pops in instantly and the switcher's tab names expand instantly instead of rolling out. His constraints: **no crossfade/slide** for bubbles (they should disappear and reappear *bouncy*), the strip should feel like the existing hover reveal over collapsed dots, and **only actually-visible bubbles animate**. Design approved visually first — mockup at `docs/active/prototypes/2026-07-20-session-switch-animation.html`.
  **Root cause worth keeping: the pill had TWO independent reasons it couldn't animate**, so "add a transition" was the wrong diagnosis and fixing either alone would have looked like the fix failed. (1) `maxWidth` was `undefined` when active (`SessionStrip.tsx:785-787`) — no numeric pair to interpolate. (2) `transition` was hard-set to `'none'` for every pack-expanded pill (`:789`), and `packSessions` puts the active pill in `expanded` unconditionally (`pack-sessions.ts:53`) — **the pill you just clicked was precisely the one with animation off.** Second finding: the "only visible bubbles" mechanism **already existed** — `ChatView.tsx:273-291`'s IntersectionObserver maintains `.in-view` for the glass optimization, so no new observer was needed.
  Implemented via `grid-template-columns: 0fr→1fr` (interpolates to *intrinsic* width, so the active pill keeps its uncapped shrink-and-ellipsis) + `bubble-switch-in/out` keyframes on `.timeline-entry.in-view` only. Panes stay mounted and stacked, so the outgoing one is held 120ms to finish its exit while incoming bubbles wait out an equal `animation-delay` — sequential, not the crossfade Destin rejected. Gated on `prefers-reduced-motion` + `reducedEffects` (the strip had no gate at all before). Desktop-only by nature: Android's `forceSingle` remounts its single always-named pill, so there's no before-state and no expansion to show.
  **Outstanding before merge** (full detail: `docs/active/handoffs/2026-07-20-session-switch-animation-handoff.md`): (1) the 120ms exit delays the incoming conversation and needs a lived-with verdict — deleting it is a one-line change; (2) it also fires on Ctrl+\` (chat↔terminal), because a ChatView's `sessionId` never changes so `visible` is the only available edge — suppressing needs a new signal from `App`; (3) **no test coverage** — nothing renders `SessionStrip` or `ChatView`, only `packSessions`' 9 pure cases are pinned, so decide whether the `.in-view`-only rule (the load-bearing perf property) earns a pinning test; (4) drag-reorder interaction with the `activeSwap` window untested; (5) narrow-viewport + remote unverified.

- [x] "Send Anyway" override on the pending-prompt send block `feature` `#renderer` (added 2026-07-16; shipped 2026-07-17, youcoded PR #165 merge `49bcf49b`)
  Carried out of the fixed "stale toast blocks send" bug (Destin's ask, recorded 2026-07-16): when the send gate claims Claude is waiting on a prompt, a human who can see there's no live prompt should be able to force the send rather than being locked out. Needs design care rather than a bare bypass — a forced send while an Ink menu IS live writes `text + \r` into the menu and can silently select an option; the affordance should probably interrupt/dismiss the pending state first or route through terminal view with a warning. **SHIPPED as ESC-first (Destin chose this over just-unlock):** a refused typed send now surfaces a "Send anyway" button on the toast (App `ToastState` gained an optional `{message, action}` form; string form preserved so the ~8 plain callers are untouched). Clicking it presses ESC in the PTY first — closes any genuinely-live Ink menu, harmless no-op on an idle bar, so it can NEVER answer a real menu — then re-sends past the gate via a `force` flag threaded through InputBar `send`/`sendMessage`. Scoped to the typed-message path (InputBar); programmatic sends (`/model`, `/sync`) keep the plain toast; the minimal touch bar (no handler) falls back to the plain toast. NOT unit-pinned — no InputBar test harness exists — typecheck-verified only; flagged to Destin for a visual eyeball on the next build. Known trade-off he accepted: clicking it while a REAL menu is up cancels that menu (the safe failure).

- [x] Chat view momentum ("flick") scrolling `feature` `#renderer` (added 2026-07-17, shipped 2026-07-17 youcoded PR #169 merge `f71c5f39`)
  Destin's ask: after flicking the trackpad, the chat should keep scrolling and decelerate at a reasonable rate; today it stops the instant the finger lifts. SHIPPED with a "tap to catch the glide" refinement (any touch while coasting freezes it in place, incl. sub-pixel resting-finger jitter). Verified in a dev instance. Root cause: the existing custom wheel handler (`ChatView.tsx:344-393`) hijacks native scroll via non-passive `preventDefault` + `scrollBy(deltaY * multiplier)` with burst-acceleration but NO inertia — and on Linux libinput emits no momentum tail (unlike macOS's ~20-30-event tail the handler's comment assumes), so scrolling dies on finger-lift. Fix = velocity-tracked `requestAnimationFrame` glide-out with friction deceleration, fed by the wheel deltas; cancel the glide on new wheel input / pointerdown / arrow-key scroll / unmount, and stop at scroll boundaries. Gate momentum to trackpad pixel-mode (`deltaMode===0`) so discrete mouse-wheel stays snappy. Shared renderer code but inert on Android (no wheel events on touch) — must not disturb the atBottom auto-scroll effects (208-266) or `scrollToBottom`. Feel is timing-sensitive → tune constants then hand Destin a dev instance to eyeball the deceleration rate.
- [x] Right-click context menu (copy/paste/context-aware + Ask about this + file-pill menu) `feature` `#renderer` `#ui` (added 2026-07-17, shipped 2026-07-17 youcoded PR #169 merge `f71c5f39` — image sub-menu split to its own item below)
  Destin's ask: standard right-click behavior for copy/paste "and other standard left-click shit." Today there is NO context menu anywhere in the app (no `onContextMenu`, no Electron `Menu`, no `contextmenu` listener — verified 2026-07-17); right-click does nothing. Decision (Destin, 2026-07-17): CUSTOM THEMED React menu (not native Electron menu) — app fonts/colors/glass blur, follows active theme + wallpaper, cross-platform (desktop + Android long-press + remote browser). Built on the app's real `.layer-surface` popover, styled like `components/ui/Select.tsx` option rows (text-2xs, text-fg-2, hover bg-inset, accent-icon primary items). Context-AWARE scope per target: selected read-only text → Ask about this · Copy · Select all; code block → Ask about this · Copy code block · Copy · Select all; link → Open link · Copy link address · Copy · Select all; image → Ask about this · Copy image · Save image as… · Copy image address; editable input bar → Cut · Copy · Paste · Select all (Cut/Copy/Paste/Select-All disabled-not-hidden when unavailable). Reuse existing primitives: `navigator.clipboard`, `IPC.CLIPBOARD_SAVE_IMAGE`, `window.claude.shell.showItemInFolder`/`openPath` (preload.ts:677/681). **"Ask about this"** (text/code/image): drop the target into the input bar with a follow-up scaffold ("In an earlier message, you said: \"…\".\n\nThe user has a follow-up: ") and park the cursor — simple v1 = plain prompt text, no new plumbing (see separate roadmap item for the native version). **File-pill menu** (NEW, Destin 2026-07-17): right-click on the file pills/buttons in chat tool cards (`tool-views/ToolBody.tsx:151`, which already OPEN on left-click) → Open file · View in folder · Copy file name · Copy as path. Kept on right-click to preserve left-click open; confirm with Destin if he wants it on left-click instead. Mockup approved (artifact 2a6ebbe2 rev 2; sparkle icon chosen for Ask-about-this). **IMPLEMENTED (pending Destin's dev review), branch `feat/chat-flick-scroll`:** new `components/context-menu/` module — `ContextMenuHost` (one document-level capture `contextmenu` listener, mounted in App.tsx by RemoteSnapshotExporter), `build-menu.ts` (pure DOM/selection → entries, scoped to `.chat-scroll` + `.input-bar-textarea` — later extended to `[data-artifact-viewer]` + `.artifact-edit-textarea`, see the artifact-viewer item below), `ContextMenu.tsx` (portalled `.layer-surface` L4 popover, edge-flip, arrow-key nav, Esc/click-outside/scroll dismiss), `menu-icons.tsx`, `clipboard.ts` (secure-context writeText + execCommand fallback for insecure remote). Ask-about-this reuses a new `youcoded:compose-insert` window CustomEvent (InputBar prepends scaffold + caret). File pills got `data-file-path` (absolute `fp`) in ToolBody.tsx; desktop-gated Open/Reveal via existing shell IPC. **DEFERRED — image sub-menu (Copy image / Save image / Copy address):** chat/artifact images are ephemeral `blob:` URLs and there is NO copy-image-to-clipboard or save-image-to-disk IPC (only `clipboard:save-image` which READS the OS clipboard). Doing it right needs 2 new main IPC handlers + bytes plumbing (`artifacts.readBinary`) + `data-artifact-path` on ImageView/ArtifactThumbnail imgs + 3-surface parity + won't work on Android/remote — split out below. Typecheck clean; not unit-pinned (no host for these renderer interactions) — verified by dev review.
- [x] Agent-shell Bash CWD — scoped persistence (Claude Code's model) `feature` `#native-runtime` `#harness` (added 2026-07-17, shipped 2026-07-18 — youcoded PR #174)
  Today every Bash tool call is a **fresh process** started at the session cwd: `cd` in one call does NOT carry to the next, and nothing tells the model that — verified 2026-07-17 (PID changed `1042732 → 1042910` across two calls). Observed cost in one session: ~6 failed tool calls (`node_modules/.bin/vitest` "not found", `git add` "pathspec did not match") before the model diagnosed that CWD simply never persists. This is a known, contested design point in agentic harnesses — Claude Code issues #42837, #35058, #28228, #22023, #37659 all debate it, and the #1 complaint across them is *silent* behavior (the model can't tell which cwd model it's in and guesses wrong). Three models in the wild: persistent process (Anthropic API Bash tool), scoped persistence (Claude Code CLI default — cwd persists only within approved dirs, `cd` outside reverted with a `Shell cwd was reset to …` notice), and stateless/always-reset (many CI harnesses). YouCoded is a fourth, undocumented case (fresh process, never persists, no notice). **Destin's call (2026-07-17): adopt scoped persistence.** Concretely: track cwd in the harness and pass it as `cwd:` to each spawned command, updating it when the command changes dir (the robust detection trick is appending `; printf '\n__YC_CWD__%s' "$PWD"` and parsing the sentinel back out of stdout); scope it to the session root (`ctx.cwd`) — a `cd` resolving outside is reverted + a reset notice appended; persist only cwd, not env (match Claude Code); update the Bash tool description to state the rules plainly. Cheapest 80% fallback if full persistence is deferred: keep the stateless process but emit the reset notice + an explicit tool-description line ("each Bash call starts fresh at the workspace root; use absolute paths or `cd X && …` in one command"). Companion bug half (Grep `spawn ENOTDIR`) — PR #172's `cwd:` fix was a MISDIAGNOSIS: the bug kept reproducing in the packaged app. Real root cause found + shipped 2026-07-20 — youcoded PR #194: `@vscode/ripgrep`'s `rgPath` resolves INSIDE `app.asar` (a file), so `spawn()` of that command path throws `ENOTDIR` SYNCHRONOUSLY (before the 'error' handler can attach); the fix rewrites rgPath to `app.asar.unpacked` (mirroring the pty-worker pattern) + try/catches the sync throw in both Grep and Bash. Full investigation + research: `docs/archive/investigations/2026-07-17-agent-shell-cwd-and-grep-enotdir.md`.
- [x] Right-click menu — artifact viewer "Ask about this" (file + line-number reference) `feature` `#renderer` `#ui` (added 2026-07-19, shipped 2026-07-19 youcoded PR #186 merge `a0478dea`)
  Destin's ask (2026-07-19): the same "Ask about this" affordance the chat right-click menu has, but for text documents in the artifact viewer, with a scaffold naming the file and the referenced lines — `The user is referencing lines 12-18 from "docs/notes.md". Respond to the following prompt accordingly:` then the caret. Scope decisions (Destin, 2026-07-19): **line numbers with quote fallback** (not quote-only), and **both** CodeView and MarkdownView (all plain-text-backed artifacts). Implementation: `buildContextMenu` grew a `[data-artifact-viewer]` branch checked BEFORE the `.chat-scroll` gate (the viewer lives outside chat); CodeView + MarkdownView tag their content container with `data-artifact-viewer` / `data-doc-path` / `data-artifact-source`. `data-artifact-source` is `raw` when the `<pre>` is a verbatim copy of the file (code files, `.txt`) and `rendered` for parsed markdown — line numbers are only computed for `raw`, because rendered markdown prose has no 1:1 source mapping, and `rendered` always falls back to a quoted excerpt. Line lookup is first-occurrence `indexOf` over the `<pre>`'s **`textContent`** (NOT `innerText`: innerText is layout-dependent, would count soft-wrap breaks as source newlines on the `whitespace-pre-wrap` `.txt` `<pre>`, forces a reflow, and isn't implemented by jsdom). Reuses the existing `youcoded:compose-insert` CustomEvent — no new plumbing. Two deliberate behaviors: **no selection → no "Ask about this"** (unlike chat's whole-bubble fallback, since falling back to a whole file would paste the entire document), and the path cited is project-relative (reads better in a prompt than an absolute path). Also fixed in passing: the artifact **edit-mode textarea** now gets the Cut/Copy/Paste menu via a new `.artifact-edit-textarea` class — Electron ships no default context menu, so right-click there previously did nothing at all. Pinned by `desktop/src/renderer/components/context-menu/build-menu.test.tsx` (6 tests — the module had no test file before this). **Note for the image sub-menu item below:** this feature uses `data-doc-path`, deliberately NOT `data-artifact-path`, which stays reserved for that item's absolute-path-on-`<img>` tagging.
- [ ] Right-click menu — image sub-menu (Copy image / Save image as… / Copy address) `feature` `#renderer` `#ui` (added 2026-07-17)
  Split out of the right-click context menu above (which shipped for text/code/link/file-pill/composer but NOT images). Approved in the mockup but deferred because it needs real backend work: chat + artifact images render as ephemeral `blob:` URLs (see ImageView.tsx / ArtifactThumbnail.tsx — file:// is deliberately avoided; bytes come via `window.claude.artifacts.readBinary(absolutePath)`), and there is NO IPC to copy an image to the clipboard or save one to disk — the only image IPC is `clipboard:save-image`, which READS the OS clipboard. To do it: (1) add `image:copy-to-clipboard(absPath)` (main: `clipboard.writeImage(nativeImage.createFromPath)`) and `image:save-as(absPath)` (main: save dialog + copy) with full 3-surface parity (preload / remote-shim no-op / SessionService.kt) + `ipc-channels.test.ts`; (2) tag chat image elements with `data-artifact-path` (absolute) in ImageView + ArtifactThumbnail so the menu can recover the path; (3) "Copy image address" only makes sense for http(s)/data markdown images (blob URLs are useless) — show conditionally. Desktop-only (Android/remote can't save/copy binary via shell/dialog). Also "Ask about this" on an image (re-attach the image to the composer) belongs here — needs the file path + InputBar `addFiles`, and markdown http images aren't attachable.
- [ ] Right-click menu — touch/long-press support on Android `feature` `#renderer` `#ui` `#android` (added 2026-07-19)
  The context menu (chat + artifact viewer + composer) has **no touch handling at all** — verified 2026-07-19: no `touchstart`/`onTouchStart`/`longPress` anywhere in `desktop/src/renderer/components/context-menu/`, and the sole trigger is the document-level capture `contextmenu` listener in `ContextMenuHost.tsx`. The original menu spec (item above) named "Android long-press" as in-scope and `build-menu.ts` already gates the desktop-only shell actions behind `isDesktop()`, so it was *designed* for touch — it just was never wired or dogfooded. **Status is genuinely unknown, not known-broken:** Chromium synthesizes a `contextmenu` event from a long-press, so the menu may already fire in the Android WebView. First step is a dogfood pass on a debug APK, NOT code. Four things to check, each of which could need real work: (1) **selection timing** — long-press is also how Android *starts* a text selection, so at `contextmenu` time the selection is usually empty, which means "Ask about this" hides itself in the artifact viewer and falls back to whole-bubble in chat; the useful gesture is probably long-press *after* selecting, or a selection-toolbar action instead; (2) **`preventDefault()` suppresses the native selection handles and Android's own copy/paste bar** — so a naive port could make selecting text *harder*, trading a working native affordance for ours; (3) **positioning** — `e.clientX/clientY` works, but a menu under the user's thumb needs offsetting the way the buddy floater does; (4) **dismissal + hit targets** — Esc/click-outside/hover styling assume pointer + keyboard, and the L4 popover rows are sized for a cursor. Not a regression from the artifact-viewer work (youcoded#186) — the limitation predates it and applies to the whole menu equally.
- [x] **Artifact pane → credible code editor (Tier 1 workstream — one PR)** `feature` `#renderer` `#ui` `#artifacts` (added 2026-07-20, shipped 2026-07-22 youcoded PR #200 merge `1cf9cbf2` — items 1/2/3/5 + review fixes + drawer icon refresh; item 4 search split to the follow-up below)
  Destin's ask (2026-07-20): make YouCoded useful for real developer work, not just non-developers, starting with how code is edited/managed in the artifact viewer. Five changes that are cheap because the substrate already exists; scoped as ONE workstream/PR because they all land in the same three files and splitting them would mean three rounds of the same regression testing. **Positioning caveat, decided before building:** `docs/active/specs/2026-07-09-platform-vision-roadmap.md:139` positions the app as "the open, personal Cowork" with non-developer accessibility as core identity, and its competitive matrix (L112–139) has NO row for an editor, file tree, or diff view — the named gaps are multi-model freedom, approval inbox, memory. So this is a deliberate direction change, not a gap-fill. The framing chosen is **not** IDE parity (unwinnable against Cursor on its own turf) but *"I can trust and steer what the agent did to my code"* — review, diff, navigate, small surgical edits. That keeps the non-developer audience intact; every item below is justified against it, and anything that only serves IDE parity belongs in Tier 2/3 items below, not here.
  **(1) Unlock code editing.** Editability is gated by a hard-coded extension allowlist at `artifact-views/ActiveArtifactView.tsx:48` (`md | markdown | txt`) — that one line is the ONLY thing blocking it. The save path underneath already handles arbitrary paths: `window.claude.artifacts.save` → `preload.ts:1234` → `ipc-handlers.ts:3094`, atomic `.tmp`+rename (`:3113-3114`), sidecar version event, `artifacts:changed` broadcast, path-traversal guard at `:3135-3138`. Conflict handling already exists too (`ActiveArtifactView.tsx:76-88` + banner at `:155-179`). Invert the allowlist to a binary-file denylist rather than enumerating code extensions. Today a developer literally cannot fix a typo in a `.ts` file without leaving the app.
  **(2) Replace CodeView with CodeMirror 6.** `artifact-views/CodeView.tsx` is 21 lines and does not render code — it wraps the file in a markdown fence and hands it to the chat renderer (`:9-11`), so highlighting goes through a full react-markdown + rehype-highlight parse per render (`MarkdownContent.tsx:15,234`). No line numbers, no gutter, no folding, and it re-parses the whole document on every keystroke once (1) lands. **CM6 over Monaco specifically:** Android runs this exact React bundle in a WebView (`WebViewHost.kt:160`), and Monaco is heavy, worker-dependent, and touch-hostile; CM6 is ~150KB tree-shaken and works on touch. Hard constraint for whatever ships: it must load from the asset origin and go through `window.claude.artifacts.*` — anything Node-only or `file://`-dependent breaks Android (see `useArtifactBytes.ts:20-23`). The single biggest perceived-quality jump on this list.
  **(3) Use the `diff` library already in package.json.** `diff@^9.0.0` + `@types/diff` are declared but **nothing in the renderer imports them** — both diff renderers are hand-rolled. `ToolBody.tsx` prefers Claude Code's `structuredPatch` (`:331-343`) but falls back to its own O(m·n) LCS (`diffLines()` at `:260-289`); and the edit-conflict "diff" at `ActiveArtifactView.tsx:181-192` isn't a diff at all, just two `<pre>` columns side by side (the comment at `:154` already flags that a real diff library was deferred). Swap in jsdiff and unify both call sites on one component. Keep `DIFF_PREVIEW_LINES` capping behavior.
  **(4) Cross-file content search.** `@vscode/ripgrep@^1.18.0` is already a dependency but is wired ONLY to the agent's Grep tool (`main/harness/tools/grep.ts:3,35`) — no renderer caller. The UI can currently only filter by *filename*: `FilesTab.tsx:226-227` matches file names and explicitly not folder names, `SessionDrawer.tsx:360-370` is a name search, and `ContentFindBar.tsx` is within-the-open-document only. One IPC channel exposing the existing binary gets project-wide content search nearly free. Needs 3-surface parity (preload / remote-shim / `SessionService.kt`) per the usual rule — and note Android has no ripgrep binary, so it needs a Kotlin-side fallback or an honest unsupported notice.
  **(5) Watch the project directory.** There is no watcher on the project dir — `artifacts:changed` is push-broadcast from write sites (`ipc-handlers.ts:2879,2899,2915,3125,3142,3201,3225`), so an external change (a `git checkout`, an edit in another editor, a rebase) leaves the pane showing stale content with no indication. `chokidar@^4.0.3` is already a dep and already used this way for themes (`main/theme-watcher.ts:53`) and sync-spaces (`main/sync-spaces/engine.ts:83`) — point it at the project root and reuse the existing broadcast. Watch out for the debounce/cost profile on large repos (respect the existing cap-truncation in `project-file-discovery.ts`) and for a save-triggers-watcher-triggers-reload loop against (1)'s edit buffer.
  **SPEC: `docs/archive/specs/2026-07-20-artifact-pane-code-editor.md`** (2026-07-20) — read §2 before implementing. Speccing surfaced four pre-existing defects the 3-extension allowlist has been masking, and the fixes are IN scope, not follow-ups: (a) **the conflict banner is unreachable dead code** — `ActiveArtifactView.tsx:80` filters `evt.by === 'agent'` but every emitter in `ipc-handlers.ts` hardcodes `by: 'user'`, and with no filesystem watcher an agent CLI write emits nothing at all, so the "Claude also edited this file" safety net has never fired; (b) **no dirty tracking anywhere** — switching artifacts silently discards the draft, and the `:71-73` force-exit exists to close a window where a save during the `content === null` fetch transient would truncate a file to empty (the highest-risk regression in the work); (c) **no size cap on `artifacts:get`** — a naked `readFile(utf8)` with no stat/sniff/cap, so a 5MB file blocks the main thread twice over; (d) **save is unconditional last-write-wins** with no mtime/hash token and a console-only error path. Net effect: the watcher (item 5) is the PREREQUISITE for unlocking editing (item 1), not an independent nice-to-have. Four decisions taken 2026-07-20 (spec §3): **D1** syntax colors DERIVED from existing tokens (`--accent`/`--fg-2`/`--link`/`--code`) the way `theme-engine.ts:239-245` already derives `--code`, rather than a new per-theme token family — no `wecoded-themes` authoring story or back-fill; **D2** cross-file search is desktop-only with the existing `remote-unsupported.ts` notice + a ~3-line Kotlin stub (the `project:*` precedent), not a divergent Kotlin reimplementation; **D3** unsaved changes get a Save/Discard/Cancel prompt, NOT auto-save (which would write real source files with no explicit action and race the agent); **D4** any text file is editable with a binary denylist, matching `project-file-discovery.ts:18-22`'s deliberate no-allowlist stance — this also fixes CodeView's 9-extension gap (`rs`/`go`/`java`/`sh`/`kt`/`sql`/`toml`/`c`/`cpp` currently fall to BinaryFallback) in the same change. Spec §9 gives the in-PR sequencing (watcher first, CM6 late, search cuttable to a follow-up). Expect `build-menu.test.tsx` to go red — the right-click menu keys off `<pre>`/`data-artifact-source` and `.artifact-edit-textarea`, and a CM6 editor is neither; re-point the branches, don't weaken the tests.
  **AMENDED 2026-07-20 (consequence review — spec §12). D5 RESOLVED 2026-07-22 — no longer blocked; implementation plan: `docs/archive/plans/2026-07-22-artifact-pane-code-editor-implementation.md`.** A review pass found the workstream as first specced ships a security regression, and that four sections of the spec were wrong (all corrected in place, marked `CORRECTED 2026-07-20`). The blocking finding: **D4 removes the only barrier in front of arbitrary file writes.** `artifacts:get`/`save` have a path-traversal check and NOTHING else (`ipc-handlers.ts:3019-3027`, `:3134-3139`) — only `read-binary` carries a sensitive-path deny-list (`read-binary-access.ts:62-71`, which blocks `.ssh`/`.aws`/`.env*` etc). So one channel refuses to READ `.env` while another will happily OVERWRITE it. `artifactId` is a raw relative path and the discovery pass's dot-dir skip is a listing filter the IPC never consults, so lifting the renderer gate exposes `.git/hooks/pre-commit` (code execution on next commit), `.claude/settings.json` + `.claude/hooks/*` (hook config IS command execution), `.env`, `CLAUDE.md`, and `.youcoded/artifacts.json` — the last of which escalates OUT of the project root, because the tracked save branch writes `artifact.absolutePath!` with no traversal check at all (`:3110-3112`). Not a regression the workstream introduces (main never enforced anything; the renderer allowlist was load-bearing security by accident) but it hands the UI a live path to all of it. **The predicate must live in MAIN, not the renderer** — the first draft put it in `ActiveArtifactView.tsx` and called it "the security boundary," which was exactly backwards. **NEW DECISION D5 NEEDED before implementing:** per-path policy for `.git/` `.claude/` `.youcoded/` `.env*` `CLAUDE.md` — not editable / editable-behind-confirm / free. *(Resolved 2026-07-22 after verifying all §12.1 claims against master: `.git/`, `.youcoded/`, and the read-binary sensitive set minus dotenv are never editable, checked in MAIN against the resolved absolute path; `.claude/` and `.env*`/`.envrc` editable behind a confirm with a main-required `confirmed` flag; `CLAUDE.md` free. Table + refusal UX in the plan §1, spec §3/D5 updated.)* Other corrections: **(§5.3)** the draft said to expect `build-menu.test.tsx` to go red — the opposite is true and it is the dangerous direction, the test hand-builds a synthetic `<pre>` and never mounts CodeView (`build-menu.test.tsx:10-19`) so it stays GREEN while the feature breaks; and CM6 virtualization makes the `indexOf`-over-`textContent` line-number technique emit *plausible wrong* citations (a selection at line 800 reports "line 41") into prompt scaffolds, so it must move to `view.state.doc.lineAt()` and the test must be rebuilt around the real component — §5 is meaningfully larger than a viewer swap. **(§8.2)** a filesystem watcher CANNOT emit `by: 'agent'` — a `git checkout`/`npm install`/formatter would all be labeled "Claude also edited this file", a guessed cause in a user-facing string; emit `by: 'external'` and reword the banner + its three actions. **(§8.5)** there is no per-window active project — `projectRoot` derives per-session from `session.cwd` (`App.tsx:1328`), sessions detach freely across an uncapped number of windows, and one window can show two projects at once; the watcher belongs in main, refcounted by `projectRoot`, following the `topicWatchers` shape (`ipc-handlers.ts:2253`) and its documented leak trap (`:2314-2315`), NOT per-window and NOT leader-owned. Also newly captured: **§2.4's last-write-wins defect was identified in the first draft and then never scheduled a fix** (now §9 step 2 — round-trip an mtime/hash through get→save, or D3's prompt sits on top of a save that still silently clobbers the agent's work, which is exactly what "Keep mine" does by design); capture-phase key handlers guard on `tagName === 'TEXTAREA'` and won't recognize CM6's contenteditable, so **Shift+Space would cycle the model and Shift+Tab would reach the PTY** from inside the editor (`App.tsx:1806-1817`, `:2419-2429`); Android soft-keyboard support is NEW work not preservation (the artifact pane has zero `--vvp-offset` consumers today, and `adjustNothing` + `overlays-content` is precisely the contract CM6's `scrollIntoView` assumptions break against); and search has nowhere to land until a jump-to-line concept exists (zero hits for `scrollIntoView`/`gotoLine` in `artifact-views/`; selection is keyed by artifactId not path, but `FilepathToken.tsx:84-130` already implements the path→id upsert resolution to reuse). Two findings deliberately NOT scheduled in this workstream and filed separately below: the write-guard bypass and unbounded sidecar growth.
  **STATUS 2026-07-22: steps 1–5 implemented — youcoded PR #200 open** (watcher, D5 boundary + Kotlin mirror, jsdiff, D4 unlock + dirty tracking, CM6). Cross-file search (item 4) cut at the plan's cut line to a follow-up PR; md/txt kept the textarea edit path. Kotlin tests gated on CI (local JDK 26 incompatible). Destin's eyeball items: editor feel, syntax colors (Crème + a wallpaper theme), diff readability, confirm wording, Android device pass.
  **Verification note:** per the live-app-safety rule this is dev-instance work (`bash scripts/run-dev.sh`), and per CLAUDE.md's 2026-07-16 lesson the final "does the editor feel right" pass is Destin's eyeball, not a scripted CDP rig. DOM-level assertions (line numbers present, save fires, diff rows correct) should still be unit-pinned — `build-menu.test.tsx` is the precedent for testing this area.

- [x] Cross-file content search (cut from the Tier 1 code-editor PR) `feature` `#renderer` `#artifacts` (added 2026-07-22, shipped 2026-07-22 youcoded PR #205 merge `813a6c83`)
  Shipped per the archived plan step 6 + in-review design decisions (Destin, 2026-07-22): NO name/contents toggle — one search box, two labeled sections ("Matches by file name (N)" cards, then "Matches by file contents (N)"); content hits GROUPED per file, groups collapsible and collapsed by default, sorted by match count desc with the display cap keeping whole groups; no prose empty state in search mode (the (0) counts carry it). Engine: `artifacts:search-content` over bundled ripgrep `--json`, literal `-F -i` query, caps 20/file + 200 total + 1MB stdout + 5s kill, desktop-only (Kotlin stub, remote unsupported → names-only). Jump-to-hit via `revealLine(n)` on ActiveArtifactHandle (CM6 dispatch, retries across the lazy-chunk mount) — the reusable open-at-line primitive the future git surface needs. Real-ripgrep e2e test pins literal/case/exclusion/truncation behavior.
- [ ] HTML preview: chase url() refs inside inlined CSS `feature` `#renderer` `#artifacts` (added 2026-07-22)
  `html-inline-assets.ts` inlines relative stylesheets/scripts/images but deliberately does not resolve url(...) refs INSIDE fetched CSS (fonts/background images) — v1 cut, noted in the module header.
- [x] Artifact pane: real loading state instead of the "no longer on disk" flash `bug` `#renderer` `#artifacts` (added 2026-07-22, **FIXED 2026-08-12 — youcoded PR #303, merge `1b69194e`**: `content === null` split into a four-state `ArtifactContentState` (loading/ready/missing/error — the fourth mandated by the no-misleading-errors rule: read errors now surface accurately instead of as "no longer on disk"), owned by a new shared `useArtifactContent` hook that replaced two near-duplicate host effects; loading renders the house `LoadingState` primitive. The review-round delta also fixed a recreated-file stuck-notice regression and pinned the stale-resolve race. **Destin: eyeball the brief "Loading file…" spinner on artifact open** — the alternative (blank pane during the blip) is a two-line change if you prefer it. Follow-up filed: binary-sniffed text-extension files render a blank pane.)
  Viewers show "This file is no longer on disk." during the content-fetch transient (content===null covers both loading AND orphan). Pre-existing (MarkdownView always did it), more visible now that every file type opens in the pane. Needs an explicit loading vs orphan signal from the hosts.
- [ ] Editor tabs — multiple open files in the artifact pane `feature` `#renderer` `#ui` `#artifacts` (added 2026-07-20)
  Both artifact hosts are strictly single-file today: `SessionDrawer.tsx:334` holds one `activeArtifactId`, and `FilesTab.tsx:641-659` opens one detail overlay at a time. Developers work across files constantly, and this is the most-missed thing after syntax highlighting. Needs open-file state (probably in `ArtifactContext`), keyboard nav, close/reorder, and a narrow-mode story — the drawer already collapses its 210px list column (`SessionDrawer.tsx:585-592`) and becomes stack navigation on narrow, so tabs need to degrade the same way rather than fight it. Depends on the Tier 1 workstream above (a tab bar over a read-only markdown-fence viewer isn't worth much).

- [ ] Persistent file tree for the artifact pane `feature` `#renderer` `#ui` `#artifacts` (added 2026-07-20)
  What exists today is a *synthetic* one-level-at-a-time folder browser, not a tree: `listDir()` at `FilesTab.tsx:84` buckets flat artifact paths into folder cards, with `currentDir` state at `:181` ('' = root). There is no expandable tree widget and no sidebar tree anywhere. A real tree also needs a directory-listing IPC channel that does not exist — the `artifacts:*` surface (`main/artifacts/ipc-channels.ts:2-44`) has `list-session`/`list-project`/`list-all-files` but no generic `fs:list`, and `list-all-files` is a cached, cap-truncated discovery pass (`project-file-discovery.ts`), not a live directory read. Pairs naturally with the project-dir watcher from Tier 1 item (5).

- [x] Git surface in-app — diff vs HEAD, stage, commit, branch `feature` `#renderer` `#ui` `#git` (added 2026-07-20; SHIPPED 2026-07-23 — youcoded PR #213 merged to master.
