Per-file review in the SessionDrawer: footer `+N −N` entry, pushed review view with uncommitted card + rename-following commit timeline, real-index stage/unstage, commit composer, revert-with-confirm. Desktop-only; Android/remote degrade to `not-implemented-on-mobile`. Branch ops + push/PR are the separate phase-2 entry below. Spec/plan archived to `docs/archive/{specs,plans}/2026-07-22-git-surface.md` — the spec's §2 UI is superseded in places by Destin's live-testing revisions, recorded in its "Live-iteration amendments" section)
  **The highest-value developer feature on this list and the real differentiator** — reviewing what the agent just did is the core anxiety of using an agent-first tool, and right now the only in-app view of a change is the per-tool-call diff inside a chat card (`ToolBody.tsx:318`). There is no way to see the cumulative working-tree diff, no staging, no commit, no branch awareness. Git currently appears in this codebase only as *plumbing* — the sync transport (`main/sync-spaces/git-transport.ts`) — and never as a user-facing surface. Sequencing note: `ROADMAP.md:43` already plans to drop the hand-installed `gh` dependency in favor of direct GitHub REST, which is adjacent work and should probably be settled first so this doesn't build on a dependency that's being removed. Recommended to jump the queue ahead of tabs and the file tree once Tier 1 ships.

- [ ] Git surface phase 2 — branch ops, push/PR creation, repo-wide review, hunk-level staging `feature` `#git` `#renderer` (added 2026-07-22)
  The spec's §8 non-goals for the shipped per-file MVP (`feat/git-surface`), deferred deliberately. Also from the MVP's final review: `-z`-format parsing for paths containing literal quotes/newlines (quotepath residual — must also cover the `--numstat` log parsing, whose human-readable rename-arrow syntax additionally misreads a real filename containing a literal ` => ` as a rename; `-z` delivers rename paths as separate NUL fields and fixes both — see the accepted-ambiguity comment in `porcelain.ts`), a `refresh()` error state in GitReviewView, and prose polish for raw error codes (`unknown-project-root`, `path-outside-project`).

- [x] Git surface: untracked files can't be staged from the review card — UX decision needed `task` `#git` `#ui` (added 2026-07-22; DECIDED + FIXED 2026-07-23 — Destin chose the "legible mirror" during live testing: "Include in commit" checkbox on EVERY uncommitted card incl. brand-new files, empty-cart hint under the commit button, "Open file ↗" removed, discard relabeled "Revert Changes…" with dialog confirm "Move to Trash"/"Revert Changes". youcoded PR #213 commit `2301cf34`; supersedes the original mockup's hidden-checkbox decision)

- [ ] Git surface: profiling checkpoint before Android or multi-window `task` `#git` `#perf` (added 2026-07-22)
  `git:file-status` spawns up to 3 git processes per refresh and refreshes on every `artifacts:changed` for the project AND every `git:changed` from ANY repo (filtering needs a renderer-side projectRoot→repoRoot map that doesn't exist yet). Plan accepted "correctness first; profile before optimizing" — profile before anything multiplies subscribers. Also: `locateOrFail` extraction in git-service.ts to dedupe the outside/!loc split across its sites (re-counted 2026-08-12: **five**, not three — `git-service.ts:100/141/220/255/296`).

- [ ] Go-to-definition / find-references without a full LSP `feature` `#renderer` `#artifacts` (added 2026-07-20)
  Symbol navigation covers most of what developers actually reach for, and does NOT require a language server: tree-sitter, or even ctags-grade indexing, gets ~80% of the value at a fraction of the cost and — unlike an LSP — runs inside the Android WebView, so it preserves desktop/Android UI parity. Depends on the CM6 editor from Tier 1 item (2) for the click targets and jump affordance. Explicitly the cheap alternative to the LSP idea filed under Someday.

- [ ] "Ask about this" — native reference UX `feature` `#renderer` `#ui` (added 2026-07-17, **scoped + approved 2026-07-26** — spec `docs/active/specs/2026-07-26-ask-claude-reference-ux-design.md`, plan `docs/active/plans/2026-07-26-ask-claude-reference-ux.md`, mockup artifact `bed5f7ea`; **BUILT BUT PARKED 2026-07-28 — branch `feat/ask-claude-reference-ux`, worktree `worktrees/ask-reference`, 27 commits, +5685/-173, youcoded PR #263 open as a DRAFT titled "DRAFT / INCOMPLETE". All nine plan tasks landed with ~50 tests. Destin's verdict that day: "the 'ask about this' ui was decent for messages, but janky af for the artifact viewer. needs some additional work before integrating." The PR body separately warns a reviewer to expect substantial rework or a REWRITE of the overlay layer. Resumption starts with a product decision, not a bug queue. Drift: 245 renderer commits on master since; only 2 textual conflicts but three master changes land on the unsettled overlay half — the `[data-reduced-effects]` convention + guard test (`b3239f6f`), the perpetual-animation frame-budget policy (`af8a06bb`, `4b7dc41a`), and the Phase A accent/bubble tokens (PR #327).**)
  Follow-up to the simple "Ask about this" that ships with the right-click menu above (which just prefills raw prompt text into the input bar). Destin flagged 2026-07-17 he wants a more native treatment. **Brainstormed and designed 2026-07-26.** The reference becomes state (`PendingReference`) instead of composer text: right-clicking a chat message **lifts it out of the flow to screen centre** over a window-wide scrim with an accent outline that traces around it, held until send/cancel, while the composer stays live above the scrim with placeholder `Ask Claude about "…"`; the scaffold is assembled at send time and never shown. Artifact-panel selections get the same treatment minus the travel (traced in place, window still dims). Key finding that drove the design: **scroll-to-center is impossible for the newest message** (it sits directly above the composer with no scroll room), so the lift is a portalled FLIP clone, not a scroll. Destin's decisions 2026-07-26: trace the *selection* when there is one (whole-card outline as fallback); dim the **whole window**, not just the pane, "so it's obvious what is being highlighted"; streaming messages get a **disabled+tooltip** menu row, not a removed one. Retires the `youcoded:compose-insert` CustomEvent entirely (verified 1 producer + 1 consumer + 3 test sites). **Deliberately NOT** a structured transcript reply-to relationship — the send payload stays prepended prompt text, so nothing about the transcript format or native-runtime protocol changes (this is why the item lost its `#native-runtime` tag). Android/touch and narrow-viewport are out of scope — blocked on the existing context-menu touch item below.

- [x] `sync-spaces-engine` macOS CI flake — `fs.watch` test still blows the 5s vitest timeout `bug` `#ci` `#sync` (added 2026-07-19)
  **SHIPPED 2026-07-22 — youcoded PR #198 (merge `79ac5802`). This entry's hypothesis was exactly right, and it was a partially-applied fix.** PR #180 replaced this file's six inline `{ timeout: 5000 }` literals with a named `WAIT_MS = 60_000`, but that knob bounds the `vi.waitFor` POLL, not the test. The file had no `vi.setConfig`, so it inherited vitest's default 5000ms `testTimeout` and every 60s wait was really capped at 5s — the ceiling #180 raised was unreachable. Its two sibling files got the setConfig in #163 (`sync-spaces-project-discovery.test.ts:23`, `sync-spaces-git-transport.test.ts:18`); this one was missed. Verified by probe: a file with no setConfig doing `vi.waitFor({timeout: 60_000})` dies with exactly `Test timed out in 5000ms` — the PR #181 macOS message — and with the setConfig added a 7s test in this file passes. **Two knobs are required per file: one bounds the poll, one bounds the test.**
  **Same gap found and closed in two more files** while sweeping: `subagent-watcher.test.ts` and `transcript-watcher.test.ts` both carried `WATCH_MS = 15_000` with no setConfig — i.e. PR #163 was partially applied the same way #180 was (poll ceilings raised in four files, test ceilings in only two). Those are precisely the files the `sync-warnings` entry above named as the family's remaining victims. A repo-wide sweep for "poll ceiling above the file's effective test ceiling" now returns nothing; `sync-spaces-service` and `conversations-service` also lack setConfig but use `vi.waitFor`'s 1000ms default, safely under the 5s budget. The five slowest remaining tests (`conversation-store` 6.0s, `secrets-store` 3.0s, `native-home` 3.0s, `update-installer` 2.0s, `sync-spaces-two-device` 1.0s) all already carry deliberate inline per-test timeouts of 10–30s and are correctly bounded by that other mechanism — do NOT "normalize" them to setConfig without adding one, the inline arg is currently their only knob.
  PR #180 ("one named 60s ceiling for the fs.watch integration waits + macOS CI") reduced but did NOT eliminate this. Hit again on youcoded PR #181: `tests/sync-spaces-engine.test.ts > "still syncs real lockfiles a user keeps in a project (Cargo.lock)"` failed macOS-only with `Test timed out in 5000ms`, then passed cleanly on a plain re-run with no code change. Likely cause: #180 raised the ceiling on the internal *waits* but the per-test vitest `testTimeout` is still 5000ms, so a slow macOS runner blows the test timeout before the wait budget matters. Fix is probably an explicit per-test timeout on the fs.watch integration cases (or a suite-level `testTimeout` for that file), not a longer wait. Costs a CI re-run every time it fires, and trains people to re-run red macOS builds without reading them — which is how a real failure gets merged.

- [x] UI consistency system — shared primitives + the 51-change migration `feature` `#ui` (added 2026-07-16, COMPLETE 2026-07-24)
  Full design settled with Destin 2026-07-16 across 8 interactive workbench sessions; every change approved by number (Session 8 approved in full 2026-07-16 after three render iterations). Scope: `components/ui/` primitives (Button 5 variants × 4 sizes incl. icon + CloseButton, Toggle 36×20 accent w/ bordered knob, TextInput/Textarea/Select with themed dropdown menus — no OS-rendered controls anywhere except the documented color-picker/title= exceptions, Checkbox/Radio, native accent-accent sliders (custom styling rejected — wiring fix only), Loading/Empty/ErrorState family incl. the two-action error card, AnchorTip, Toast, SegmentedTabs (transparent inactive), ProgressBar), card-surface + badge-tone unification, one screen layer (ProjectView z-8000 → z-40) + one exit affordance, named micro type scale (text-2xs/3xs/4xs over ~538 raw px sites), --link + --on-destructive derivation for community packs, creme.json contrast fix (real shipping bug), SessionDrawer adopts ProjectView's FileFilterPopover, games-lobby token migration (Accept = Button primary), coarse-pointer hit areas + hover:hover guards, BrailleSpinner on the model-loading strip, headerless Settings drawer (no title/✕; Esc + click-outside close; macOS traffic-light padding moves to the scroll body) + SettingsRow type/icon bump within the same card size. **Spec with exact class recipes, full ledger, native-control inventory, tranche plan + implementation risks: docs/archive/specs/2026-07-16-ui-consistency-design-spec.md** (artifact links inside). Implement tranche 0 (tokens + primitives + pinning tests + protection-cascade fix) first, then 8 surface-by-surface tranches, worktree + one commit per surface (SettingsRow playbook). Follow-on guardrails (ui-primitives doc, rule updates, audit-ui-tokens pass, ESLint) listed in spec §8, not yet approved. **Update 2026-07-17 — tranches 0+1 SHIPPED (youcoded PR #164, merge `31900a2f`):** tranche 0 = type tokens, `--link`/`--link-hover` + `--on-destructive` derivation, creme.json contrast fix + audit-reads-JSON + pinning test, the protection-cascade fix, and all 14 `components/ui/` primitives with 56 pinning tests; tranche 1 = buttons, changes 1–14. Five spec recipes were WRONG against the real code and shipped corrected — read **spec §10 (implementation log) BEFORE tranches 2–8**, not §1–§9: change 43's contrast arithmetic (white on #DD4444 is 4.213:1, not 4.7 — the stated rule would flip every danger button to near-black at lower contrast), §9.K's "add `.layer-surface .bg-destructive`" (would CREATE the hover bug, not fix it), change 7's pill className override (Tailwind resolves by CSS source order, so the pills silently rendered as rounded rectangles — `buttonClasses()` now merges), the Toggle knob offsets (17/1 with a constant border, not 18/2), and AnchorTip's capture-phase Esc (`useEscClose` is a LIFO stack that already does it, and gets Android hardware-back free). Remaining: tranches 2–8. **Update 2026-07-19 — the button triage §10.7 owed is DONE and the count was wrong:** not ~25–30 hand-rolled buttons but **~153 across ~50 files** (plus 23 icon-only closers and ~52 non-button surfaces that belong to SegmentedTabs/cards, not Button). Triaged into 8 mechanical patterns + 16 judgment calls + the CloseButton sweep = **changes 52–76, all decided by Destin 2026-07-19** — see **spec §11** for the full ledger with per-site recipes, and the workbench artifact <https://claude.ai/code/artifact/d9174f7b-139d-44e6-b009-c2da3e7d80c1>. Two decisions departed from the recommendation and are marked ▲ in §11.3 — do not "fix" them back: **61** keeps the permission prompt's inverted red ("Always allow" stays red even though red means "No" one row below — muscle memory on the app's most-clicked control wins), and **62** gives skip-permissions filled `danger`, accepting that it now looks identical to "Remove project". Also note **change 75**: five buttons ship `hover:bg-accent` over a `bg-accent` base and have no hover feedback today, so tranche 2 changes behavior, not just appearance — call it out in the PR. Still true from before: the `bg-accent text-on-accent` grep also matches the user chat bubble and theme swatches, which must NOT become Buttons. **Update 2026-07-19 — tranche 2's BUTTON half SHIPPED** (youcoded PR #181, merge `2bf29a44`; branch + worktree cleaned up): ~150 buttons across 55 files now go through the primitive, plus the change-76 CloseButton sweep (29 closers, 9 of which had no accessible name at all — now enforced by the type signature). Also found and fixed: **§10.7's "changes 1–14 are done" was false** (AccountSection/LocalModelsSection/SettingsPanel/ConnectGithubModal still held ~21 hand-rolled buttons, none of them in the 153 count — see spec §11.7); **decision 62 missed a third skip-permissions Create button** on the welcome form; and SyncPanel's confirm dialog had an unreachable `confirmColor='blue'` branch (one call site, passes "red") carrying one of the last hardcoded-blue buttons — deleted, not migrated. **All seven open items DECIDED 2026-07-19 (spec §11.8):** A/B/C/F/G shipped in `6bf5e7e2` (Add Device → secondary; ToolCard's AskUserQuestion pair → primary+ghost; the compact split button keeps its joined shape as a documented exception; the attachment ✕ keeps 16px but gains an accessible name; the send button migrates, keeping `bg-accent` because community packs style it through that class). **D and E changed shape and became new changes 77/78** — D is now a general pattern ("fields with a submit/copy put the button INSIDE the field, right-aligned", ~10 sites, needs a new `InputGroup` primitive with `focus-within` on the wrapper, ships WITH change 20 — spec §11.9), E becomes a `Select` under change 21 (spec §11.10). Also found: **Halftone Dimension's `custom_css` has a `.send-btn` rule that matches nothing in the renderer** — the send-button glow comes from its `.bg-accent` rule instead; a `wecoded-themes` fix. Formerly, plus two queued visual checks (the `panel-glass` nav chips and ThemeScreen's "Browse Theme Marketplace" lost their resting fills when decision 60 collapsed the filled-grey family to outline). Tranche 2's TOGGLE half (changes 15–21) is untouched and still open. **Update 2026-07-19 — tranche 2's INPUT half SHIPPED (youcoded PR #183), so TRANCHE 2 IS COMPLETE.** Changes 15–17, 19–21, 42, 77 across 51 files; 2668 tests green. Consumers after the sweep: Toggle 28, TextInput 31, Textarea 9, Select 10, InputGroup 11 across 10 files — before this tranche every one of those primitives had ZERO consumers (they shipped in tranche 0 and sat unused). **New primitive `InputGroup` (change 77)**: it could NOT be a className on TextInput, because plain FIELD puts the border AND `focus:border-accent` on the `<input>` — move the border to a wrapper and the bare input has no border to recolor, so the field would show no focus state at all; the wrapper takes `focus-within:border-accent`. `FIELD_SURFACE`/`FIELD_TEXT` were split out of FIELD so a bordered field and a grouped one can't drift apart. Pinned by tests. **Latent bug fixed en route:** `fieldClasses` CONCATENATED its className while `buttonClasses` has merged since tranche 0 — Tailwind resolves competing utilities by CSS SOURCE order, so a caller's `text-sm`/`px-4` was an accident of emission order, not an override. Identical mechanism to the tranche-0 pills-render-as-rectangles bug (spec §10.3); fields carried it unnoticed because they had no consumers. Now routes through `mergeClasses`, two tests pin it. **Behavior changed, not just appearance — mention on release:** ~20 toggles gained an accessible name (most were bare `<button>`s inside a `<label>`, which names nothing); four `<label>` wrappers became `<div>`+`aria-label` around Select triggers for the same reason; `PerformancePopup`'s GPU control announced as a switch while rendering a 16×16 SQUARE and is now a real toggle. **Spec §11.9 was wrong about three InputGroup sites** — QuickChips is a two-field form with a footer (`addCustom` needs BOTH fields), the add-provider submit sits below three fields, the Android add-device submit below four, and LocalModelsSection has NO endpoint field at all (InputGroup went to its model search, whose clear-X was already an inside-field action faked with `absolute`+`pr-8`). All verified in code, not assumed — same §11.7 lesson. Corrections in spec §13.4. **Found outside the sweep:** `index.tsx` (the remote login screen) still had a hardcoded `bg-blue-600` button and a gray-focus password field — the button half missed it because that sweep covered `components/`, not the renderer root; that was the last hardcoded blue. And `App.tsx:2843` held a THIRD copy of the skip-permissions warning string on a raw `#DD4444` (the toggle above it had been migrated, the text walked past) — same shape as change 62's missed third Create button. All four copies plus the info tooltip heading now ride the destructive token. **Deferred: change 78** (folder picker → Select) — `projectFolder` is a single string and there is no recents list anywhere in the app, so it needs a new settings key + IPC + cap + eviction policy. A feature, not a migration; the only tranche-2 item that would grow scope. Spec §11.10. **Residue:** two `Cancel` text buttons the BUTTON half missed (`QuickChips.tsx:374`, `RatingSubmitModal.tsx:345`) — both `text-[10px]` where the nearest primitive step is 11px, so left rather than silently resized; two hardcoded-blue spinners in SyncSetupWizard; and three queued eyeball checks (four `text-sm` fields became `text-xs` — a visible shrink on two marketplace search boxes; the `panel-glass` nav chips; a sync toggle's `cursor-wait`→`cursor-not-allowed` while provisioning). AccountSection's Save height mismatch is structurally resolved — those buttons now live inside the field's own wrapper. Implementation log: **spec §13**. Tranches 3–8 remain — **resume from `docs/archive/handoffs/2026-07-19-ui-consistency-tranche-3-handoff.md`**, which carries the open contrast decision, the queued eyeball checks, the residue list, and a warning that tranche 3's stated scope looks understated the same way tranches 1–2 were (greps suggest ~58 `bg-panel`+border sites and ~54 `bg-well` sites against changes 22/25's handful of named components — upper bounds, not counts; audit before implementing). Also verified 2026-07-19: **ESLint does not exist in this repo** (no config, no lint script), so §8's "ESLint guardrail" is adopt-from-zero, not add-a-rule — size it accordingly. **Update 2026-07-23 — tranches 3 + 5 (partial) COMMITTED to branch `feat/ui-consistency-tranche-3` (UNPUSHED, no PR yet — not merged to master):** **Tranche 3** (cards 22–25, commit `1f68a7f0`) + a new `.hover-lift` CSS guard that puts the card scale behind `@media (hover: hover)` — the guard didn't exist and three cards shipped a bare `hover:scale-[1.02]` that stuck after a tap on the Android WebView (behavior fix, retro-applied to MarketplaceCard + both FilesTab cards). The §14 audit found the ledger **overstated** (opposite of tranches 1–2): `SkillCard`'s `marketplace` variant was **dead code** (only importer is CommandDrawer, never passed the prop; LibraryScreen's lookalike renders a MarketplaceCard) — deleted, not migrated, taking 4 of change 23's 6 hexes with it; change 22's real grid was **3 sibling card species** in one drawer grid (2 unledgered), not one; a **second, drifted** `locked` tone map existed in MarketplaceScreen; and change 25's `bg-well` idiom had **3 identical copies** in one panel, not the one named. Rulings: dead branch deleted; star gold stays a raw hex (app-store convention, no `--gold` token exists) as one shared `STAR_GOLD_CLASS`; "Published successfully!" goes neutral (errors-are-neutral logic, design rule 6). **Tranche 5** (states) — **change 34** (`c6590224`): error-MESSAGE text → `text-destructive-fg` at 42 sites/23 files. **Ledger token correction**: the spec said `text-destructive`, but text must use `text-destructive-fg` (the fill token is too dark to read as text on dark themes; all 11 prior sites + FieldError use `-fg`) — following §1.6 literally would reintroduce the contrast bug the split fixed. Excluded (verified): status scales/tones, status glyphs, danger-action affordances, games (change 47), diff/tool-output tones, git-stat metrics, red-tinted boxes (change 33's job). **Changes 31/32** (`9634e58d`): adopted `LoadingState` (0→8) + `EmptyState` (0→2); each loader now names what it loads; ResumeBrowser's empty gains a Clear-search action. Left deliberately: the two "quiet skeleton, not a spinner" loaders (Providers protected by name in §2), project-view tab loads (block would center them), and LibraryScreen `Section`/ReviewList's own clean empty abstractions. **Change 33 (ErrorState) HELD** — its per-site recoverable-vs-general split is the error-message audit's own core decision; the component is built and ready, which is all "tranche 5 unblocks the audit" ever meant. Impl logs: **spec §14 (§14.11) + §15**. **Update 2026-07-23 (same branch) — tranche 7 (form controls 38–40) COMMITTED (`f779c3f0` + `06d96ccf`).** This turns the LAST zero-call-site primitives live: Radio, RadioGroup, and Checkbox now have call sites, and the renderer has **zero native `<input type=radio|checkbox>`**. **39** — Radio at all 3 native-radio sites (PreferencesPopup permission list; SyncSetupWizard's create/existing repo group); the adoption forced a RadioGroup fix (its wrapper arrow-key handler hijacked keys typed into an option's nested TextInput — guarded to ignore INPUT/TEXTAREA/SELECT/contenteditable, pinned by a new test). **40** — roundness slider made controlled; NOT a one-word swap (currentRoundness derives from the theme file via async writeFile, so a bare `value=` freezes the thumb mid-drag) — fixed with a draft+resync effect like EngineCard's context knob. **Checkbox** — adopted at ProjectView's delete-consent checkbox (ledger :807 → actually :846). **38** (structural) — SessionDrawer's native sort `<select>` + two CheckboxGlyph rows collapse into one sliders trigger opening the shared FileFilterPopover ("filter menu like project view"); FileFilterPopover generalized so `typeFilter`/`onTypeFilter` are optional (Project View's call site UNCHANGED, drawer omits the Type group); orphaned `deletedCount` removed. **Visible drawer interaction change — eyeball flagged.** Log: spec §16. **REMAINING: tranches 4 (screens/nav 26–30), 6 (type/tokens 35–37 — the ~594-site `text-[Npx]` rename is the one big mechanical sweep; 37 = creme.json contrast, a real bug shipping today, pull it out early), 8 (session-8 41–51 — CloseButton half of 41 already done), plus change 33 (held for the error-message audit) and deferred change 78.** Remaining recommended order 4→8→6 (5 + 7 done; see the tranche-3 plan doc). **Update 2026-07-23 — MERGED to master: youcoded PR #245, merge `dd3e5b30`** (tranches 3 + 5[31/32/34] + 7[38/39/40], 14 commits; CI green on all three platforms; 3239 tests). **Every `components/ui/` primitive now has call sites** — before this branch seven shipped primitives had ZERO consumers, and the renderer now has zero native `<input type=radio|checkbox>`. Destin reviewed the whole branch in a live dev instance before merge; seven follow-ups came out of it and TWO reversed his own earlier rulings — full log in **spec §17**: the card-hover regression (`.layer-surface`'s unlayered background silently beats any competing Tailwind utility — the same trap that forced `!rounded-lg`; fixed with a named `.card-interactive`), no-results empty states with a way out across all three file surfaces (reversing the 2026-07-22 "the (0) headers carry it" ruling), "Session artifacts" → "Session Files" (3 sites + an INVERTED test guard that had pinned the opposite), a shared `SearchFilterPill` replacing two divergent search+filter controls that each carried their own sliders glyph, multi-select Type filter, and the retirement of "Hide code & configs" (removed down to theme-context, where it was a persisted+synced setting nothing read; also exposed that Project View used a LOCAL hideCode state while the drawer used the persisted context flag — one visible control, two drifting settings). **Behavior changes for release notes:** Android sticky-hover fixed (a tap used to leave cards stuck enlarged — three cards shipped an unguarded `hover:scale-[1.02]`); error text is now theme-derived so community packs can shift it (built-ins pixel-identical); **the default file view is no longer documents-first** — code/config files now appear by default in both browsers (Destin accepted; to restore, initialise the type selection to `{document,image,sheet}` rather than reviving the hide toggle); `RadioGroup` no longer hijacks arrow keys typed into a nested field. **REMAINING: tranches 4 (screens/nav 26–30), 8 (session-8 41–51), 6 (type/tokens 35–37 — the ~594-site rename; 37 = creme.json contrast, a real bug shipping today), plus change 33 (held for the error-message audit) and deferred change 78.** **Update 2026-07-24 — TRANCHE 8 MERGED (youcoded PR #252, `a5e6e8f1`; spec §20). THE MIGRATION IS COMPLETE — 49 of 51 shipped.** The audit again found the ledger stale in both directions: changes 42 (Textarea) and 43 (--on-destructive) had ALREADY shipped in tranches 2 and 0, and 41 was ~80% done. **The find: `FirstRunView` declared its own local `ProgressBar`** — a near-exact copy of the shared primitive — and that local declaration SHADOWED the import site, so the primitive read as adopted (a grep for the component name matched) while actually having zero consumers, and the file the spec called "already matched" was the reason nobody noticed. Second shadow-copy this workstream has found (tranche 3 killed SkillCard's dead `marketplace` variant) and the first where grepping the name actively hid the problem. Guarded now: `tests/primitive-adoption.test.ts` fails if any `components/ui/` primitive has no call site outside `ui/`. **Two things the Toast ledger missed:** the primitive is `pointer-events-none` (so it never swallows clicks meant for the app underneath) but App's toast carries a real clickable "Send anyway" action — routing it through `message` would have shipped a DEAD button, so it gained an explicit `pointer-events-auto` action slot; and the 14 manual `setTimeout`s did NOT agree on a duration (3s/4s/6s/8s, scaled to reading length — the 8s ones are the cross-device handoff warnings), so a single primitive default would have silently cut those to 3s. Duration moved onto the toast state; all 14 timers deleted. **Change 50 was approved, built, and REVERSED on sight** — Destin rejected the headerless Settings drawer in the dev instance on 2026-07-24. Esc and click-outside do work (verified before the ✕ came out), but the title row is what tells you which drawer you are in and the ✕ is what a non-technical user reaches for; **design rule 12 gets NO Settings-drawer exception, do not re-delete the header on the strength of the 2026-07-16 approval.** The reusable half is the macOS coupling: the header carried `.mac-titlebar-inset .settings-drawer-header{padding-top:28px}`, the only thing keeping the first row clear of the native traffic lights — the guard now pins the PAIR (it reads which anchor SettingsPanel uses and requires globals.css to pad that same selector), because breaking it is invisible on Linux and Windows. **Judgment calls made without asking:** ConnectFour's piece colours stay hardcoded (`state.myColor === 'red'` is a wire-protocol value, not styling — theming it desyncs the board from game state); ThemeScreen's swatch button uses `bg-current/15` rather than the ghost Button's `hover:bg-inset`, because it floats on a swatch painted in the PREVIEWED theme's colours where an app-theme fill can land invisible (this retires the app's last raw `hover:bg-black/20`); TerminalToolbar keeps 40×40 instead of `size="icon"`'s 28px since it is a primary touch control over a terminal. **Release-note behaviour changes:** friend-request Accept/Decline + Challenge are real buttons instead of coloured text (Accept was `text-green-400` — "yes" by colour alone, the exact pairing rule 5 retires); Library's inactive tabs lose their permanent fill and shrink a step; Settings rows read one step larger at the same height; the Settings ✕ is 28px with a focus ring and a real accessible name; the update panel finally shows a progress bar (download % previously existed only inside a disabled, dimmed button label); Toggles gain an invisible 44×44 Android touch target (desktop byte-identical); community-theme game-lobby links and errors now follow the theme. **The one rule worth carrying out of eight tranches: audit before editing, and treat a count in a spec as a hypothesis.** Spec archived to `docs/archive/specs/2026-07-16-ui-consistency-design-spec.md`. Remaining ledger items are tracked elsewhere: change 33 under the error-message-standards entry, change 78 as its own entry below. **Next: the whole-UI review pass entry below.** **Update 2026-07-24 — tranche 4 MERGED (youcoded PR #246, `cc1d3944`; spec §18):** `Overlay.tsx` is now the sole z-index authority (design rule 11 enforced by `tests/overlay-layer-authority.test.ts`). ProjectView dropped from `z-[8000]` to the shared `z-40` screen layer, which **un-swallowed toasts, context menus and info bubbles** that had been painting behind it; seven settings popups collapsed into one `SettingsPopup` and two byte-identical donate blocks into one `DonateConfirm` (−243 lines net). The §18 audit again found the ledger wrong in both directions: change 26's stated warning pointed at the right edit for the wrong reason, two clauses had already shipped, change 29 was half-wrong (ModelLoadingBar stays positioned in-column), change 30 needed component extraction rather than in-place swaps, and three unledgered magic z-indexes turned up. **Update 2026-07-24 — tranche 6 MERGED (youcoded PR #249; spec §19) — and the ledger had it split across two tranches without saying so.** Verified before editing: 35's `@theme` tokens, 36's `theme-types` optionality AND its whole `computeOverlayTokens` derivation, and **all of change 37** had already shipped in tranche 0 / the 2026-07-19 contrast rewrite — creme is at `#7D7161`/`#9A8F7F`, better than the values the spec proposed. **The "pull 37 forward, it's a live bug" advice carried in two handoffs was stale; there was no bug.** What was actually open: (a) the rename — 572 sites / 87 files, of which **546 are pixel-identical** (`text-[12px]`→`text-xs` is free: Tailwind's xs IS 12px and nothing overrides the root font-size). `text-[8px]` (2 sites) wasn't in the ledger at all. **DECIDED: `--text-sm-tight: 13px` is a new named token, not a fold** — all 22 of those sites are Project View body copy on the screen Destin signed off two days earlier, and folding to `text-sm` would have resized the whole screen for nothing; the 15/17/8px sites fold UP so titles keep their step. Guard `tests/type-scale-authority.test.ts`, mutation-verified. (b) 36's one unshipped clause, "the contrast audit gains a link check" — **which was hiding a shipping bug.** Building the check first and measuring before coding showed the tranche-0 derivation's `>40` accent-vs-fg guard only asks "distinguishable from body text", never "readable": **6 of 11 shipped themes fail AA on their link, including built-in `light`, worst `kuromi-dreamer` at 2.90:1.** Adding the rule as specced would have turned CI red on all six. **DECIDED: give `deriveLink` the same AA nudge `deriveDestructiveFg` already has, then the HARD rule is safe.** Surface set is canvas + panel + **inset** — the assistant bubble is `bg-inset` and `MarkdownContent` renders inside it, so the bubble is where links actually live; auditing canvas alone is the exact §12 mistake (canvas is the best case, so measuring it alone ships the worst). `well` excluded on purpose (command-drawer search surface, renders no links; gating on it fails two packs over a pairing the app can't produce). **Only DERIVED links are nudged** — a declared link is the author's choice (creme's olive is deliberate) and auto-correcting it would make the new rules unfailable. Built-in `light` fixed by hand (`#2563EB`→`#2055CA`, 3.59:1 on inset). Four community packs get a readable link; zero new failures vs a rule-by-rule baseline. **Also learned: the contrast rules file is vendored THREE ways** (canonical in wecoded-marketplace, copies in wecoded-themes + youcoded) and wecoded-themes CI diffs its copy against marketplace master — so merge order is mandatory (marketplace #64 → themes #25 → youcoded), and getting it wrong red-lights somebody else's theme submission rather than your own PR. **REMAINING: tranche 8 (session-8 41–51) ONLY, plus held change 33 and deferred change 78. Audit 41 before implementing — its CloseButton half shipped in tranche 2 and tranche 4 already collapsed seven settings popups, so its real scope is smaller than the ledger's.** On completion, see the whole-UI review pass entry below.

- [x] Session launch + model + tag UX overhaul — new-session menu, resume browser, Model Providers `feature` `#ui` `#renderer` `#sessions` `#native-runtime` (added 2026-07-29, **shipped 2026-07-31 — youcoded#279**)
  **Shipped.** All five findings below were addressed. One `ModelPicker` replaces the four model UIs; `NativeModelSelect.tsx` and `RuntimeBindingFields` are deleted, so the Runtime toggle no longer exists anywhere — the runtime is derived from the model picked. The resume row's expanded panel is launch-only, with tags/note moved to an in-card sheet reachable WITHOUT expanding (so it also reaches rows that can't be resumed on this device). Priority became a built-in, undeletable tag and Complete became a one-click check on the card, retiring the parallel flags-vs-tags shapes. `TagManagerPopup` is the central tag management that finding (5) says did not exist. Claude Code sessions now record their model so the resume card can show it. **Structural question answered:** New Session and Resume stay two surfaces — Destin's call, 2026-07-30.
  Design came out of ten rounds in the workbench comparison view; the surviving open items are tracked in `docs/archive/handoffs/2026-07-31-session-launch-ux-open-items.md` and the two entries added below.
  Destin's ask 2026-07-29, verbatim shape: he "quite dislike[s]" the new-session menu and the resume browser, wants both **simplified with better navigability**, wants **native model handling improved across those two and the Model Providers settings menu**, and wants the **tags and model-selection interfaces** cleaned up specifically. Nothing is designed yet — the session pivoted to building the workbench first so alternatives can be mocked in the real renderer. **The structural question is still open and unanswered:** should New Session and Resume stay two surfaces, become one launcher, or become one launcher plus a separate session-organizing surface? Destin hadn't considered merging them and wants to see proposals for both before deciding.
  **Findings from the 2026-07-29 source read (all verified, cite these rather than re-deriving them):**
  (1) **The new-session form exists twice** — `SessionStrip.tsx:999` (dropdown) and `App.tsx:2932` (welcome screen). They share `RuntimeBindingFields` but each keeps its own Claude-alias model row. Field stack is Folder → Runtime toggle → Provider → Model → Preset → *(or)* alias row → Skip Permissions → Launch in New Window → Create.
  (2) **Model selection has FOUR distinct UIs**: a 4-button alias row (new session, CC runtime); two stacked `<Select>`s for Provider then Model (`RuntimeBinding.tsx:252-298`, native); `NativeModelSelect.tsx` — search box + list grouped by provider (resume, native); and `ModelPickerPopup.tsx`'s own third native rendering (in-session). `NativeModelSelect`'s header comment says it was extracted FROM `ModelPickerPopup` — so two of the four are known duplicates that were never reconciled.
  (3) **The resume row's expanded panel mixes two unrelated jobs** — launch options (model, skip-perms, new-window) and metadata editing (Flags, Tags, Note) — as 7 stacked labelled fields injected into a list row (`ResumeBrowser.tsx:511-668`).
  (4) **Flags and Tags are parallel systems with different UI**: Priority/Complete are a hardcoded 2-button row (`FLAG_ORDER`, `ResumeBrowser.tsx:154`), custom tags are a `TagPicker`. The Tags *filter* is a third shape again (a checkbox dropdown, `:881-910`).
  (5) **No central tag management exists.** `TagPicker` — which owns rename, recolor, archive and delete — has exactly 3 call sites (`ResumeBrowser`, `CloseSessionPrompt`, `SessionTagsChip`); `useTagRegistry` has the same 3 consumers; SettingsPanel has no tag section, only a "show tag options when closing a session" toggle. So editing a tag requires expanding a past-session row. Verified by two searches plus a settings read, 2026-07-29.
  (6) **Live bug, fix on sight:** the two dead-end pointers disagree — `RuntimeBinding.tsx:249` says "Add a provider key in Settings → **Model Providers** first", `NativeModelSelect.tsx:144` says "Settings → **Providers**". Neither is a link, and only the first name is real.
  (7) Browser-only workbench caveat that bites this work directly: `detachAvailable` gates the "Launch in New Window" toggle in BOTH forms (`SessionStrip.tsx:1068`, `ResumeBrowser.tsx:584`), so the mock must fake `detach.openDetached` or that control vanishes from the surfaces under redesign. Already handled in the workbench plan's Task 4.

- [x] UI Workbench — build every new surface against a mock backend before the backend exists `feature` `#ui` `#renderer` `#tests` (added 2026-07-29; SHIPPED 2026-07-30, youcoded PR #273 `9fa50b14`). **Two deltas from the plan below.** The toolbar has no variant switcher — the registry would have shipped with one entry, zero call sites and a switcher that did not re-render, so it is deferred to the first real two-candidate comparison. And `stress` seeds data only; slowness is a separate latency control (0/150ms/2s), because timing — not data shape — is the failure mode UI-first development actually introduces. Also fixed en route, and NOT workbench-only: `resolveAssetPath` only passed through `theme-asset://`, so any theme shipping a `data:` URI, a remote image or a root-absolute path resolved to a dead link **in the real app too**.
  Destin's decision 2026-07-29: **all future feature UI gets built in a live mockup of the app first, then the backend behind it.** A dev-only `?mode=workbench` boots the REAL renderer in a plain browser tab (Vite only — no Electron, no PTY, no main process) against a fake `window.claude`, so every menu is reachable, interactive and stateful. Because it runs the real components against the real `globals.css` and theme engine, "the app takes on the exact format of the mockup" stops being a goal and becomes structurally unavoidable — there is no second copy to translate. Design alternatives live side by side as real components behind a toolbar variant switcher; picking a winner means deleting the losers, not porting anything. **Supersedes `docs/archive/plans/2026-07-20-mockup-browser-renderer.md`** (draft, never built, never tracked here — which is most of why it sat): that plan made conversations live and menus inert, and today's purpose is the inverse. Adopted from it: the `Proxy` catch-all so unimplemented channels warn-once and resolve empty instead of hanging 30s, the Vite-only launcher, hydrate snapshots as data rather than event scripts, and the parity guard. Its "resist stubbing namespaces for completeness" rule is retired and replaced by a `MOCK_ONLY` registry — every hand-written channel either mirrors a real `preload.ts` channel or is registered against the unbuilt feature it belongs to, so a fake can never quietly ship as real **and the registry doubles as the generated backend to-do list.** The typed `Window.claude` contract already exists (`renderer/hooks/useIpc.ts:17`, 21 namespaces), so the mock is compiler-checked; the `(window as any).claude` call sites that escape it (ResumeBrowser's `browse`/`setFlag`/`setTag`/`setNote`, App's `firstRun`/`detach`, all of `theme`) get fixed opportunistically as each surface is touched. **Also deletes the ToolCard sandbox** (`?mode=tool-sandbox`, `run-sandbox.sh`, `ToolSandbox.tsx`, `fixture-loader.ts`) — its 24 `.jsonl` fixtures are absorbed as a workbench tool-gallery surface, which is the "catalog" in phase 1 rather than a follow-up. **And rewrites the `ui-mockup` skill**: the rendering half (hand-reimplemented Tailwind utilities, token tables, `computeOverlayTokens` recipes, glass-cascade rules, artifact publishing) is deleted because the real CSS now supplies it; the process half survives intact — numbered changes, change-ledger, approve-by-number, before/after on real surfaces, Halftone Dimension as the stress theme, explicit fidelity notes. **One risk designed against:** identical appearance is guaranteed, identical *behaviour under real data* is not — so a `stress` scenario (200 sessions, 80-char names, missing fields, a timing-out provider) ships first-class, and no surface is approved until it has been seen under `stress` and `empty`, not just the happy path. Phase 2 (separate entry when wanted) is the superseded plan's live play-through replayer. Spec: `docs/archive/specs/2026-07-29-ui-workbench-design.md`.

- [ ] Adopt the `FieldError` primitive — 25 hand-rolled copies remain `bug` `#ui` (added 2026-07-26)
  Found by widening the tranche-8 adoption guard to see `states.tsx` (youcoded PR #255). `FieldError`
  shipped in tranche 0 and has **zero call sites**, while **25 sites across 14 files** still write its
  exact markup by hand as `<p className="text-{2,3}xs text-destructive-fg">{error}</p>` —
  AccountSection alone has 6. Unlike change 33 this is not a held decision, just unfinished work.
  **Not a blind swap:** 19 of those sites are `text-3xs` and 6 are `text-2xs`, while the primitive
  hardcodes `text-3xs` — so either the 6 shrink (a visible change Destin hasn't seen) or the
  primitive gains a size prop. Pick one deliberately. Remove `FieldError` from
  `INTENTIONALLY_UNADOPTED` in `desktop/tests/primitive-adoption.test.ts` when it lands; a second
  test already fails if the exemption outlives its reason.

- [ ] Folder picker becomes a Select with a recents list `feature` `#ui` (added 2026-07-24)
  Was change 78 of the UI-consistency ledger; reclassified out of it in tranche 2 (spec §11.10) and
  now homeless, hence this entry. The ask is small on the surface — the project-folder control
  should be the shared `Select` like every other dropdown — but `projectFolder` is a single string
  and **there is no recents list anywhere in the app**, so a Select has nothing to list. Real scope:
  a new settings key, IPC to read/write it, a cap, and an eviction policy. That is a feature, which
  is exactly why it was pulled out of a migration tranche rather than shipped inside one. Sequence
  it after the whole-UI review pass below — that review may change what the control should look
  like, and building the recents plumbing first would be building to a spec that is about to move.

- [ ] Whole-UI review pass once the consistency migration is complete `feature` `#ui` (added 2026-07-24)
  Destin's ask, 2026-07-24: when ALL UI-consistency work is done (every tranche merged, no
  held/deferred changes outstanding), he wants to sit down and review the app's UI as a whole and
  expects to make additional minor tweaks — theme tokens, individual elements, spacing — **under the
  new consistent structure**. That framing is the point and the reason this is a separate entry
  rather than more tranche scope: the migration's job is to make every control go through one
  primitive and one token set, so a tweak at that layer moves every call site at once. Tweaks made
  *before* the migration finishes would land on surfaces that are about to be rewritten, and tweaks
  made *outside* the primitives would reintroduce exactly the divergence the 51 changes removed.
  **Update 2026-08-25 — the review pass itself is DONE; the decisions are not.** ~600 headless
  screenshots of every surface × 6 themes (+ a real Electron pass) are distilled into
  `docs/active/design/2026-08-25-ui-audit-findings.md` (ten worst surfaces ranked, the
  same-thing-drawn-N-ways table, per-theme contrast findings, and a ledger for Destin to
  approve by number — 18 items at first, 21 now, grouped into build phases A–F) and `docs/active/design/2026-08-25-ui-design-guide.md`
  (the standard: five laws, tokens/surfaces/type/radii, primitive usage, per-surface
  anatomies, theme-pack guarantees, pre-review checklist). Evidence sheets:
  `docs/active/design/2026-08-25-ui-audit/gallery.html`. Decisions are recorded phase by
  phase below; tick this item when phases C–F are decided (C is next: a brief of the measured
  bits first, since most of C is judgment).
  **Later 2026-08-25:** the first rig had filed 40 mislabelled sheets; it was rebuilt as
  reusable, self-verifying tooling — `scripts/ui-review/` (`run-review.sh` does the whole
  sweep, every shot must prove it opened, `coverage.md` names what didn't) and the `/ui-review`
  skill — and the previously unreviewed surfaces were captured (findings §6b, ledger now
  P-1…P-21, grouped into build phases A–F). Re-run the sweep before/after any UI tranche; a review must quote `coverage.md`.
  **Phase A decided and shipped 2026-08-25** (review page `docs/active/design/2026-08-25-ui-audit/phase-a-review.html`): P-11 approved (youcoded #327 — muted text ≥ 4.5:1 in every built-in, fg-faint decorative-only, pinned); P-12 REJECTED (dark built-ins are monochrome by design — guide G-8, `Button.test.tsx` pins "disabled never fills"); P-13 REJECTED; P-16 minimal (Meadow `edge-dim` 50→80% — wecoded-themes #27; no validator rule: 1.5:1 would fail 6 of 7 packs). Rig: sharded capture (~5 min full sweep), `montage-ab.sh`, and the review-page pattern (rationale + 1:1 crops + per-item decision + copyable feedback) is the template for phases B–F.
  **Phase B decided and shipped 2026-08-26** (`phase-b-review.html`, youcoded #328): P-15 approved as revised (title + ✕ on every dialog; no Cancel beside a ✕; Donate tidied; close-session footer one line), P-18 approved (real tool-card dividers, AskUserQuestion header/rows), P-10 REJECTED (status bar unchanged — 10px text stays by decision). **Phase C decided 2026-08-27** (`phase-c-cards.html`, the new one-point-per-step review deck): 13 of 16 points yes — Themes dialog (preview cards, fixed height, “Favorited Themes”, Browse-all removed), marketplace hero border/plurals, Library empty-state bug + pill tabs, marketplace bar switch/search/empty/loading/phone title; NO to greying the built-in pencil and to both Projects points (P-5 closed unchanged). **Shipped 2026-08-27 as youcoded #332** after two review-deck rounds (hover-only star, greyed pencil with tooltip, compact pill switch saying “Plugins” on both surfaces; the P-21 #2 row reservation was tried, shown to have no visible effect, and reverted). **Phase D decided 2026-08-27** (`phase-d-brief.html`, first one-run “today” deck): P-6 yes but bigger — the welcome screen gets the Terminal view’s bare frame (gear, Projects folder, window controls; no session switcher, status chips or chat bar); P-14 yes (find bar gets its own row); P-9 pills yes + Add Skills yes, icon-buttons-out-of-the-field NO (they stay, with their name on hover); P-19 attachment chips → mock-ups first (name strip at the bottom, previews per file kind); P-20 terminal width → reproduce in a dev instance first (may be a capture artefact), wallpaper backing → show a few opacity renderings first. **Review 2026-08-27 (`phase-d-review.html`):** P-6 frame, P-9 pills/Add Skills/empty state approved as built; P-14 revised — keep the bordered pill, just in its own reserved lane so it never overlaps a message (round 2). **Mock-ups 2026-08-27:** attachment chips → **design C** (128×96 card, preview + 12 px name strip, ✕ always on) with the rule that text previews render as formatted markdown, not raw `##` — here and on every other tile that previews a file (Project View included); terminal backing → **panel colour at 80%** under wallpaper themes (explained scrim vs panel in chat; Destin: "fine with 3 at 8-% instead of 90"). **Built 2026-08-27:** design-C chips (new `fs:read-head` bridge, rendered-markdown previews, `MarkdownContent preview` mode), panel-at-80% terminal guarantee, find-bar lane+pill; branch `feat/ui-phase-d` **SHIPPED 2026-08-27 as youcoded #339** (welcome frame, find-bar lane+pill, shared FilterChip pills + Add Skills + empty state, design-C attachment cards with rendered-markdown previews + `fs:read-head`, terminal panel at 80%). **Phase E decided and shipped 2026-08-28 as youcoded #347** over four decks (`phase-e-brief`, `phase-e-review`, `phase-e-review2`, `phase-e-review3`): **P-7** — the Keyboard Shortcuts dialog was worse than filed, it was `prompt` + `scrollBody={false}` and the last THREE shortcuts were unreachable at any window size, not merely clipped; amber keeps its own word, "Needs a Look", picked over folding it into red's), tags as the picker's named chips capped at three plus a count, list height 336 → 432 px. **P-4 DECLINED in full** — its `undefined` no longer renders (the local-models list was rewritten by #341, verified before the brief) and a visible scroll bar would land in every scrolling panel in the app for one dialog's complaint. Review page `docs/active/design/2026-08-25-ui-audit/phase-b-review.html` — each mixed item has an "approve the measured part only" option, which means reverting the judgment half before merge. **Two questions raised for Destin and unanswered:** the theme chip's cycle glyph is hover-only so Android gets no cue; and two chips (teal, orange announcement pill) still carry coloured outlines. **Doc drift to fix with the decision:** the findings ledger predates both Phase B commits and carries no build annotation; its P-15 row claims About/Permissions need a scroll fade they already have (`ui/Dialog.tsx:244`); the coverage figure quoted here, in the ledger, in that directory's README and in `docs/MAP.md:19` is stale (103/104 → **112/113**); `coverage-second-pass.md` was deleted as collateral by `80512b3` while two live docs still cite it; and **the design guide states P-13 — which was REJECTED — as a live rule**, plus four unbuilt Phase B/D proposals written as current, which matters because CLAUDE.md points every session at that guide as the standard.

LATER SAME DAY: first in-app consumer shipped early — ThemeMascot (Icons.tsx) now renders the rig on every non-buddy surface (welcome screen, gates, settings, buddy welcome) when the theme ships mascot.rig; static pose + blink only; Electron-desktop only (Android/remote keep flat — theme-asset:// fetch unavailable there); youcoded PR #155, CDP-screenshot-verified on golden-sunbreak. Invariants folded into docs/PITFALLS.md § Buddy Floater. - [ ] Custom theme icons: wire `theme.icons[slot]` through to the UI `feature` `#themes` (added 2026-07-22)
  Make the icon-override field a real capability instead of dead data. **The plumbing already exists and works** — `theme-asset-resolver.ts:56-62` resolves every `icons` entry to a `theme-asset://` URL, `ThemeIcons` is typed, the manifest schema accepts it, and the theme-builder skill documents it. The only missing half is CONSUMPTION: zero components read the resolved value (verified 2026-07-15, re-verified 2026-07-22), so golden-sunbreak's `send` override has been inert since it shipped.
  Scope to get right:
  - **Slots are too few and unevenly chosen.** `IconSlot` is `'send' | 'new-chat' | 'settings' | 'theme-cycle' | 'close' | 'menu'` (`theme-types.ts:92`), but the renderer has **163 inline `<svg>` across 69 files**. Decide whether the slot list stays a deliberately small, curated set (recommended — a themeable icon is a support burden per slot) or grows; do NOT let it grow implicitly.
  - **There is no icon component to wire.** Icons are hand-inlined at each site, with four partial collection modules (`Icons.tsx`, `context-menu/menu-icons.tsx`, `project-view/icons.tsx`, `project-view/detail-tool-icons.tsx`). Wiring N slots means first introducing a single `<Icon slot=... />` that falls back to the built-in glyph — otherwise every override is a separate one-off edit.
  - **Sanitize.** Theme-supplied SVG is untrusted. `sanitize-rig-svg.ts` already implements the right policy for mascot rigs (strips scripts, `foreignObject`, `<style>`, SMIL, `on*`, external URLs) — reuse it rather than writing a second one.
  - **Themeable icons must still recolor.** Inline glyphs use `currentColor`; a theme-supplied file loaded via `<img>`/`theme-asset://` will NOT inherit text color, which is the same trap the mascot rigs hit (hence their `var(--rig-accent)` convention). Decide inline-and-recolor vs. `<img>`-as-is before authoring guidance goes out.
  - **Android parity** — the WebView renders the same React UI, so a shared `<Icon>` covers both; confirm no Kotlin-side icon path needs the same treatment.
  Blocked on nothing; it is a decision about scope, not feasibility. Pairs with the cleanup item below — whichever lands first should settle the `custom-icons` feature badge.

This entry is now the CLEANUP half: if the feature is not built, delete `icons` from `theme-types.ts` + the manifest schema + theme-builder SKILL.md + golden-sunbreak's dead `send` override. Either way the `custom-icons` FEATURE BADGE pushed by `local-theme-synthesizer.ts:24` must be settled — today the Library advertises a capability that does nothing)

- [ ] Sign + size-cap the announcement payload `feature` `#security` `#announcements` (added 2026-04-21)
  AnnouncementService fetches announcements.txt over unauthenticated/unsigned HTTPS — a repo/file compromise could push arbitrary banner text to every client, and length is unbounded. Sign with a static committed public key + verify (fail-closed) in both fetchers, cap parsed length (~512 chars) before persisting, document the trust model. Defense-in-depth; no current incident. (from knowledge-debt)

- [ ] Remote access system rework (5 review findings) `feature` `#remote` (added 2026-07-15)
  From the 2026-07-10 full-codebase review: (1) chat:hydrate ID collision corrupts remote chat history (reseed/namespace counters in deserializeChatState); (2) rolling PTY replay buffer does an O(4MB) string copy per chunk with zero clients connected (chunk array + join-at-connect + broadcast early-return); (3) ~14 hand-rolled remote-server pref handlers duplicate ipc-handlers.ts and have 3 live drift bugs (defaults/folders); (4) live/replay ordering gap at connect double-applies events; (5) replayed events stamped Date.now() not transcript time. Full findings + fix shapes: docs/active/handoffs/2026-07-10-remote-access-review-handoff.md. (from master-review handoff)

- [ ] VM testing flow for first-run install/setup/sign-in (Linux host) `feature` `#tests` `#install` (added 2026-07-15)
  Snapshot-revertible VMs on Destin's Linux machine (KVM — no Hyper-V conflict, unlike the abandoned Windows-host attempt) to exercise the prerequisite installer, first-run setup, and sign-in flows on clean guests, plus any future changes to those flows. Prior art (methodology + when-to-use list): docs/archive/local-dev-vm.md and docs/archive/investigations/2026-04-29-vbox-hyperv-conflict.md.
  **Update 2026-07-16 — DONE for Windows + Ubuntu; macOS usable-but-slow.** All three guests provisioned on quickemu 4.9.9 + qemu-desktop with verified `clean` snapshots (revert→desktop ~45–50 s): Windows 11 25H2, Ubuntu 24.04, and macOS Sonoma 14.8.7 (yes, on Zen 5 — quickemu masks the CPU as Intel Haswell so AMD_Vanilla-style kernel patches never enter the picture). `scripts/vm/vm-exec.sh` drives Windows/Ubuntu guests through qemu-ga (Ubuntu needed a one-time `apt install qemu-guest-agent`). Beta-build loop verified end-to-end with the real 111 MB installer over SMB. First real findings already banked: the shipped AppImage dies on stock Ubuntu 24.04 (`dlopen(): error loading libfuse.so.2`) while the `.deb` needs zero FUSE → fixed by youcoded#153; `winget` is absent on a fresh Win11, so the baseline exercises prerequisite-installer's native `claude.ai/install.ps1` fallback branch. Recipe + every trap in `docs/vm-testing.md`. **Remaining:** a `clean-winget` Windows snapshot to cover `detectWinget`'s other branch; test the deb/rpm/pacman artifacts — **no longer blocked on v1.3** as of youcoded#158 (`a316d350`, 2026-07-16), which stopped desktop-test-build.yml discarding the three native packages it was already building, so any beta dispatch now yields all four Linux formats (first run to carry them: 29553208961, stamped 1.3.0-beta; the `.pacman` verifies clean — deps resolve to the five pinned in electron-builder.yml, payload installs to `/opt/YouCoded` with a real menu entry). Still untested *in the VMs*, which is the open part; and the full first-run/setup/sign-in pass itself. macOS caveat: no GPU acceleration exists for it at any price on this host (Apple's AMD support stops at RDNA 2; the 8060S is RDNA 3.5), so it's a functional target only — CI's `macos-latest` runner + a borrowed Mac beats it for interactive work, and tests arm64 which an x86 VM cannot.

- [ ] Workbench screenshot rig — deterministic per-theme/scenario captures `feature` `#tooling` `#ui` (added 2026-07-30, DEFERRED pending design discussion)
  Proposed 2026-07-30 as item D of a dev-tooling review; items A (`scripts/verify.sh`) and B (workspace CI) shipped the same day, D deferred by Destin for further discussion first. Shape: extend `scripts/workbench-boot-check.mjs`'s raw-CDP harness with `Page.captureScreenshot` over scenario × theme × viewport, writing PNGs to a gitignored `.shots/`. **Not Playwright** — no new dependency, no ~200 MB browser download, and the boot check already launches headless Chrome, enumerates all seven routes, and waits for mount. Determinism: `&latency=0`, animations killed via injected CSS, fixed `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride`. Modes: capture (default), `--baseline` to record, `--check` to diff and fail on drift. Value: turns CSS/layout regressions into a verdict instead of consuming Destin's eyeballs, and stays on the allowed side of CLAUDE.md's "flag interactive verification for Destin" rule, which explicitly permits one-shot screenshots of static screens. **The open decision that deferred it:** the workbench has no `&theme=` URL param — themes are chosen through the app's own settings UI into the mock store, and the frame parses only `child`/`scenario`/`latency`/`view`. Either add the param to `WorkbenchFrame.tsx` (clean, matches the existing params — **and no longer branch-gated: `feat/ui-workbench` merged 2026-07-30 as PR #273, so this is now an ordinary master change, not a reason to defer; noted 2026-08-12**) or have the rig seed the mock store over CDP before navigating (no app change, but couples the rig to storage internals that will rot). Recommendation was the URL param, now just a small standalone PR.

## Someday / ideas

- [ ] Sandboxing vs. "scratch workspace" — pick one, as its own design pass `idea` `#native-runtime` `#permissions` `#security` (added 2026-08-26)
  `docs/active/investigations/2026-08-09-native-skip-permissions.md` §3d argues AGAINST OS sandboxing and the reasoning should not be re-derived: coverage is real on Linux/macOS, free on Android, and effectively cosmetic on Windows, so a "Safe Mode" that is a kernel boundary on one device and a suggestion on another is arguably worse than none — and sandbox denials are undiagnosable for the students and professionals in the accessibility pillar. **Cheaper alternative with the same spirit:** run risky sessions against a git worktree or a copy, then show a diff to accept or discard — no kernel primitives, identical on all five platforms, one sentence to explain. It bounds "my work got wrecked", NOT "secrets were exfiltrated", and must not be sold as prompt-injection protection. If sandboxing is pursued anyway, the highest-value narrow slice is Landlock/Seatbelt around the native `Bash` tool only, opt-in per session, with Windows told plainly it is unavailable.


- [ ] Specialists — six follow-on ideas named but not designed by plan 1c `idea` `#specialists` (added 2026-08-16, plan 1c's own deferred-item ledger — `docs/archive/plans/2026-08-16-native-specialists-plan-1c-implementation.md` §"Deferred-item ledger")
  Named so they aren't lost, none scoped: (1) **promote a foreground hire to background mid-run**, so a long Task call stops blocking the parent conversation without restarting the child; (2) **open a helper's own transcript in a viewer** — the JSONL is real and resumable via `task_id`, but nothing in the UI reads it directly today; (3) **a project-level native specialists folder** (something like `<cwd>/.youcoded/specialists/`, alongside the existing personal folder and the two Claude Code folders); (4) **per-helper token/cost accounting** surfaced on its own card, not just rolled into the parent conversation's total; (5) **a strict per-action approval toggle** — today a hire's charter (read-only / read-write) is the only per-child scoping, and some users may want a fresh ask on every tool call instead of the charter-wide consent envelope; (6) **live-updating Settings roster** — 1c deliberately dropped file watchers (turn-start re-read plus a manual Settings Refresh cover normal use, see the rule), so editing a specialist file while Settings is open shows nothing until Refresh is clicked.

- [ ] Calibrate WebFetch's extraction-coverage thresholds against real pages `idea` `#native-runtime` `#harness` (added 2026-08-10)
  `MIN_EXTRACTED_CHARS=40` and `SUBSTANTIAL_PAGE_CHARS=500` (`web-fetch.ts`) shipped in the 2026-08-10 harness-tool-honesty work as reasoned defaults, unlike the JS-render density floor next to them, which is calibrated against four saved real-page fixtures (`docs/archive/specs/2026-08-06-harness-tool-honesty-design.md` §4's coverage table — vitest.dev/config, docs.python.org, nodejs.org/api/fs, example.com). No such fixture pass exists yet for these two constants. Worth doing the same measurement pass before trusting them on pages far outside that sample.

- [ ] Glob nested brace groups (`{a,{b,c}}`) `idea` `#native-runtime` `#harness` (added 2026-08-10)
  `glob.ts`'s brace-alternation support (added 2026-08-10, `docs/archive/investigations/2026-08-10-harness-search-tools-prior-art.md`) deliberately matches only non-nested `{a,b,c}` — the same restriction ripgrep's own `globset` crate enforced pre-15.0.0, which is what YouCoded's tool surface targets for compatibility. A nested pattern is refused with an explicit error message rather than silently mismatching or expanding wrong. Worth revisiting only if a real nested-brace use case shows up; ripgrep itself has since lifted the restriction (`globset`/ripgrep 15.0.0+), so there is a precedent to follow if it becomes worth the parser complexity.

- [ ] Context & knowledge as product surfaces — five-idea outline `idea` `#context` `#sync` `#marketplace` (added 2026-07-28)
  Preliminary outline only, no design work done: `docs/active/specs/2026-07-28-context-knowledge-app-features-outline.md`. Five ideas, sequenced **1 → 2 → 5 → 3 → 4**: (1) grow `ContextPopup` into a real surface — per-item token cost, attribution ("this rule loaded because you touched X"), post-hoc "why did it do that?", session mutes; (2) **correction capture** — detect the "no, don't do that" moment and offer one-tap "remember this?", i.e. the codify step of compounding engineering for users who don't know what an invariant is; (3) **work state as a first-class object** — what's in flight / blocked / tried-and-rejected, JSON and agent-writable, distinct from the conversation list (may be a promotion of `OpenTasks` rather than a new object — unresolved); (4) sync + **share knowledge packs** through the existing marketplace the way themes already ship; (5) **provenance + revocation**, which is a hard gate on (4) — shared knowledge writing into memory is OWASP ASI06 memory poisoning as a product surface, and the mitigation is origin-visibility and complete removal, not content filtering. #1 is the highest value-to-effort and the surface the others render into; #2 has the highest ceiling but is detection-dominated (prototype against real transcripts before any UI). Explicitly **not** doing a knowledge graph — files + JSONL + sync spaces carry all of it. Background: `docs/active/investigations/2026-07-28-agent-harness-frontier-research.md`.

- [ ] "Run in background" option — keep the local engine serving after app close `idea` `#local-models` (added 2026-07-20)
  Destin's note during the engine-lifecycle fix: some users may want YouCoded's downloaded models to keep serving EXTERNAL AI tools (anything that speaks the OpenAI-compatible `http://127.0.0.1:9920/v1` API) after the YouCoded window closes. Today the engine is deliberately reaped on quit (the 2026-07-20 lifecycle fix made quit-kill reliable precisely so a stale engine can't squat the port and get wrongly adopted). A background-serve mode would need an explicit opt-in toggle, a supervisor that hands the child off to a user-service/daemon instead of killing it, a "YouCoded engine is running" tray/menubar presence with a Stop control, and a reconcile-on-launch path (adopt-or-restart a daemon we own, distinct from a foreign process). Not the current intended behavior — file for later, only if real demand shows up.

- [ ] Full LSP — real diagnostics, hover types, rename-symbol `idea` `#renderer` `#artifacts` (added 2026-07-20)
  The genuine IDE-parity tier for code intelligence, and deliberately deferred. Costs: per-language server lifecycle management (spawn, crash recovery, workspace roots, one server per language per project), a protocol client the repo has none of (no LSP client, no tree-sitter, no codemirror/monaco as of 2026-07-20), and — the part that actually decides it — **it is almost certainly desktop-only**. Android cannot host language servers, so shipping this hard-forks the desktop/Android UI parity that currently holds because both run the same React bundle (`WebViewHost.kt:160`). Real win for developers, large architectural commitment. Do the tree-sitter/ctags item under Features first and see whether the remaining 20% is worth the fork.

- [ ] Debugger / breakpoints `idea` `#renderer` (added 2026-07-20)
  IDE table stakes, enormous effort, and genuinely not this product's fight — filed only so it's on the record as considered-and-declined rather than overlooked. Revisit only if the "open, personal Cowork" positioning (`docs/active/specs/2026-07-09-platform-vision-roadmap.md:139`) is abandoned outright.

- [ ] `chrome-style: 'minimal'` — bare chrome elements with no wrapping surface `feature` `#renderer` `#themes` (added 2026-07-19)
  Destin's ask while reviewing the rebuilt theme-builder Kit: a third chrome option that keeps the bare minimum elements (session switcher, header icons, status chips, input area) with **no backgrounds and no wrapping chrome** — distinct from `floating`, which detaches them into bordered pills.
  **Not expressible today, which is why it wasn't built.** `chrome-style` accepts only `default` and `floating` (globals.css), and `floating` is the ONLY value that hides `.chrome-glass`. The closest combination — `chrome-style:floating` + `header/input/statusbar: minimal` — gets transparent backgrounds (the `input-style:minimal` rule even wins on `!important`) but still leaves `margin`, `border-radius`, `border: 1px solid var(--edge)` and `box-shadow` from the floating pill rules (globals.css:1076-1100), which `header-style:minimal` doesn't reset (it only clears `background` and `border-bottom`). Result would be outlined ghost pills, not bare elements.
  Needs a real `chrome-style: 'minimal'` branch in globals.css (hide chrome-glass, strip bar surfaces AND the pill geometry, keep the controls), plus the manifest schema + `manifest-template.jsonc` + the theme-builder Kit preset. Deliberately NOT approximated preview-only: the preview would then advertise a layout the app can't render, which is the exact bug class the 2026-07-19 framed-shell fidelity work existed to remove.
  **Naming caution:** `input-style: minimal` and `header-style: minimal` already exist and mean something finer-grained, so a third "Minimal" would be ambiguous in the Kit UI — consider `bare`.

- [ ] Make "the model can show you an image in chat" a real, documented capability `idea` `#renderer` `#markdown` (added 2026-07-19)
  Discovered by accident 2026-07-19: Claude wrote a plain markdown image (`![alt](/tmp/…/final.png)`) in a chat reply and the packaged app **rendered it inline**, full-bleed. Destin's read: this is nifty and worth having on purpose — eventually tell models in the system prompt "you can show the user an image by X". Not now; capture only.
  **Why it works today (all accidental, none of it designed):** `MarkdownContent.tsx` runs react-markdown 10.1.0 with no `img` component override and no `urlTransform`; react-markdown's `defaultUrlTransform` only blocks dangerous *protocols*, and `/tmp/…` has no colon so it's treated as relative and passed through verbatim → `<img src="/tmp/…">`. Production loads the renderer with `win.loadFile` (`main.ts:590`), so the origin is `file://` and a root-relative src resolves to a real path on disk.
  **Three things make it unfit to advertise as-is.** (1) **It's dev/prod inconsistent** — in dev the origin is `http://localhost:5173`, so the same markdown 404s. A capability that only works in the packaged build is a support nightmare. (2) **It bypasses the app's deliberate image path.** `ImageView.tsx:23` and `ArtifactThumbnail.tsx:112-121` explicitly avoid `file://` and read bytes over `window.claude.artifacts.readBinary(absolutePath)` into a same-origin `blob:` URL — see the "Image context-menu actions" item under Features, which documents that choice. Markdown images route around all of it. (3) **No width constraint.** The only `img` rule is `.doc-html img { max-width: 100% }` (`globals.css:1806`), and `.doc-html` is DocxView — so a 1500px screenshot overflows the chat bubble, which is exactly what happened.
  **Shape of the real thing:** give markdown images an `img` override that resolves local absolute paths through the same `readBinary` → `blob:` path the artifact views use (fixes dev/prod parity and keeps one image mechanism), add `max-width:100%; height:auto`, then decide `urlTransform` deliberately — today ANY local path the renderer can read will render, which is fine for trusted model output but is a live consideration for pasted/untrusted transcripts. Only once that's settled is it safe to document in the system prompt. Pairs with the image context-menu item (it needs `data-artifact-path` on chat images anyway, which this would provide).

- [ ] Visual-regression harness for renderer chrome invariants (framed-shell, chrome-glass, overlay layers) `idea` `#tests` (added 2026-07-15)

Two candidate approaches: bump xterm `scrollback` to 5000+ so history coexists with the duplicates, OR set CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ALTERNATE_SCREEN=1 at launch (needs a test-conpty probe — alt-screen also affects getScreenText buffer reads). CC v2.1.120/121 also fixed some scrollback-dup cases upstream; re-verify current behavior first. (from knowledge-debt + PITFALLS sweep)

  CC v2.1.139 added "agent view" (`claude agents`) — one list of every CC session. If YouCoded ever wants CC-daemon-managed background sessions, it integrates with the SessionRegistry/SessionStrip model here. Large, speculative. (from knowledge-debt)
- [ ] Third-party agent CLIs as session providers (Codex, OpenCode, Cursor, …) `idea` `#native-runtime` `#sessions` (added 2026-07-17)
  T3-Code-style: run other vendors' agent CLIs inside YouCoded as extra `SessionProvider`s, terminal-view-only at first (no chat view). **Cuts against a standing decision** — `docs/active/specs/2026-07-09-platform-vision-roadmap.md:261` removed the Gemini provider entirely and set the runtime selector to `Claude Code | YouCoded`; OpenCode was "mine it, don't merge it" (§ line 45); the direction is a first-party native harness reaching every MODEL via OpenRouter/direct key, not wrapping other HARNESSES. Kept here as a deliberate what-if, not a commitment. **Cost shape (mapped 2026-07-17):** the desktop terminal-only seam is cheap and PROVEN — the deleted `feat/add-gemini-cli` shipped it in ~99 insertions / 8 files (`session-manager.ts` `command:'claude'` is already inside a provider branch; `pty-worker.js` already takes any `command`+`args`; `TerminalView.tsx` is provider-agnostic; widen the `SessionProvider` union; ~90 lines of renderer UI). Three parts are NOT cheap: **(1) auth/install per vendor** — `prerequisite-installer.ts` has ~5 hardcoded Claude-only functions (detect/installClaude/OAuth-URL-scrape/set-key) with no seam; each CLI needs its own set and breaks when that vendor changes their installer. **(2) Android is structural** — `Bootstrap.kt` hard-gates app boot on CC's npm layout (`@anthropic-ai/claude-code/cli.js`, pinned 2.1.112) and `PtyBridge.kt` string-concats an absolute path to it with no provider param; `claude-wrapper.js` is ~90% generic and would carry another Node CLI, but the bootstrap rework is weeks per CLI (same gap as the "no Android parity" native-runtime line). **(3) "works with our conventions" IS Phase 3** — installing a skill today writes CC's four-file plugin registry (`settings.json` enabledPlugins + installed_plugins.json + known_marketplaces.json + marketplace.json); Codex/OpenCode/Cursor read none of it, so a wrapped CLI gets a bare terminal with no skills/marketplace/hooks/memory until the `~/.youcoded` conventions inversion (ADR 008, Accepted) demotes the CC registry to an export adapter. Themes are the one thing that'd work free (xterm palette reads YouCoded's own CSS vars). Net: revisit only if there's real user demand AFTER Phase 3 lands the portable conventions layer — by then the desktop side is still ~100 lines. Related: the agent-view idea above, and "Native/local runtime has no Android parity". (from the 2026-07-17 T3 Code / Odysseus comparison discussion)

Not scoped — no design pass, no target easing/duration, no list of which panels. Likely pairs with the UI-consistency work under Features once that reaches tranches touching session-switcher UI.
- [ ] Session switcher should use the theme's rounding rules `idea` `#renderer` `#ui` `#themes` (added 2026-07-20)
  Destin's ask: the session switcher's (`SessionStrip.tsx`) corners should follow whatever rounding rule the active theme sets. **Checked 2026-07-20 and the premise may already hold, in which case this is a verify-not-build item:** `SessionStrip.tsx` already uses plain Tailwind `rounded-full`/`rounded-lg`/`rounded-sm` utilities throughout (no hardcoded `rounded-[Npx]` or inline `border-radius` found), and `globals.css`'s `@theme` block maps those straight to `--radius-*` custom properties, which `theme-engine.ts`'s `applyThemeToDom` overwrites per-theme from an optional `shape.radius*` block in `ThemeShape` (`theme-types.ts`) — `theme-builder`'s `manifest-template.jsonc` even lists corner rounding as a *required* Kit field with named presets ("Heavily rounded" vs "Minimal rounding — brutalist", `kit-presets.json`), and the community `golden-sunbreak` theme already overrides it. **The likely real gap:** all four BUILT-IN themes (light/dark/midnight/creme) currently ship identical radius values (`globals.css`), so there's nothing to visually confirm this against without installing/building a theme with a different rounding preset — if Destin observed the switcher NOT rounding correctly under some theme, that's a repro to chase (possibly a stale/cached class, a z-order surface that isn't `.session-strip` itself, or a spot missed by the grep), not a missing token. Verify against an actual differently-rounded theme before assuming code needs to change.
